# Research ledger (evidence snapshot; 2026-11-20)

This is an evolving ledger, not a claim that ecosystem research is complete. Vendor pages are used for product semantics; repository README claims are not accepted as verification until source/tests are reviewed.

## Product boundaries verified from vendor documentation

- ChatGPT Projects contain project-scoped chats, files, instructions and memory settings; branching and project-only memory affect how context is scoped. See [OpenAI Projects](https://help.openai.com/en/articles/10169521-projects-in-chatgpt).
- ChatGPT account export includes chat history in JSON plus other data, but an export is not documented as a guaranteed restore/import mechanism for recreating every Project and sidebar relation. See [OpenAI data export](https://help.openai.com/en/articles/7260999-exporting-your-chatgpt-history-and-data).
- Claude's product now has distinct classic Projects and a rolling Claude Code Projects beta. In the beta, one coordinator conversation routes tasks to parallel cloud-session threads; a project also owns its context, Library, and scheduled tasks. Anthropic's own move of eligible existing projects can carry instructions, files, full chat history, and scheduled tasks, but the docs do not describe a general external-project importer. See [Claude Projects help](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/9517075-what-are-projects) and [Claude Code Projects](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-projects).
- Claude Code Cloud sessions and teleport require claude.ai account authentication; API-key/Console-only or third-party-provider authentication cannot create hosted Cloud sessions. The user clarified that hosted Cloud is not the first-phase destination: target local/self-hosted Claude Code plus Claude Desktop client-managed sessions using its third-party inference/gateway configuration. Keep these separate adapters; a shared gateway does not prove shared session storage or import semantics. See [Claude Code Cloud](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web), [Claude Code gateway docs](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/llm-gateway), and the user-scope evidence note.
- Claude Desktop documents a Developer → Configure Third-Party Inference flow: Microsoft Foundry's integration guide says model requests route to the configured resource, billing remains on Azure, conversations remain on user devices, and applying locally restarts the app in third-party mode. Cloudflare's gateway guide documents the Desktop gateway/API-key configuration and request routing. These are provider-specific operational docs; they do not document an external ChatGPT/Codex archive importer or a stable Desktop session-file contract. The user confirmed all three Desktop modes—Chat, Cowork, and Code—are in scope; record OS and exact version per mode before claiming session restoration. [Microsoft Foundry setup](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/foundry-models/how-to/configure-claude-desktop) · [Cloudflare AI Gateway](https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai-gateway/integrations/coding-agents/claude-desktop/)
- **New first-party Claude Desktop 3P import boundary (checked 2026-11-21):** Anthropic's current third-party Desktop docs expose a local per-mode store and a built-in, source-specific import wizard. It imports Claude.ai histories/projects and local earlier-install Cowork/Code sessions, not arbitrary ChatGPT exports. Chat/Cowork state and audits are separate from Code metadata/transcripts; some imported folders need reattachment, Code workspaces must be copied/trusted, and credentials, connector sign-ins, permissions, environment values, and some settings are deliberately not moved. Treat the gateway/API key as an inference credential only, never a history-import permission. A Desktop export ZIP is unencrypted. See [Desktop import](https://claude.com/docs/third-party/claude-desktop/import), [local data](https://claude.com/docs/third-party/claude-desktop/data-storage), and [gateway contract](https://claude.com/docs/third-party/claude-desktop/gateway); exact feature/version thresholds and test matrix are in `docs/research/evidence/claude-desktop-3p-chat-cowork-code-2026-11-21.txt`.
- Claude Code can store settings, history and plugins in a configurable directory via `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; configuration has user, project, local and managed scopes. See [Claude Code settings](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings).
- The installed local CLI reports version `2.1.172` and exposes `--resume`, `--session-id`, `--bare`, `--no-session-persistence`, and `--max-budget-usd`. This is local runtime evidence, not a stable cross-version native import contract.

## First-party Codex app project/section observation (2026-11-20)

- `mcp__codex_app__list_projects` returned a `schemaVersion: 2` snapshot with 13 Project records; all returned records were `projectKind: local` and had a project ID, host, path and Git-repository flag. No ChatGPT-hosted Project row was exposed by that local app query. This is a bounded current-client observation, not an account-wide absence claim.
- `mcp__codex_app__list_threads({mode:"recent", limit:50})` exposed separate top-level `pinnedThreads`, `threads`, and `sections`. Each sidebar Section record had `{sectionId, name, itemKeys}`; current section IDs included built-in `pinned`, `threads`, `chats`, plus one custom UUID. The returned `itemKeys` used source-qualified `codex` and `chatgpt` prefixes. The observed membership counts were pinned=1, custom=5, threads=9, chats=30 (14 ChatGPT + 16 Codex keys); because the thread endpoint is recent/limited, treat counts as this snapshot, not a complete archive inventory.
- ChatGPT history mode was queried separately for active and archived chats (source=`chatgpt`): it returned 27 active entries and 0 archived entries in one page each, with no next cursor; no title or retrieval-summary match for “Destruction Era” appeared. The query checked summaries only, not full message bodies; it does not prove the project or session is absent from the account. It also returned no hosted Project-level index, so the requested “Destruction Era” object still needs a user-selected export or a reachable project listing for definitive binding.
- Design implication: sidebar Section membership is a separate, source-qualified association, while Project is a separate ID/path/host record and a Thread may carry `projectId`. Preserve `section_id → source-qualified thread_id` separately from project containment; do not assume Sections are nested under Projects. These are observed first-party app API shapes, not a public portability contract.

## Local CLI continuation experiment (2026-11-20)

- A synthetic ChatGPT fixture was converted into two branch-specific JSONL sessions in a disposable `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.
- Explicitly invoking `/Users/vincentadamnemessis/.local/bin/claude` (2.1.172) with a dummy API key and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:18765` loaded the generated session and returned the mock response `MOCK_CONTINUED` (exit 0). The localhost mock recorded the previous user/assistant turns plus the new synthetic prompt. No request reached Anthropic.
- The test caught an environment issue: `/opt/homebrew/bin/claude` resolves to 2.1.76 from `/tmp` and does not accept `--bare`; the workspace's `~/.local/bin/claude` is 2.1.172. A target adapter must pin/report the exact CLI executable and version rather than trusting ambient `PATH`.
- This verifies local native-file loading and API-key-shaped CLI routing against a mock server. It does **not** verify live Anthropic authentication, provider billing, hosted Claude Code Cloud, or long-term format stability.

## Claude Code Cloud Projects and session boundaries (official docs checked 2026-11-20)

- The new Claude Code Projects beta is a different hierarchy from the older “project contains independent chats” model. Its root is one long-running coordinator conversation; it starts/tracks worker Threads, each a separate Session/context window, and receives their reports rather than every internal step. Threads show lifecycle state in Overview (working, waiting, ready for review, landing, idle/resolved); their branches/PRs and produced files live in related surfaces such as Changes and the project Library. Model the Project→coordinator and Project→thread containment, plus thread execution/result and branch edges, separately; do not infer every parent edge from prompt similarity. See [Claude Code Projects: organization and Overview](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-projects).
- The project-wide starting context is deliberate but not equivalent to a copied local configuration: cloud threads start with project repositories/files, instructions/memory, root `CLAUDE.md`/`.claude/CLAUDE.md`/root rules, account connectors, and the cloud environment. They do **not** load repository `.claude/skills/`, agents/commands, `.mcp.json`, `.claude/settings.json` plugins/permissions/hooks, or local environment variables. Skills must be enabled for the Claude account; plugins added in Project settings; MCP servers provided through account connectors; packages and secrets belong to the cloud environment. A thread run on the user's machine uses local MCP/tools/settings but gets project instructions without its memory files. See [Project thread context](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-projects#what-threads-pick-up-from-your-repositories).
- Local Claude Code CLI sessions are saved continuously by working directory as JSONL (`~/.claude/projects/<encoded-path>/<session-id>.jsonl`). The documented contents include messages, tool calls/results, model request records and metadata; the adjacent store includes subagent transcripts, spilled tool results, pending MCP-task state, file-history checkpoints, plans, task lists, paste cache and version-dependent image/upload data. Cleanup defaults to 30 days. The JSONL format is internal and changes between versions; `/export` creates a human-readable text rendering, while non-interactive JSON/stream-JSON exposes result, session ID, usage and cost rather than serving as an archive-import contract. These are distinct fidelity obligations; a generated transcript alone is not a full runtime-state restore. See [Manage sessions](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sessions) and [Explore the .claude directory](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-directory).
- Cloud sessions run on Anthropic-managed or self-hosted cloud environments. CLI `--cloud` creates a new Cloud session from a task, while `--teleport` pulls a Cloud session to a matching local checkout; the CLI docs explicitly say an existing local session cannot be pushed to Cloud. An expired Cloud VM restores conversation history but not still-running commands/subagents/session-scoped schedules. Local dirty/unpushed work is not automatically present in the remote clone; repo/branch/credentials and environment have their own rules. A local-to-cloud handoff from Claude Desktop is a separate native flow, not evidence of an external ChatGPT/Codex import. See [Cloud sessions and teleport](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web).
- Cloud scheduled tasks (formerly routines) are another resource type: a saved prompt/repository/connectors configuration may have schedule, HTTP API, or GitHub-event triggers, and each run executes as a cloud session. They are a research preview and do not equal session-scoped `/loop` or a project conversation's coordinator/thread history. See [Scheduled tasks](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/routines).
- **Target/auth decision:** the user means local/self-hosted Claude Code and Claude Desktop client-managed conversations routed via third-party inference mode, not Anthropic-hosted Claude Code Cloud. The prototype test only proves local CLI continuation against localhost. Desktop gateway configuration is documented by vendor integrations, but the real session data store and import/recovery path remain unverified per mode/version/OS. Keep Anthropic-hosted Cloud out of first-phase target writes; its separate `claude.ai` authentication gate still applies. No production write is authorized by this clarification alone.

## Zed Agent / ACP / thread-import evidence (official docs checked 2026-11-20)

- Zed distinguishes three execution paths: its native Zed Agent, ACP-backed External Agents, and Terminal Threads. The latter two generally own their runtime, authentication, model configuration, and native tools/instructions; Zed Agent uses Zed's own configured provider, profiles, permissions, Skills, Instructions, and MCP. See [Agents](https://zed.dev/docs/ai/agents) and [AI Quick Start](https://zed.dev/docs/ai/quick-start).
- Zed groups threads by project, supports multiple independent threads, archive/history, restore, and checkpoints. That establishes Zed's own thread/project organization, not cross-provider semantic equivalence. See [Parallel Agents](https://zed.dev/docs/ai/parallel-agents) and [Agent Panel](https://zed.dev/docs/ai/agent-panel).
- Zed can import existing threads from configured External Agents by connecting over ACP. The docs say imported entries are archived, sessions lacking an associated working directory are skipped, and already-imported threads are skipped on re-import. The feature is integration-dependent; the docs explicitly say Cursor and Gemini CLI are not currently supported. This is useful evidence of a selective bridge, not a universal importer or proof that all transcript, branch, checkpoint, media, token, or tool state is carried over. See [External Agents](https://zed.dev/docs/ai/external-agents) and [Parallel Agents](https://zed.dev/docs/ai/parallel-agents).
- Zed documents `SKILL.md` folders in global `~/.agents/skills/` and project `<worktree>/.agents/skills/` locations, flat-only discovery, trusted-worktree gating for local skills, and that Skills apply to Zed Agent—not automatically to external agents or terminal threads. Zed also separates always-on instructions from reusable Skills; instruction files have a defined project precedence list. See [Skills](https://zed.dev/docs/ai/skills) and [Instructions](https://zed.dev/docs/ai/instructions).
- Zed Agent uses Zed-managed MCP settings; ACP agents may receive forwarded MCP servers or use their native configuration. Credentials and policy must therefore remain host-/agent-scoped and require target-side re-authentication/approval. See [External Agents](https://zed.dev/docs/ai/external-agents) and [all settings](https://zed.dev/docs/reference/all-settings).
- Unverified: direct restoration of ChatGPT/Codex hosted Projects into Zed, complete import fidelity for arbitrary external-agent histories, and transfer of user media, provider billing/token ledgers, schedules, plugin hooks, tool approvals, or hidden project stores. These remain unknown until source-format and per-integration tests exist.

## Kiro IDE / CLI / Web / Mobile evidence (official docs checked 2026-11-20)

- Kiro describes one shared agent harness across IDE, CLI, Web and Mobile, with clients talking to it over ACP (including Kiro-specific `_kiro/` methods). Local IDE/CLI usually run the harness on the machine; Web/Mobile and explicit cloud sessions run it in an account-backed sandbox. The same user can resume an account cloud session across surfaces. This is a substantially different organization from a set of unrelated clients that only happen to share a skill format. See [How Kiro works](https://kiro.dev/docs/how-kiro-works/).
- Projects are repository/workspace-centered rather than proven equivalent to ChatGPT hosted Projects: Web sessions select one or more GitHub/GitLab repositories and may be added to a group; `.kiro/` project files travel with the repository. Scope layers include global `~/.kiro/`, project `.kiro/`, agent-specific config, and optional synced Cloud configuration. IDE/CLI/Web/Mobile each expose different subsets; personal global paths are not available in Web/Mobile unless explicitly synced through cloud configuration. See [Web sessions](https://kiro.dev/docs/web/using-the-agent/) and [configuration scopes](https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/chat/configuration/).
- Session export is versioned: Kiro CLI auto-saves each turn to a local per-directory database under `~/.kiro/`; V3 `/chat save` produces a ZIP archive, while CLI 2.x uses JSON. Custom save/load scripts receive/emit session JSON, which offers a possible integration seam, but the public docs do not define a stable cross-vendor archive schema. Local archive save/load is not available for cloud sessions; those live in the account-side store and can be resumed from another machine. See [CLI session management](https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/chat/session-management/) and [CLI commands](https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/reference/cli-commands/).
- The V3 session dashboard can list local and cloud sessions, group by workspace/recency/status, filter main sessions, and search local V2/V3 prompt/assistant text. Tool output is not indexed; cloud transcripts are listed by metadata but not included in local transcript content search. The `main sessions` filter hides child sessions derived from Tangent, rewind and subagents. Workflow step sessions are managed through their parent workflow and are not equivalent to ordinary resumable chat sessions. These details argue for separate session, derived-child, workflow-run and event-store relations. See [CLI session management](https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/chat/session-management/) and [Web workflows/session details](https://kiro.dev/docs/web/using-the-agent/).
- Kiro distinguishes checkpoint restore from conversation fork/rewind: checkpoints can revert files plus later context, while a fork preserves files and starts a new conversation from an earlier turn. The IDE fork is version-gated (1.4.215+), local-chat-only, and does not carry artifacts; CLI checkpointing is marked experimental/version-dependent. CLI `/chat save`/`load` can create a separate local copy, but cannot be used on cloud sessions. See [Checkpoints and rewind](https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/chat/rewind/).
- IDE 1.x can export an individual conversation as Markdown and search/restore/delete session history; that is weaker than the CLI's structured archive route and does not imply a complete project/config export. Workflow runs have a parent chat and inspectable step tree. See [Kiro IDE Chat](https://kiro.dev/docs/ide/chat/).
- Shared capability files are richly structured: Specs create `requirements.md`, `design.md`, and `tasks.md`; Steering supports scoped/conditional inclusion; Skills follow the open Agent Skills standard; MCP and Powers, agents, permissions, hooks, workflows, and project/global config have separate scopes and precedence. Kiro Web Automations are a separate scheduled object: each run clones selected repos into an independent sandbox, runs on one of up to five configured schedules, and can produce a PR. Do not convert a schedule into a session, or an entire `.kiro/` tree into a single Skill. See [Specs](https://kiro.dev/docs/specs/feature-specs/) · [Skills](https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/skills/) · [Hooks](https://kiro.dev/docs/hooks/types/) · [Web Automations](https://kiro.dev/changelog/web/introducing-automations/).
- `Kiro Crew` has its own session and cron documentation (including export/import and scheduled jobs); treat it as a related but distinct product/surface, not as the same object store as Kiro IDE/CLI/Web. A dedicated Crew adapter is not yet reviewed. See [Crew sessions](https://kiro.dev/docs/crew/chat/sessions/) and [Crew scheduling](https://kiro.dev/docs/crew/features/cron/).
- No Kiro CLI or Web client was exercised here. These are vendor-doc findings only, not a tested import. Required follow-up: inspect the versioned archive contents with a disposable supported client; compare source and loaded event graphs; test files/artifacts, workflow children, schedules, credentials and Cloud/local boundaries separately before assigning transfer grades.

## Warp Agent / Warp Terminal / Oz Automation Platform evidence (official docs and API references checked 2026-11-20)

- Keep three layers distinct: interactive Warp Terminal conversations, Warp Agent local/cloud sessions, and Oz/Warp Automation Platform cloud runs. Personal conversations default to local storage and can opt into cloud snapshots; cloud-agent run transcripts are retained separately even when personal sync/analytics are off. Different devices resume from snapshots rather than a live shared mutable transcript. Shared conversations fork on restore; deleting a cloud-synced conversation is permanent. See [Interacting with agents](https://docs.warp.dev/agents/local-agents/interacting-with-agents/) and [Cloud-synced conversations](https://docs.warp.dev/agents/local-agents/cloud-conversations/).
- Handoff is unusually rich but has specific direction/harness gates. Local Warp Agent → cloud forks the transcript and transfers tracked/untracked workspace changes plus attachments. Cloud → cloud follow-ups can continue supported Warp Agent, Claude Code or Codex conversations with their workspace state. Cloud → local creates a local fork but currently does **not** apply the cloud workspace patch to the local checkout. Local → cloud handoff is documented for Warp Agent, not third-party local CLI sessions; apply failures are best-effort and reported. See [Handoff](https://docs.warp.dev/platform/handoff/).
- Warp can run Claude Code as a Warp-hosted cloud harness using a Warp-managed Anthropic API-key secret; this is a cloud execution environment using Claude Code, not Anthropic's own Claude Code Cloud product. The platform API creates cloud `agent/runs`, returns run IDs/status and optional session links, supports follow-up and cancel, and can expose the full run transcript, commands and file changes through the linked session. It does not document an endpoint for importing arbitrary ChatGPT/Codex historical sessions. See [Claude Code harness](https://docs.warp.dev/factories/harnesses/claude-code/) · [Managed harness authentication](https://docs.warp.dev/factories/harnesses/authentication/) · [API endpoints](https://docs.warp.dev/factories/api-and-sdk/) · [API quickstart](https://docs.warp.dev/factories/api-and-sdk/quickstart).
- Cloud orchestration has explicit parent-child lineage (`--parent-run-id`), and can coordinate different harnesses such as Warp Agent parents with Claude Code/Codex children. This run DAG is separate from ordinary chat-fork history. Cloud agent config/credentials are separately scoped; do not assume a child inherits every secret, MCP server or integration. See [Oz CLI](https://docs.warp.dev/agents/cli/oz-cli/) · [Warp Agent harness](https://docs.warp.dev/factories/harnesses/warp-agent/) · [orchestration](https://docs.warp.dev/platform/orchestration/).
- Scheduled Agents run a fixed prompt on a cron schedule and start a **fresh session for each run**; each task/session history can be inspected afterward. Environment persistence is external to the session. This represents a schedule definition, execution history and run/session identity—not a resumption of one accumulating chat. See [Scheduled Agents](https://docs.warp.dev/platform/triggers/scheduled-agents).
- Warp's file-based Skills use the open `SKILL.md` convention and support project/home roots shared with common harness-specific directories. Cloud agents discover repository skills; cloud execution may publish selected skills into native Claude Code/Codex paths. Warp Drive Rules, MCP servers and Prompts have separate object stores, while Skills remain file-based rather than Warp Drive cloud objects. MCP credentials must be redacted/re-authenticated; cloud OAuth requires managed installation. See [Skills](https://docs.warp.dev/agents/capabilities/skills) · [Warp Drive objects](https://docs.warp.dev/knowledge-and-collaboration/warp-drive/ai-objects) · [MCP](https://docs.warp.dev/guides/external-tools/using-mcp-servers-with-warp).
- **Agent Memory** is a high-relevance adjacent product: the Nov. 17, 2026 docs describe a team-gated Research Preview with personal/agent/team stores, read/write permissions, per-store instructions, source traceability and change audit. It is shared across Warp Agent, Claude Code, Codex and more; third-party harnesses are only covered when run as cloud agents (local third-party runtimes are not supported in preview). The docs list programmatic API access and self-hosting as coming soon. Thus it is not currently a verified public memory-migration API and does not replace session/project migration. See [Agent Memory](https://docs.warp.dev/agents/agent-memory/).
- Unverified: import arbitrary external transcripts into a Warp session, preserving ChatGPT Project/Section IDs, branch graphs, tool outputs, tokens, hidden stores or schedules. The interactive export command is Markdown; no supported general session-import API was found in the reviewed docs. API run creation and local/cloud handoff are not evidence of arbitrary-history import.

## Continue IDE / Continue CLI (`cn`) evidence (official docs and current repository source checked 2026-11-20)

- Continue's IDE Chat guide describes prior conversation history as context within the current session and `Cmd/Ctrl+L` (VS Code) or `Cmd/Ctrl+J` (JetBrains) starting a fresh session and clearing prior context. I found no documented IDE session export/import workflow in the reviewed docs. See [How Chat Works](https://docs.continue.dev/ide-extensions/chat/how-it-works).
- The CLI uses the same underlying agent as IDE extensions and supports full-history `--resume`, session listing and fork (`/fork` or session-ID fork). Official docs say resume restores full history; `/info` exposes usage/cost. Current `main` source stores local session JSON under `~/.continue/sessions/<UUID>.json` plus a `sessions.json` metadata index. `cn ls --json` supports scripting. The source explicitly says remote sessions are no longer available after Hub integration removal; `getRemoteSessions()` currently returns `[]`. Treat this as branch-specific evidence and recheck the deployed CLI version. See [CLI TUI](https://docs.continue.dev/cli/tui-mode) · [CLI README](https://github.com/continuedev/continue/blob/main/extensions/cli/README.md) · [session implementation](https://github.com/continuedev/continue/blob/main/extensions/cli/src/session.ts#L2119-L2129) · [history manager](https://github.com/continuedev/continue/blob/main/core/util/history.ts).
- In the inspected CLI code, each session JSON writes `sessionId`, `title`, `workspaceDirectory`, `history`, plus optional `mode`, `chatModelTitle`, and `usage`; `sessions.json` indexes date, workspace, title and assistant-message count. The fork code creates a new UUID session from copied history; that constructor does not record an explicit parent pointer, so Nexus should preserve fork lineage in a sidecar rather than infer ancestry from transcript similarity. Token/cost usage is present, but attachment/tool payload fidelity still needs fixture-level schema inspection and an actual client replay test. See [session implementation](https://github.com/continuedev/continue/blob/main/extensions/cli/src/session.ts#L1427-L1477) · [history manager](https://github.com/continuedev/continue/blob/main/core/util/history.ts#L749-L873).
- Configuration is layered: CLI config selection, saved config and `~/.continue/config.yaml`; local/workspace rules, models, prompts and MCP have separate roles. MCP is available in Agent mode; Continue documents `.continue/mcpServers/` and reusing JSON MCP configuration files from other tools, while secrets resolve from environment/.env rather than moving with the config. See [CLI configuration](https://docs.continue.dev/cli/configuration) · [MCP setup](https://docs.continue.dev/customize/deep-dives/mcp) · [configuration guide](https://docs.continue.dev/guides/configuring-models-rules-tools).
- Unverified: portable Agent Skills support, scheduled jobs, IDE conversation archive/restore, stable cross-version session JSON schema, remote session deletion/export semantics, branch parent IDs, and preservation of images/attachments/tool results through a fork or imported session. No Continue runtime import test was run.

## DeepSeek Harness (DSH) architecture and own-export importer review (repo commit `2e39e704`, package `0.2.2-rc.5`, checked 2026-11-20)

- This official `deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness` repository is MIT-licensed. The harness models one Session as the append-only source of truth: typed events have sequence/time/payload; model-visible `Message[]` history is derived, not stored as a competing transcript. Event types are plugin-extensible, and projections provide UI/session summaries. This is a strong canonical-log design reference, but its raw events are DSH-specific. See [session subsystem](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/docs/subsystems/session.md) · [core package map](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/core/README.md).
- Persistence is optional and separate from the in-memory session core. The documented production choices include one append-only JSONL/Zstandard log per Session and a SQLite provider; versioned adjacent migrations and checkpoint policy are explicit. `ctx.sessions.fork()` can capture an exact event boundary and an inherited-event count, but the current package README explicitly says the ordinary fork operation does not record a general source-session relationship; `parentSession` records a subagent's direct creator, with some historical ordinary forks also retaining it. Do not assume a complete durable parent/fork tree without inspecting each fork origin. The Session Controller exposes create, resume, prompt, fork, attachment, event/history and state operations. See [session package contract](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/core/session/README.md) · [persistence contract](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/session/session-persistence/README.md) · [session controller](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/api/session-controller/README.md).
- Workspace is a first-class record with stable ID, canonical directory path, title/timestamps and an ordered Session-ID list. Attaching a Session validates that its stored header `cwd` resolves to exactly the Workspace path. No Section/Section Group analogue was found in the inspected Workspace entity; treat that as “not evidenced,” not proof it is absent. See [Workspace entity](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/workspace/workspace/src/entity.ts).
- The optional **experimental** `deepseek-chat-import` package is first-party, but imports only DeepSeek website `deepseek_data-*.zip` format v3 into an explicitly chosen Workspace. The owner-only route buffers up to 512 MiB in memory; the plugin is off by default. It selects the newest capped conversations, maps each conversation into one stored Session and attaches it to the Workspace. It selects only the exported `current_message_id` chain (or fallback leaf), so alternate regenerated/edit branches are discarded. See [package README](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/experimental/deepseek-chat-import/README.md) · [design note](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/.agents/notes/implemented/feature/2026-11-11-deepseek-chat-export-import.md).
- Import fidelity is explicitly partial: selected text/reasoning, web search/fetch actions, Markdown read links and stored images are projected into DSH events. Non-image file bodies are not present in that export; failed/unsupported images become synthetic file cards with no bytes. Tool declarations/request headers, model/provider selection, usage/token accounting, several UI-only fragments, other branches and some timing/detail metadata are not preserved. The README documents that re-importing duplicates Sessions. The import never calls a model; it stores source-derived history for a later continuation.
- Code/test review: `packages/experimental/deepseek-chat-import/src/archive.ts` uses fflate `Unzip` streaming with `UnzipInflate`; existing archive/import tests synthesize archives with fflate `zipSync`. A same-day unanswered [Discussion #13800](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/discussions/13800) reports DSH `0.2.2-rc.5` cannot import a real 205 MB DeepSeek website ZIP with 1,146 data-descriptor entries, alleging a streaming parser failure after 1,131 entries. I could not access that actual user export. I generated a valid local 1,146-entry mixed STORED/DEFLATE ZIP with bit 3/data descriptors and tested it with fflate 0.8.2: central-directory read succeeded and the streaming pass delivered all 1,146 entries. This synthetic pass does **not** disprove the report; the real-archive failure remains unresolved/unreproduced here. Regression coverage should include a fixture matching the website's writer, not only `zipSync` output.
- Targeted package validation: after `pnpm install --filter @deepseek-ai/dsh-deepseek-chat-import... --include-workspace-root --ignore-scripts --frozen-lockfile`, the first 145-test run exited 1 because two tests needed the macOS native addon that the install deliberately skipped; the visible failure was missing `native/system/packages/darwin-arm64/bin/system.node`, and the progress assertion's sessions were consequently skipped. `pnpm run build:native-system` built `darwin-arm64/bin/system.node`; re-running `pnpm exec vitest run packages/experimental/deepseek-chat-import/tests` passed **145/145 tests across 9 files**. This is strong fixture/package evidence, not proof of importing the actual 205 MB website ZIP.

## Google Antigravity App / CLI evidence (official docs checked 2026-11-20)

- Antigravity CLI provides a native App→CLI thread import path: the `/resume` picker has an “Other” tab for App-originated threads and, on confirmation, clones the history, context and tool trajectories into a CLI session. The CLI supports session IDs, recency search, rename/delete, `-c` for the most recent conversation keyed by absolute workspace path in `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/cache/last_conversations.json`, and `--conversation <id>`. This is cross-surface continuity inside Antigravity, not an external-harness import format. See [CLI resume/import](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/resume/).
- Antigravity CLI organizes Sessions under project IDs: launch into `default-cli-project`, pass `--project` or `--new-project`, and use `/fork <project_id>` to create a fork associated with another project. Resuming automatically uses the Session's associated Project. App one-off conversations can run in a scratch folder outside Projects. See [Projects](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/projects/) · [feature overview](https://antigravity.google/docs/features).
- The App conversation side panel exposes Subagents, Background Tasks, Goals, Documents, Artifacts, Links, Modified Files and Uploads, while search can span past conversations by title, project and full text. These are distinct hierarchy/resource types and should not be flattened to transcript text. See [side panel and Overview](https://antigravity.google/docs/side-panel/).
- Skills, Rules, MCP servers, plugins and JSON hooks are shared App/CLI customization types. `/automation` creates a persistent sidecar at `~/.gemini/config/sidecars/<id>/sidecar.json`; each scheduled run starts a **fresh conversation with no memory of prior runs**. `/schedule` is different: it can wake the current conversation with its full history and is tied to that conversation's lifetime/scope. See [features](https://antigravity.google/docs/features) · [Automations](https://antigravity.google/docs/commands/automation) · [Schedule](https://antigravity.google/docs/commands/schedule/).
- Unverified: any arbitrary ChatGPT/Codex or third-party transcript import, a public session archive schema, fidelity of app→CLI trajectory cloning for attachments/artifacts/hidden state, and migration into Antigravity Projects/Automations from external formats. Keep App↔CLI native clone distinct from cross-provider migration.

## OpenCode native session interchange review (official docs + source commit `011f9043e8a90dc9a1ac42fb1181486dd4d13b71`, checked 2026-11-20)

- OpenCode's CLI documents JSON export/import (including import from a file or share URL and target `--directory`); `--sanitize` is a separate redacting export mode. The HTTP routes `/api/experimental/session/import` and `/api/experimental/session/{sessionID}/export` are explicitly marked experimental. API docs state that the export is one projected transcript and a supplied `parentID` must already exist, so bulk tree migration must import parents before children. See [CLI commands](https://opencode.ai/docs/cli/commands/) and [API reference](https://opencode.ai/docs/api).
- Pinned source shows `SessionTransfer.Data = { info: Session.Info, messages: SessionMessage.Info[] }`. `Session.Info` carries project/location/directory, parent and fork metadata, agent/model, metadata/permissions, time, outcome, token usage/cost, and revert state. Export returns the session info plus ordered messages filtered to settled records; the message schema includes structured text/reasoning/tool/file parts. This is a native OpenCode transfer schema, not an intermediate format for arbitrary harnesses. See [transfer schema](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/011f9043e8a90dc9a1ac42fb1181486dd4d13b71/packages/schema/src/session-transfer.ts) and [Session.Info schema](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/011f9043e8a90dc9a1ac42fb1181486dd4d13b71/packages/schema/src/session.ts).
- Import takes one session payload and a target location; it requires an existing parent, filters unsettled messages, preserves session-level token/cost and original creation/idle/archive values, but rewrites `time.updated` to import time. It rehomes the session to the selected Project/Location and stores inline media in blobs when the destination media-blob feature is enabled. There is no evidence in this route of recursive child-session, workspace snapshot, automation, Skill/MCP configuration, or project-level export/import. See the [pinned transfer implementation](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/011f9043e8a90dc9a1ac42fb1181486dd4d13b71/packages/core/src/session/transfer.ts).
- **Fork-boundary inference:** the source `Session.Info` schema includes both `parentID` and an explicit fork `{sessionID, boundary}` record, and the event schema has a distinct `SessionEvent.Forked`. The importer publishes a `SessionEvent.Created` containing `parentID`, but does not appear to publish the Forked event or pass its boundary. Parent-child containment therefore appears preservable while exact branch-point fidelity is unproven and likely lost; verify with an actual fork/export/import fixture before relying on it. See [Session.Info](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/011f9043e8a90dc9a1ac42fb1181486dd4d13b71/packages/schema/src/session.ts), [session events](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/011f9043e8a90dc9a1ac42fb1181486dd4d13b71/packages/schema/src/session-event.ts), and [import implementation](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/011f9043e8a90dc9a1ac42fb1181486dd4d13b71/packages/core/src/session/transfer.ts).
- Sanitization is intentionally lossy: it redacts titles/paths, user/assistant/reasoning/tool content, skill text and metadata, and blanks inline file data. This improves privacy for sharing but cannot serve as a resumable high-fidelity backup. Treat the unsanitized JSON as sensitive, scan it for secrets, and never transfer auth credentials/grants. See the same [pinned transfer implementation](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/011f9043e8a90dc9a1ac42fb1181486dd4d13b71/packages/core/src/session/transfer.ts).
- The project's open [issue #54336](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/issues/54336) reports re-import under a new session ID failing with HTTP 500 when original message IDs collide. The pinned implementation checks the session ID and inserts message IDs from the export; it does not visibly preflight message-ID collisions. Treat this as a live adapter gate and require a regression test before using OpenCode import in batch migration. This is issue/source evidence, not a runtime test by Nexus Memory.
- Review level: official docs and source at a pinned `dev` commit; **no OpenCode CLI/API runtime or test suite was run** in this research pass.

This is strong offline evidence, not 18×18 live-client verification.

## Pi coding agent — first-party docs/source review (2026-11-20)

- Reviewed upstream earendil-works/pi at commit 1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc; repository is MIT licensed and the coding-agent package snapshot is 1.5.2. This is documentation/source review, not a Pi runtime migration test.
- Session storage is JSONL under ~/.pi/agent/sessions/, grouped by working directory; session-directory overrides exist. The v3 header carries UUID, timestamp, cwd, and optional parentSession file path. Normal entries use id/parentId, making one file a branch tree. /tree navigates branches; /fork and /clone create separate session files. The header’s parentSession path does not itself prove the exact fork-boundary entry.
- The durable event stream includes user/assistant/tool-result messages, model and thinking-level changes, usage/cost, compaction, branch summaries, labels/names, context edits, custom messages, and extension state. Images can be embedded as base64. Compaction records a summary and firstKeptEntryId; raw entries remain in the file, while active model context is a projection of the selected branch and retained range. Pending assistant messages are not persisted.
- The session manager migrates v1 linear history to v2 id/parent tree entries and v2 hookMessage roles to v3 custom entries. Treat this as a local loading migration, not an external stable import contract.
- Resources remain distinct: user/project settings, trusted project .pi config, context files, Skills, MCP config, prompts, themes, extensions and extension-owned state. MCP documents stdio and streamable HTTP. Skills use Agent Skills-compatible roots plus Pi-specific discovery/trust behavior. Extensions can register tools and append custom session state.
- Reviewed core docs/source use cwd and per-project resources rather than a first-party universal Project/Section entity. This is a bounded non-observation, not proof no UI/package layer could add one. The reviewed core docs did not establish a built-in scheduler; community scheduler packages are separate.
- Pinned test source was inspected through the GitHub contents API, but Pi's tests were not executed. `migration.test.ts` contains two focused cases (v1 ID/parent migration and already-migrated idempotence); despite asserting the resulting header version is v3, it does not directly fixture/assert v2 `hookMessage`→`custom` conversion. `tree-traversal.test.ts` covers append-parent chains, branches/siblings/deep branches, active-leaf context projection, branch summaries and usage; `file-operations.test.ts` covers cwd-scoped listing, flat session directories, malformed/corrupt files, large files, and failed writes. These test fixtures exercise local persistence/tree invariants, not cross-harness import fidelity, Cloud synchronization, Project/Section mapping, or scheduled-task transfer. No schedule, Project/Section import, cloud session, or target-runtime restoration claim is made.
- Primary sources pinned to the reviewed commit: [package manifest](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc/packages/coding-agent/package.json), [sessions](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc/packages/coding-agent/docs/sessions.md), [session format](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc/packages/coding-agent/docs/session-format.md), [message types](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc/packages/coding-agent/docs/message-types.md), [Skills](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc/packages/coding-agent/docs/skills.md), [MCP](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc/packages/coding-agent/docs/mcp.md), [extensions](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc/packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md), [session manager](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc/packages/coding-agent/src/core/session-manager.ts), [migration tests](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc/packages/coding-agent/test/session-manager/migration.test.ts), [tree traversal tests](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc/packages/coding-agent/test/session-manager/tree-traversal.test.ts), and [file operation tests](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/1e34c3b4c7df78e716a8f53ad0bcf3a00feceedc/packages/coding-agent/test/session-manager/file-operations.test.ts).



## Open-source candidate: a2migrate (pinned implementation review, 2026-11-20)

- Repository snapshot: MrMirhan/a2migrate at commit e7851151761653078a1806a34339e626084bad8f (Go 1.25). GitHub's repo API returned license `NOASSERTION`, but the pinned LICENSE file and README badge explicitly identify Apache-2.0; use the file, not the API label, as the license evidence. Snapshot metadata showed one star and last push on 2026-08-10, so treat maintenance freshness as a risk, not a quality conclusion.
- Supported implementation scope in its own README: Claude Code ↔ OpenCode sessions, Skills, commands, agents, rules, MCP and system prompts are implemented both ways. Codex, Qwen Code, Gemini CLI and Factory Droid are listed as parser-pending scaffolds; this is not a ChatGPT/Codex source adapter.
- Source review: `domain.Session` associates a `ProjectDir`, a sequential message list, and optional parent/subagent IDs. `PartType` represents text, reasoning, tool and step events, but not an image/file attachment part or arbitrary event tree. The Claude reader pairs tool-use/result IDs and maps usage; the OpenCode writer inserts project/session/message/part rows and uses one SQL transaction. The reverse Claude writer regenerates JSONL events and can write a subagent link; both writer paths are local filesystem/SQLite integrations, not vendor import APIs. Project scope is workspace-root identity, not ChatGPT Section membership.
- The repository has meaningful local tests for subagent parent links, database rollback on a failed insert, idempotent migration/backup behavior, MCP config preservation, and Claude session parsing. The pinned commit's GitHub check-runs showed passing Go 1.25 tests on Ubuntu/macOS/Windows, `go vet`, formatting, lint, and smoke build; Docker was skipped. Local `go test` was not run because `go` is absent (`rtk proxy go version` exit 1). CI evidence does not test ChatGPT import or a real Anthropic/OpenCode resume.
- Security gate: the Claude MCP writer serializes `Environment` and `Headers` values directly into the legacy global `~/.claude/mcp.json` using mode 0644; its test includes a literal bearer header. The session writer also writes local transcript files with mode 0644 and overwrite is only backed up when backup is configured. Do not reuse this behavior in Nexus Memory: redact credentials, use restrictive permissions, require explicit target activation, and keep grants outside archives.
- Assessment: high-value low-star implementation reference for two-harness configuration/session adapters, transactionality and test cases; not a complete migration solution for the requested source or hierarchy. Do not report target-native fidelity until exact-client resumes are tested. Review is pinned source/CI evidence, not our own runtime migration.
- Sources: [README](https://github.com/MrMirhan/a2migrate/blob/e7851151761653078a1806a34339e626084bad8f/README.md), [Apache license](https://github.com/MrMirhan/a2migrate/blob/e7851151761653078a1806a34339e626084bad8f/LICENSE), [domain model](https://github.com/MrMirhan/a2migrate/blob/e7851151761653078a1806a34339e626084bad8f/internal/domain/domain.go), [Claude reader](https://github.com/MrMirhan/a2migrate/blob/e7851151761653078a1806a34339e626084bad8f/internal/source/claudecode/parser.go), [session orchestration](https://github.com/MrMirhan/a2migrate/blob/e7851151761653078a1806a34339e626084bad8f/internal/migrate/sessions.go), [OpenCode transactional writer](https://github.com/MrMirhan/a2migrate/blob/e7851151761653078a1806a34339e626084bad8f/internal/target/opencode/writer.go), [Claude/MCP writer](https://github.com/MrMirhan/a2migrate/blob/e7851151761653078a1806a34339e626084bad8f/internal/target/claudecode/writer.go), [Claude MCP path resolver](https://github.com/MrMirhan/a2migrate/blob/e7851151761653078a1806a34339e626084bad8f/internal/platform/platform.go), [writer rollback/subagent tests](https://github.com/MrMirhan/a2migrate/blob/e7851151761653078a1806a34339e626084bad8f/internal/target/opencode/writer_test.go), and [pinned CI checks](https://github.com/MrMirhan/a2migrate/commit/e7851151761653078a1806a34339e626084bad8f/checks).

## Open-source candidate: sessionport (pinned implementation and test run, 2026-11-20)

- Repository snapshot: azizmass/sessionport v0.3.6 at commit f5b0ffbc608ad6a2bef1d67c9deac3a9b3a15afc, MIT, Node >=20. Its source/target registry is Claude Code, Codex and OpenCode; `SourceTool` has no ChatGPT, Project/Section or Cloud object.
- Source review: the Claude reader maps each event's UUID/parentUuid into MessageIR IDs/parentId, and it recognizes tool results, model/usage data and titles. The Claude importer then emits a new linear parentUuid chain using `prevUuid` in message iteration order rather than each MessageIR.parentId. **Inference from the pinned reader/writer source:** alternate branch edges are flattened when restoring to Claude, even though the IR can carry a parent pointer. Codex importer similarly writes a new single rollout in iteration order. Therefore “full fidelity” in the README is not evidence of branch-preserving restore.
- It writes Codex rollouts/index rows and Claude JSONL files, and supports OpenCode SQLite v1/v2 writers; the tests check representative serialized fields and v2 schema tables, not a live native CLI resume. The Codex writer emits a synthetic `turn_context` with `approval_policy: auto` and workspace-write defaults; these are writer-generated target settings, not source permissions. The OpenCode importer creates a `.sessionport-backup` only if no such file exists, so repeated imports do not necessarily refresh that backup. Treat permission defaults and backup freshness as safety gates.
- Local reproduction at the pinned commit: on Node 26.7.0, `npm install` failed (exit 1) because better-sqlite3 had no prebuilt Node 26 binary and its native build failed against V8. Switched to Node 22.23.5 (within the package's declared >=20 range); install completed, `npm test` passed **122/122 tests across 5 files**, and `npm run build` (`tsc`) exited 0. The tested target is source fixtures/unit behavior, not a live Claude/Codex/OpenCode client or user account.
- Assessment: a useful, actively published small project for concrete Claude/Codex/OpenCode serializers and a testable SQLite v2 path. It does not handle ChatGPT exports or Project/Section hierarchies; branch topology and target permission defaults prevent treating it as an end-to-end compatibility proof. Retain as an adapter/reference candidate, not a drop-in solution.
- Sources pinned to the reviewed commit: [package manifest](https://github.com/azizmass/sessionport/blob/f5b0ffbc608ad6a2bef1d67c9deac3a9b3a15afc/package.json), [IR types](https://github.com/azizmass/sessionport/blob/f5b0ffbc608ad6a2bef1d67c9deac3a9b3a15afc/src/ir/types.ts), [Claude reader](https://github.com/azizmass/sessionport/blob/f5b0ffbc608ad6a2bef1d67c9deac3a9b3a15afc/src/readers/claude.ts), [Claude importer](https://github.com/azizmass/sessionport/blob/f5b0ffbc608ad6a2bef1d67c9deac3a9b3a15afc/src/importers/claude.ts), [Codex importer](https://github.com/azizmass/sessionport/blob/f5b0ffbc608ad6a2bef1d67c9deac3a9b3a15afc/src/importers/codex.ts), [OpenCode importer](https://github.com/azizmass/sessionport/blob/f5b0ffbc608ad6a2bef1d67c9deac3a9b3a15afc/src/importers/opencode.ts), and [importer tests](https://github.com/azizmass/sessionport/blob/f5b0ffbc608ad6a2bef1d67c9deac3a9b3a15afc/test/importers.test.ts).


## First-party Codex external-agent import (current upstream source snapshot, 2026-11-20)

- Pinned `openai/codex` main at 6eea32808167c4b87508e9611ba116f061ee315c (commit timestamp 2026-11-19T19:04:56Z). The app-server protocol exposes `externalAgentConfig/detect` and `externalAgentConfig/import`; current migration item types include Config, Skills, AgentsMd, Plugins, McpServerConfig, Subagents, Hooks, Commands, Memory, and Sessions. This is Codex-owned import/export surface, not a generic third-party archive contract. The reviewed type set contains no ChatGPT Project or Section object.
- The current session detector handles Claude Code and Cursor source formats. Default limits in the pinned source are 30 days of source modification age and 50 sessions; Claude imports require the recorded cwd to still be a directory. Thus a named but old “Destruction Era” conversation would not necessarily appear in the native import picker. The “50/30 days/existing cwd” bounds are source constants/logic, not inferred from UI behavior.
- Current main contains a changed-session append path: it matches the source path ledger, compares content SHA-256, and tries to append only a missing suffix when the target is a cold, unarchived imported thread and its model-visible transcript is an exact prefix of the source. It fails closed on active/archived/malformed/diverged destinations and on unsupported rollout items such as compaction/rollback event shapes. If the source changes during import, the ledger checkpoint is not advanced.
- Important reliability caveat: when this append helper returns false, `import_requested_session` yields `Ok(None)` and the importer loop ignores `Ok(None)` rather than recording a per-session failure. GitHub issue #53403, still `open` in the API at this check (last updated 2026-10-21), reports the prior changed-session silent no-op. The new main source includes an append attempt, but no evidence here proves a released Codex build fixes the report or that every no-op condition is surfaced. A consumer must check the import result count and verify target history, not only see an import-completed event.
- This is a useful first-party target-native bridge for supported Claude/Cursor → Codex resource/session flows. It does not import ChatGPT exports, does not reproduce Section membership or arbitrary Project hierarchy, and is not Claude Code Cloud. The native app-server route also means its current behavior is version-gated to the installed Codex CLI/app-server.
- Pinned sources: [app-server migration protocol types](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6eea32808167c4b87508e9611ba116f061ee315c/codex-rs/app-server-protocol/src/protocol/v2/config.rs), [session importer](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6eea32808167c4b87508e9611ba116f061ee315c/codex-rs/app-server/src/external_agent_migration/session_importer.rs), [source limits/model](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6eea32808167c4b87508e9611ba116f061ee315c/codex-rs/external-agent-migration/src/model.rs), [Claude session detection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6eea32808167c4b87508e9611ba116f061ee315c/codex-rs/external-agent-migration/src/detect/sessions/cla.rs), [suffix append implementation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6eea32808167c4b87508e9611ba116f061ee315c/codex-rs/external-agent-migration/src/sessions/append.rs), [append tests](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6eea32808167c4b87508e9611ba116f061ee315c/codex-rs/external-agent-migration/src/sessions/append_tests.rs), and [issue #53403](https://github.com/openai/codex/issues/53403).

## Open-source candidate: zen (pinned native-import / local-writer review, 2026-11-20)

- Repository snapshot: mgreau/zen at commit 45ce301d68eaff37055f320536d1e3c36940f50c, MIT. Its docs explicitly pin the reviewed format behavior to Claude Code 2.1.200 and Codex CLI 0.142.0 and say both on-disk formats are undocumented internals. The pinned commit's GitHub check-runs reported successful CI, build/release jobs; no Go toolchain was present locally, so Zen tests were not run here.
- Direction is asymmetric. Claude Code → Codex delegates to Codex’s own `externalAgentConfig/import` over app-server JSON-RPC and uses Codex’s import ledger. Codex → Claude Code parses a rollout and synthesizes a local Claude session JSONL file. The second direction is the more relevant reference for an API-key local Claude CLI workflow; it is not Anthropic-hosted Cloud.
- The Claude writer handles the path-encoding/resolved-worktree requirement, sets session ID/model/parent UUID fields, maps the shell tool family to native Bash, and marks the preamble as a migrated foreign-agent conversation. Other tool calls are flattened to marked text. Reasoning is discarded; sidechains/subagent transcripts, images, and file-history snapshots are listed as dropped. Individual tool outputs are truncated at 32 KiB and generated files are capped at 4 MiB, with a truncation note and tool-pair-aware trimming. This is honest context handoff, not full session-state preservation.
- Claude→Codex import is limited by Codex’s native detector (50 newest, 30 days, cwd must exist). The current Codex main has an append-suffix implementation for changed source files, but the pinned Zen docs/test basis was Codex CLI 0.142.0; deployment behavior and issue #53403 remain release-specific. No general ChatGPT Project/Section reader or cloud target exists in Zen.
- Tests inspected include a scripted fake app-server protocol, ledger SHA-256 deduplication, Claude JSONL envelope/tool-result assertions, and 4 MiB cap tests. These establish local serializer/protocol behavior; they are not a real Codex import or live `claude --resume` test in this task. Test recipes in the project require actual CLI versions for end-to-end verification.
- Assessment: a useful, high-signal adapter reference for leveraging the target's native importer in one direction and generating a resumable local Claude transcript in the other; the explicit loss matrix, size caps and app-server protocol are valuable. It is not a project/Section migrator, is limited to coding-session context, and does not prove hosted Cloud or API-key behavior. Keep it as a version-pinned research reference, not as evidence of universal compatibility.
- Sources: [migration design](https://github.com/mgreau/zen/blob/45ce301d68eaff37055f320536d1e3c36940f50c/docs/session-migration.md), [Codex importer](https://github.com/mgreau/zen/blob/45ce301d68eaff37055f320536d1e3c36940f50c/internal/migrate/codex_import.go), [Codex rollout reader](https://github.com/mgreau/zen/blob/45ce301d68eaff37055f320536d1e3c36940f50c/internal/migrate/codex_read.go), [Claude writer](https://github.com/mgreau/zen/blob/45ce301d68eaff37055f320536d1e3c36940f50c/internal/migrate/claude_write.go), [neutral transcript](https://github.com/mgreau/zen/blob/45ce301d68eaff37055f320536d1e3c36940f50c/internal/migrate/transcript.go), [app-server fake tests](https://github.com/mgreau/zen/blob/45ce301d68eaff37055f320536d1e3c36940f50c/internal/migrate/codex_import_test.go), [Claude writer tests](https://github.com/mgreau/zen/blob/45ce301d68eaff37055f320536d1e3c36940f50c/internal/migrate/claude_write_test.go), and [pinned CI checks](https://github.com/mgreau/zen/commit/45ce301d68eaff37055f320536d1e3c36940f50c/checks).

## Supplemental candidates: txcript, agentmove, agent-capsule and Ciao (2026-11-20)

### skillsynchq/txcript — current open-source session adapter

- Snapshot: Apache-2.0, crate `txcript` 0.14.4, pinned `skillsynchq/txcript` commit `2bf712eb76dd733ccea97997947265f5e4f1f9cb` (2026-11-18); GitHub metadata at review: 225 stars, 25 forks, 39 open issues. Main was pushed 2026-11-18. The pinned commit's check-runs all succeeded: MSRV 1.96, stable lint/test, Windows, WASM, npm dry-run, CLI release build, and crates dry-run. Local tests were not run: this environment has rustc/cargo 1.95 while the package declares rust-version 1.96.
- The Rust model stores each native transcript body with a common transcript projection. The README lists 17 harnesses/sources, including local Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Cursor CLI/Desktop, Pi, Antigravity, plus read-only live-account adapters for ChatGPT, Claude Chat and Cloud Cowork. MCP search/read is documented as read-only; local target writing is separate from account reads.
- The ChatGPT reader is explicit and source-only: it reads an existing Codex `auth.json` token/account ID only when `auth_mode` is `chatgpt`, calls private `chatgpt.com` list/detail GET endpoints, and refuses create/update/delete/resume. It does not accept an OpenAI API-key login or the official ChatGPT export ZIP/`conversations.json`. The account read by txcript may differ from the browser's ChatGPT login. Native ChatGPT JSON retains the full mapping/raw fields; the Common conversion follows `current_node`, so inactive branches, object-valued multimodal parts, attachments and unknown/UI records stay out of the writable projection. No Projects/Sections or cloud writer are in this adapter.
- The Cloud Cowork adapter reads Claude-hosted `cse_…` sessions through private APIs and the signed-in Claude Desktop macOS cookie/keychain; it refuses writes/resumes to Cloud Cowork. Its ordinary local Claude Code target is a distinct destination. This does not solve ChatGPT/Codex → Claude-hosted Cloud Projects.
- The companion Skillsync product CLI is not the open-source txcript library. Its current docs say `skl sync` uploads a manually chosen session to a Skillsync private thread; ChatGPT/Claude Chat can be synced, but are not writable targets; Cloud Cowork is neither a target nor syncable. The 2026-11-17 privacy policy says uploaded sessions are stored on Skillsync servers and are not end-to-end encrypted, and AI features may process them through third-party providers (primarily Anthropic). Treat it as a separate hosted product and privacy boundary, not a local-only txcript feature.
- Evidence: codec tests preserve unknown fields in native text, check active-branch tool mapping and refuse ChatGPT writes; a mock HTTP test asserts ChatGPT uses GET-only requests and the expected auth/account headers. The docs cite live-account verification on 2026-08-25, but no signed-in account, CLI import or target resume was exercised in this research pass. [Pinned README](https://github.com/skillsynchq/txcript/blob/2bf712eb76dd733ccea97997947265f5e4f1f9cb/README.md) · [ChatGPT format](https://github.com/skillsynchq/txcript/blob/2bf712eb76dd733ccea97997947265f5e4f1f9cb/docs/formats/chatgpt.md) · [Cloud Cowork format](https://github.com/skillsynchq/txcript/blob/2bf712eb76dd733ccea97997947265f5e4f1f9cb/docs/formats/cowork-remote.md) · [Skillsync compatibility](https://api.skillsync.com/docs/transcript-support) · [Skillsync sync semantics](https://api.skillsync.com/docs/session-sync) · [privacy policy](https://api.skillsync.com/privacy)

### wookat/agentmove — broad local configuration/resource transfer

- Snapshot: Apache-2.0, package `agentmove-cli` 0.106.0, pinned main commit `17372b12905b15611e0562d802daac2b2c2ce9a3` (2026-09-03); GitHub metadata at review: 0 stars, 4 open issues, last repository push 2026-11-11. Its model enumerates 47 clients. GitHub main workflow observed 2026-11-18 succeeded.
- The canonical bundle is configuration-oriented: model/raw config, MCP servers, instructions, persona, memory entries, Skills, agents and commands, with user/project file adapters. It has no conversation/session/branch/subagent thread graph, hosted Project/Section hierarchy, scheduled-task model or ChatGPT adapter. This makes it a complement to transcript engines, not a session migrator.
- Local evidence: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` and CLI build succeeded on Node 26.7.0/pnpm 11.25.0. Running tests before building failed with exit 1 because `dist/cli.js` did not exist; after building, `pnpm --filter agentmove-cli test` passed 53/53 files and 433/433 tests. The package typecheck passed from its own directory. Tests use fixtures and CLI e2e against copied homes; no live client was resumed.
- Secret handling is helpful but bounded: `stripSecrets` replaces key/token/secret/password/authorization/cookie-named values in MCP env/headers and raw config with env placeholders; it does not act as an all-fields content scanner, and `--include-secrets` bypasses redaction. Preserve the pattern of per-route warnings, but require a stricter allowlist/secret review for Nexus bundles. [Pinned model](https://github.com/wookat/agentmove/blob/17372b12905b15611e0562d802daac2b2c2ce9a3/packages/agentmove/src/model.ts) · [bundle redaction](https://github.com/wookat/agentmove/blob/17372b12905b15611e0562d802daac2b2c2ce9a3/packages/agentmove/src/bundle.ts) · [e2e tests](https://github.com/wookat/agentmove/blob/17372b12905b15611e0562d802daac2b2c2ce9a3/packages/agentmove/test/e2e.test.ts)

### z2z23n0/agent-capsule — native local session and Codex-profile capsules

- Snapshot: Apache-2.0, pinned main `f506789509c3082318af4a3965347e4028925ab7` (2026-07-23); GitHub metadata at review: 10 stars, 2 forks. Pinned Go/Worker/deploy CI checks passed. Local Go tests were not run because the Go executable is absent (`rtk proxy go version` exited 1 with “go: No such file or directory”).
- Session handoff supports local Codex and Claude Code stores. Same-agent import uses native transcript formats; cross-agent import writes a local new session from a neutral v1 transcript and retains raw source/manifest/neutral sidecars. The neutral shape has source ID/title/CWD and ordered entries (`kind`, time, role/text, tool/status/input/output) but no branch parent IDs, token/cost fields or project/section edges. Codex→Claude image assets are not a general media guarantee; the README specifically preserves Codex-linked local images but says arbitrary non-image files are not packaged. Profile migration is separate and Codex-to-Codex: selected native thread IDs, allowlisted files/skills/memories/automations and Git-backed working trees; uncommitted/untracked files and auth/provider/device/cloud/private encrypted state are excluded. It has no externally sourced ChatGPT Projects/Sections or Claude Cloud target.
- `profile schedule-import` writes a one-shot macOS LaunchAgent to stop Codex, run a delayed local profile import, verify and reopen Codex. This is a migration orchestration schedule, not conversion of a user's recurring scheduled-task definition. Cross-agent source sidecars are written with `0644` file modes under created directories; evaluate restrictive permissions before using with private transcripts. [Pinned README](https://github.com/z2z23n0/agent-capsule/blob/f506789509c3082318af4a3965347e4028925ab7/README.md) · [neutral transcript source](https://github.com/z2z23n0/agent-capsule/blob/f506789509c3082318af4a3965347e4028925ab7/internal/neutral/transcript.go) · [profile export](https://github.com/z2z23n0/agent-capsule/blob/f506789509c3082318af4a3965347e4028925ab7/internal/profile/export.go) · [schedule importer](https://github.com/z2z23n0/agent-capsule/blob/f506789509c3082318af4a3965347e4028925ab7/internal/profile/schedule.go)

### mindattic/Ciao-ChatGpt-Bonjour-Claude — direct but lossy browser bridge

- Snapshot: pinned `7aa6bc8d627362b8b8962e6e1f0f3c769cfb5270`, GitHub reports no recognized license and zero stars; repository was archived by its owner on 2026-10-08. Only two browser-console JavaScript scripts were found; no test harness or license file was found.
- The ChatGPT exporter creates flat `.txt` transcripts. The importer asks the user to choose one organization and one existing Claude web Project, creates a new conversation per file, then submits the complete formatted transcript as a single prompt through Claude's private API. Claude generates a new response. This is semantic prompt injection, not exact historical replay; project/section membership, branches, typed media, API-key authentication and Claude Code session storage are not preserved. Exclude as a dependency/target solution, while retaining it as a clear test of “imported chat” versus “restored transcript.” [Pinned README](https://github.com/mindattic/Ciao-ChatGpt-Bonjour-Claude/blob/7aa6bc8d627362b8b8962e6e1f0f3c769cfb5270/README.md) · [importer](https://github.com/mindattic/Ciao-ChatGpt-Bonjour-Claude/blob/7aa6bc8d627362b8b8962e6e1f0f3c769cfb5270/ClaudeConversationImporter.js) · [exporter](https://github.com/mindattic/Ciao-ChatGpt-Bonjour-Claude/blob/7aa6bc8d627362b8b8962e6e1f0f3c769cfb5270/ChatGPTConversationExporter.js)

These four manually found repositories are not part of the original 303-record API-search corpus. Their evidence levels and exclusions are appended to the separate supplemental list; none establishes exact ChatGPT/Codex Project/Section → Claude Cloud/API-key restoration.

## Supplemental direct converter: nrzz/claude-chat-ferry (pinned source/test review, 2026-11-20)

- Snapshot: MIT, `claude-chat-ferry` 1.1.0, pinned commit `1f637d7be4ec1fd5cba1dca379e95c407e485031` (2026-10-29); 1 GitHub star and 1 open issue at review. No runtime dependencies; Node >=18, with Node >=22.13 for Cursor's built-in SQLite path. Pinned GitHub Actions test matrix passed on Windows/macOS/Linux and Node 18/20/22/24.
- Its parser accepts ChatGPT's official JSON export (`conversations.json`) and other sources; it walks `mapping` from `current_node` to root, falling back to the longest root chain if current_node is missing/broken. It maps user/assistant text, code blocks, the chosen branch's tool messages, and model/time/title metadata to a compact turns model. Hidden/system messages are skipped; images become `[image]`, uploaded files are described by name rather than copied, and ChatGPT branch graph/project/Section memberships are not stored by this adapter.
- The target writer creates a local Claude Code JSONL session under the selected cwd/project, with a new session ID, sequential parentUuid chain, synthetic record timestamps, and zero usage values. Tool names can be summarized; default import does not include full tool outputs, and `--tools full` limits output text to about 2,000 characters. `--last N` limits exchanges. It supports optional `--redact` on import and defaults to redacting common secret patterns for Markdown/JSON exports; import otherwise keeps full content because the file is local until resumed. Resuming sends imported history to the configured Claude endpoint and may consume API tokens.
- Local tests on Node 26.7.0: default `rtk proxy npm test` observed **450 passed, 1 failed, 1 skipped / 452 total**. The failing optional E2E launched Claude Code 2.1.76 against a fake API on localhost (bogus key, no real provider request). Its first-user-message assertion failed because the CLI prepended a `<system-reminder>` before the imported-session banner. A disposable copy changed only that assertion from “starts with banner” to “contains banner anywhere”; the E2E then passed and verified all imported turn markers and the new prompt. Rerunning the unmodified suite with the Claude binary excluded from `PATH` yielded **450 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped**; this validates offline tests, not native resume. README's own successful resume record is for Claude Code 2.1.289 and is not equivalent to our 2.1.76 run.
- Assessment: promising source and target reader/writer reference for **ChatGPT export → local Claude Code**, including UI-friendly list/show/import and a versioned native writer. It is not a hosted Cloud import, does not restore ChatGPT Projects/Sections, and lacks image/file bytes and inactive branches. Keep as a high-value direct-adapter pattern with a target-version gate, not as a complete migration solution.
- Sources: [pinned README](https://github.com/nrzz/claude-chat-ferry/blob/1f637d7be4ec1fd5cba1dca379e95c407e485031/README.md), [ChatGPT/JSON parser](https://github.com/nrzz/claude-chat-ferry/blob/1f637d7be4ec1fd5cba1dca379e95c407e485031/src/exports.mjs), [Claude Code writer](https://github.com/nrzz/claude-chat-ferry/blob/1f637d7be4ec1fd5cba1dca379e95c407e485031/src/claude-code.mjs), [ChatGPT export tests](https://github.com/nrzz/claude-chat-ferry/blob/1f637d7be4ec1fd5cba1dca379e95c407e485031/test/exports.test.mjs), and [fake-API resume test](https://github.com/nrzz/claude-chat-ferry/blob/1f637d7be4ec1fd5cba1dca379e95c407e485031/test/e2e-claude.test.mjs).

This fifth manual-find batch candidate is not part of the original 303-record API-search corpus. There are now 11 supplements in `docs/research/github-candidates.json`; none proves exact ChatGPT/Codex Project/Section → Claude Cloud/API-key restoration.


## Open-source candidate: baton (pinned source and test review, 2026-11-20)

- Repository snapshot: Kaseban/baton at commit 0543594654dfae171dfbe63141a6ba91edebcce0, crate baton-mcp 0.4.1, dual MIT/Apache-2.0, Rust 1.88 minimum. GitHub metadata showed 29 stars and last push 2026-10-16. Pinned CI check-runs passed on Windows, Ubuntu and macOS.
- The README's matrix lists nine readable formats (Claude Code, OpenCode, Codex, Gemini CLI, Zed, Aider, Cursor export, Continue, Cline/Roo) and six writers (Claude Code, OpenCode, Codex, Gemini CLI, Zed, Aider). Only OpenCode has an automatic import action; Claude/Codex writers primarily create files in local session stores. `Cursor` is read from an exported state.vscdb JSON query and has no writer; Cline/Roo are read-only.
- The canonical `Session` contains source ID/agent, title, created/updated timestamps, one directory string, and an ordered `Vec<Message>`. A Message contains role, typed parts, timestamp, origin and model. There is no message parent/fork/branch ID, Section, project group, subagent graph, token-usage/cost field or schedule object. The Part union is richer than the smaller converters: text, signed reasoning, tool call/result, and attachment (path or base64).
- Route losses are format-specific. The Claude writer can serialize image blocks and OpenCode writes file parts, but the Codex writer emits `[attachment]` text for every Attachment and Gemini checkpoints also replace attachment content with a text placeholder. Thus a successful same-format or text round-trip is not proof of media fidelity on every route. The optional `--compress` deliberately removes tool calls and outputs. Unknown/unsupported records are not modeled as a full raw-event sidecar.
- Tests: local `rtk proxy cargo test --all-targets` on the exact pinned checkout exited 0 with 35 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored; compiler emitted two unused-import warnings in tests. Pinned GitHub checks also succeeded on Windows/macOS/Ubuntu. Tests exercise codec-level round-trips for selected formats, images and tool mapping; they do not exercise a real Claude/Codex CLI resume or a full 9×9 cross-format fidelity matrix. No target provider or real user data was used.
- Assessment: promising low-footprint typed codec and image handling for some routes; the no-parent/no-branch canonical model is a decisive gap for Nexus Memory's explicit hierarchy requirement. The `MCP` feature is Baton-as-an-MCP-server/installer, not migration of source session MCP grants/configs. Treat its “lossless” claim as route-specific and reject it as a complete Project/Section migration solution.
- Sources pinned to the reviewed commit: [README/format matrix](https://github.com/Kaseban/baton/blob/0543594654dfae171dfbe63141a6ba91edebcce0/README.md), [manifest](https://github.com/Kaseban/baton/blob/0543594654dfae171dfbe63141a6ba91edebcce0/Cargo.toml), [canonical schema](https://github.com/Kaseban/baton/blob/0543594654dfae171dfbe63141a6ba91edebcce0/src/canonical.rs), [Claude codec](https://github.com/Kaseban/baton/blob/0543594654dfae171dfbe63141a6ba91edebcce0/src/formats/claude_code.rs), [Codex codec](https://github.com/Kaseban/baton/blob/0543594654dfae171dfbe63141a6ba91edebcce0/src/formats/codex.rs), [OpenCode codec](https://github.com/Kaseban/baton/blob/0543594654dfae171dfbe63141a6ba91edebcce0/src/formats/opencode.rs), [native import dispatcher](https://github.com/Kaseban/baton/blob/0543594654dfae171dfbe63141a6ba91edebcce0/src/convert.rs), and [pinned CI checks](https://github.com/Kaseban/baton/commit/0543594654dfae171dfbe63141a6ba91edebcce0/checks).

## Deep review: 1naruto-1/agent-connect

Repository was already present in the original 303-result GitHub API corpus (not a manual supplement). Review pinned to `42287c14a8fd381bc7ef7a96076a5cba5b4d75f6` (MIT; the latest release page observed during review marks v0.2.5 as latest, released July 26, while noting 10 commits to `main` since that release). The reviewed `main` source commit is therefore not assumed to be identical to the v0.2.5 release binary. The repository's “lossless” / 12-direction positioning is broader than the demonstrated source schema: only Cursor, Claude Code, Codex CLI, and Pi adapters exist. The canonical event union is `user`, `assistant-text`, `thinking`, `tool`, and `marker`; there are no canonical project/Section, parent/fork graph, attachment bytes, token/cost/permission, or schedule fields. Tool MCP/subagent calls in a transcript are not equivalent to transferring MCP configuration or child-session hierarchy.

Source readers choose the effective/active branch: Claude follows its effective parent chain; Pi follows active `parentId`; Codex applies rollback/compaction markers and deduplicates event/response tracks; inactive forks and rolled-back turns are skipped rather than preserved as a full graph. Writers create new linear target histories. Claude emits sequential `parentUuid` and zero usage values. Cursor writes only new rows transactionally, requires Cursor to be closed and the project opened, and does not reconstruct prior native branch IDs. `migrate.ts` writes the target before the report, so report failure can leave a target artifact. No evidence was found for a ChatGPT/Codex Project or Section parser, a Claude hosted Cloud/API-key writer, media fidelity, or all 12 native-resume pairings.

Pinned-source validation on 2026-11-20: Bun 1.3.14 via ephemeral `npx bun` passed 122 tests / 386 expectations, and the package typecheck passed. This is the repository's own test suite, not proof of universal cross-harness fidelity. Sources: [pinned README](https://github.com/1naruto-1/agent-connect/blob/42287c14a8fd381bc7ef7a96076a5cba5b4d75f6/README.md), [canonical types](https://github.com/1naruto-1/agent-connect/blob/42287c14a8fd381bc7ef7a96076a5cba5b4d75f6/src/types.ts), [migration orchestration](https://github.com/1naruto-1/agent-connect/blob/42287c14a8fd381bc7ef7a96076a5cba5b4d75f6/src/migrate.ts), [pinned CI checks](https://github.com/1naruto-1/agent-connect/commit/42287c14a8fd381bc7ef7a96076a5cba5b4d75f6/checks), and the [v0.2.5 release page](https://github.com/1naruto-1/agent-connect/releases/tag/v0.2.5).

## Deep review: neosun100/aiw

This is an adjacent live-workspace controller, not a conversation/session migrator. The README describes CLI/Web UI/REST/WebSocket/MCP controls around persistent `tmux` sessions; source metadata in `~/.config/aiw/sessions.json` stores workspace name/tool/model/description/directory/creation time, while `get_log` reads only the current tmux pane capture.
