There is no parser/exporter for native conversation histories, no message/event or parent/fork model, and no importer for Projects/Sections, attachments, Skills/MCP configuration, memory, or scheduled jobs. MCP tools operate AIW workspaces (`create/list/log/send/kill/status/tools`); they are not portable MCP-server definitions.

Safety finding (static source review only; no payload was executed): the CLI/API default server host is `0.0.0.0`, route handlers have no authentication, and `tmux._run` uses `shell=True` for interpolated command strings. API-provided workspace names, paths, and configurable tool commands flow into shell strings; endpoints can create workspaces, send commands, and kill workspaces (including `all`). Treat network exposure as unsafe unless bound behind trusted network controls and authentication, and do not reuse the shell-construction pattern in Nexus. Tests do not exercise injection or deployment exposure.

This corpus candidate is MIT licensed, pinned to commit `a9e4221aa39e20aa7c041528805f8a763c035158` (2026-01-08); its README requires tmux and the package is Python 3.10+. Local tests passed 66/66 when run with `PYTHONPATH=src`, but they mock `_run`/tmux and do not validate durable transcript preservation or real client resume. Relevance rating: **low for migration engine; adjacent orchestration reference only**. Sources: [pinned README](https://github.com/neosun100/aiw/blob/a9e4221aa39e20aa7c041528805f8a763c035158/README.md), [tmux adapter](https://github.com/neosun100/aiw/blob/a9e4221aa39e20aa7c041528805f8a763c035158/src/aiw/tmux.py), [REST/WebSocket API](https://github.com/neosun100/aiw/blob/a9e4221aa39e20aa7c041528805f8a763c035158/src/aiw/api.py), [MCP server](https://github.com/neosun100/aiw/blob/a9e4221aa39e20aa7c041528805f8a763c035158/src/aiw/mcp_server.py).

## Deep review: Fabian-698/dsh-update-guide (2026-11-20)

This MIT Skill is a DSH-specific upgrade/recovery runbook, not a cross-Harness importer. The reviewed main commit is `0fcb77d2a9abf8c28da32119a243a568bd9c3824` (2026-09-24; one-star snapshot); its stated coverage ends at DSH `0.1.7-rc.1`. Its `repair-v0-sessions.mjs` repairs three known v0→V3 migration-rejection shapes and validates with DSH's `sessionFormatCatalog`; `--apply` requires a matching `sessions.bak-*` path unless `--force`, stages a temp file, re-decodes/revalidates it, checks source mtime/size, and atomically renames. The bundled self-test passed deterministic A–L checks on Node 26.7.0 and zstd 1.5.7, but SKIP B (owner gate scripts not installed) was explicitly reported; `--full` was not run because it scans a real user DSH home.

A synthetic temp-home test found a format-generation mismatch in the repair script: the diagnostic scanner and reader choose V4 > V3 > V2, but `repair-v0-sessions.mjs::discover()` and the pre-write guard skip a v2 file only when `session.v3.jsonl.zstd` exists, not when an authoritative `session.v4.jsonl.zstd` exists. With a synthetic v2 descriptor-v2 log, a V4 sibling and a fake session catalog, `--list --json` reported the v2 file fixable; adding a V3 sibling made it skip. In a separate synthetic-only `--apply` run with a matching backup, the script rewrote descriptor version 2→3 while the V4 sibling remained. No real DSH data/catalog was used. Treat this as a reproducible safety gap in this pinned tool, not as a validated migration for current DSH.

Version drift is material: the official DSH releases page lists `0.2.2-rc.5` on 2026-11-18, while the guide only claims coverage through `0.1.7-rc.1`; DSH itself remains a rapidly changing developer preview. The current official experimental `deepseek-chat-import` is narrower than cross-Harness migration: it consumes only DeepSeek website `deepseek_data-*.zip` exports (format v3), writes one DSH Session per conversation into a chosen Workspace, selects the exported `current_message_id` branch and drops other branches, and can create byte-less file cards for unsupported/non-image attachments. It is not a generic ChatGPT/Codex/Claude importer. An unanswered official-repository Discussion #13800 reports that DSH `0.2.2-rc.5` rejects one valid 205 MB website export using ZIP data descriptors; the tagged source indeed streams through fflate `Unzip` with only `UnzipInflate`. Our evidence here is the report plus tagged-source inspection, not a reproduction of that user's archive.

Sources: [guide at pinned commit](https://github.com/Fabian-698/dsh-update-guide/tree/0fcb77d2a9abf8c28da32119a243a568bd9c3824), [official DSH v0.2.2-rc.5 release](https://github.com/DeepSeek-ai/deepseek-harness/releases/tag/dsh-v0.2.2-rc.5), [event-sourced Session package](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/core/session/README.md), [official importer README at rc.5](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/dsh-v0.2.2-rc.5/packages/experimental/deepseek-chat-import/README.md), [tagged ZIP reader](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/dsh-v0.2.2-rc.5/packages/experimental/deepseek-chat-import/src/archive.ts), and [unanswered ZIP discussion](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/discussions/13800).


### 2026-11-24 supplemental safety/version audit

The 2026-11-20 review above remains the source for the earlier V4-successor and official-importer analysis. This follow-up adds three synthetic safety findings on the same pinned commit: (1) `verify-session.mjs` exits 0 and prints `ALL PASS` after `SKIP S9` when `zstd` is unavailable, despite skipping all content parsing; (2) `repair-v0-sessions.mjs --apply` checks a backup only for the first target, then rewrites a second fixable session that has no matching backup; and (3) the repair walker follows a workspace symlink outside the configured `$DSH_HOME` and rewrites the external file. Each was isolated to temporary data with a fake `sessionFormatCatalog`; this does not validate DSH's official conversion semantics. Default selftest: exit 0, 24 PASS, 0 FAIL, `SKIP B` (owner Skill absent); `--full`, installation, real DSH profile, and live resume were not run. The authoritative DSH release page now lists `v0.2.3-alpha.2` (2026-11-22), while this Skill's explicit compatibility ends at `0.1.7-rc.1`. Disposition remains **DSH-specific format/recovery reference only; not a cross-Harness importer, and do not reuse the mutator or gate as-is**. Full commands, stdout, and safety-probe limitations: `docs/research/evidence/fabian-698-dsh-update-guide-followup-audit-2026-11-24.txt`.

## Deep review: Chang-Tong/dsh-unified-context (former dsh-import-agents) (2026-11-20)

Repository identity: the local origin is the former `Chang-Tong/dsh-import-agents` URL, while the inspected `package.json` names package/repository `dsh-unified-context@0.5.0`; the canonical GitHub page is now `Chang-Tong/dsh-unified-context`. Review is pinned to commit `108a5f09a151754220f66e5e9a0214f98551d802` (MIT; 22-star snapshot as observed 2026-11-20). The project was raised in official DSH Discussion #1359 as a community plugin/possible future official feature, not a DSH-core capability. It is a useful DSH-bound adapter, not the user's requested ChatGPT/Codex → Claude/API-Key route: no ChatGPT reader or Claude Cloud writer is implemented.

**What it actually moves.** Current code reads local Pi JSONL, OpenCode SQLite, Codex rollout JSONL, Claude Code JSONL, and WorkBuddy JSONL; imports each selected logical source into DSH as resumable sessions and attaches by exact `cwd` to a DSH Workspace. Pi/OpenCode agents and prompts can become DSH Skills. Codex and WorkBuddy automation plans can become DSH scheduled tasks (paused by default). The context/memory layer links shared instruction/skill/memory directories. README explicitly keeps MCP server definitions, provider/model settings, and permissions harness-native; WorkBuddy global identity/memory does not have a DSH-equivalent. This is a one-way source adapter, not a neutral interchange bundle or a general multi-target converter.

**Source-level fidelity findings.**

- `lib/codex-reader.mjs::parseCodexSession` accepts `response_item` payloads only when `payload.type === 'message'` and role is user/assistant. Separate Codex `function_call`/`function_call_output` records are ignored by this reader. The conversation collector groups fork/subagent rollout files under one root; this may preserve a readable message sequence but not task/fork/subagent edges or their distinct resume identities. Existing Codex reader tests use message records and do not cover those separate function-call records.
- `lib/claude-reader.mjs::parseClaudeSession` walks user/assistant lines in file order and does not select the active `parentUuid` chain or filter `isSidechain`. Local synthetic input showed an abandoned sidechain included in the normalized stream. Tool-use and tool-result blocks are not restored as the original call/result pair: calls commonly receive synthetic placeholder `tool/result` events, while Claude `tool_result` content is separately normalized as a `[工具结果]` text marker (capped at 2,000 characters), not linked back to its call. WorkBuddy has the importer path that emits the source-recorded result as a real paired result event.
- `lib/pi-reader.mjs::parsePiSession` reads message records without following `parentId` to choose the selected branch; sibling branches can all enter the normalized stream. Image blocks become `[图片已省略]`. The OpenCode SQLite reader intentionally skips some parts (including file/patch/compaction-style data), so native store coverage is not synonymous with full portable state.
- `lib/dsh-writer.mjs` creates a single DSH session artifact per imported ID. The CLI writer emits a version-0 header and relies on DSH migration compatibility; the plugin path instead hard-codes session format v4 because the code comments report that its nested `@deepseek-ai/*` prerelease peers disagree with the running host. This manual coupling is a version-drift gate, not a stable import API.
- Agent candidates take `frontmatter.name` and `planSkillWrites()` directly evaluates `join(skillsRoot, name)` without a component/path containment check. A no-write synthetic diagnostic with `name: ../escaped` produced target `../escaped/SKILL.md` relative to `skillsRoot`. This establishes an unsafe planned path in the reviewed function; it did not call `applySkillPlan` or write outside the temporary fixture. Do not apply untrusted agent names until canonicalized as one safe path component and the resolved target is verified inside the root.
- Plugin session imports use `create → append batches → flush → close`, with an existence check keyed by session ID. Local synthetic persistence accepted two events then threw; `close` ran, and the retry skipped that ID as “already exists” (`imported 0, skipped 1`). Therefore idempotent ID handling does not prove import completion; a partial session can remain visible/incomplete. An Oct 5 community reply in Discussion #1359 reported the same failure mode on an earlier commit; our pinned-commit diagnostic independently reproduced it. No real DSH user store was opened.
- Automations are limited to Codex/WorkBuddy shapes; source model/reasoning, notification policy, WorkBuddy push behavior, per-automation skill selection, and historical runs are not carried. Schedules are intentionally paused by default, and translated RRULE semantics need per-rule verification.

**Our execution evidence.** From the disposable checkout at the pinned commit, `pnpm run build` exited 0 and `pnpm exec vitest run` exited 0 (13 files, 74 tests). Those are unit/component fixtures, not a real DSH GUI native-resume test. Synthetic diagnostics exited 0: skill planner returned `relative_target=../escaped/SKILL.md; escapes_root=true`; partial import failed after creating the stable ID and retaining two events, then retry returned `[pi] result: imported 0, skipped 1, empty 0`. The synthetic case used a fake persistence adapter and temporary files only; it did not write any real DSH session or malicious Skill.

**Assessment.** Keep as a high-value DSH destination-format and connector reference, not as a dependency or proof of cross-Harness fidelity. Preserve its separations between transcript/context/memory/automation and its one-way workspace mapping; do not copy its unchecked path planning, existence-equals-complete retry semantics, or hard-coded target-version assumptions. It does not satisfy ChatGPT Project/Section membership, branch/subtask topology, attachments, MCP/plugin/permission migration, or the requested Claude API-Key/Cloud target. See the companion rows in [COMPATIBILITY.md](COMPATIBILITY.md), design implications in [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md), and manual inventory entry in [github-candidates.json](research/github-candidates.json).

Sources: [pinned README](https://github.com/Chang-Tong/dsh-unified-context/blob/108a5f09a151754220f66e5e9a0214f98551d802/README.md), [Codex reader](https://github.com/Chang-Tong/dsh-unified-context/blob/108a5f09a151754220f66e5e9a0214f98551d802/lib/codex-reader.mjs), [Claude reader](https://github.com/Chang-Tong/dsh-unified-context/blob/108a5f09a151754220f66e5e9a0214f98551d802/lib/claude-reader.mjs), [Pi reader](https://github.com/Chang-Tong/dsh-unified-context/blob/108a5f09a151754220f66e5e9a0214f98551d802/lib/pi-reader.mjs), [plugin persistence importer](https://github.com/Chang-Tong/dsh-unified-context/blob/108a5f09a151754220f66e5e9a0214f98551d802/plugin/import-service.mjs), [Skill planner](https://github.com/Chang-Tong/dsh-unified-context/blob/108a5f09a151754220f66e5e9a0214f98551d802/lib/agents.mjs), and [official DSH Discussion #1359](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/discussions/1359).

## License exclusion: Dicklesworthstone/coding_agent_session_search (2026-11-20)

This is an original 303-corpus candidate (1,216 stars in the captured metadata; GitHub's current repository card rounds it to 1.2k) whose GitHub description advertises local indexing/search over 30+ coding-agent history sources. The repository's LICENSE is titled “MIT License (with OpenAI/Anthropic Rider)”: it defines Restricted Parties to include OpenAI, Anthropic, affiliates, and people acting for/on behalf of them; it denies those parties rights without express prior written permission and explicitly includes analysis, indexing and testing in “use.” Therefore it is excluded from Nexus dependency/benchmark consideration unless written permission is obtained. No implementation behavior or compatibility conclusions are recorded. A disposable clone and CodeGraph index were created before the rider was noticed; source review stopped at discovery and the checkout/index were deleted. No build or tests were run. This remains in the 303-row discovery corpus with `review_status=license_restricted_excluded`; it is not a manual supplement. [Repository metadata](https://github.com/Dicklesworthstone/coding_agent_session_search) · [LICENSE](https://github.com/Dicklesworthstone/coding_agent_session_search/blob/main/LICENSE).

## Deep review: jazzyalex/agent-sessions (2026-11-20)

Snapshot: `jazzyalex/agent-sessions`, pinned main commit `3dfa0405d204279b7581d85218ea04fa218af8a7`, Xcode app version 6.6 / build 91, MIT, 952 stars at the captured GitHub page. It is a local-first macOS catalog, search, analysis and native-resume UI for coding-agent session histories. README says history stays on the Mac/no telemetry and describes opening supported CLI sessions in a terminal. This is useful product UX, not a cross-Harness migration engine.

**Data model and hierarchy.** `Session` carries source slug, path, time/model/title, cwd/repo metadata, event count and normalized `SessionEvent`s (user/assistant/tool_call/tool_result/error/meta), with a `rawJSON` field and tool/message identity fields. It also has `parentSessionID`, `subagentType` and `relationshipKind` for root/subagent/side-chat display. `SubagentHierarchyBuilder` resolves and flattens that relationship for the UI, including limited Codex role-only inference. `ProjectIdentity` is a canonical working-directory identity used for local filtering; there is no ChatGPT Project/Section membership model. `SessionSource` enumerates 17 local provider identifiers, including DeepSeek Harness, but no ChatGPT source.

**Read / write boundary.** The visible Resume action calls provider-specific resume coordinators with the original session ID and working directory, then opens the native CLI in a local terminal. That resumes the source session; it does not convert or create a destination transcript. The user-facing Export action writes Markdown from a rendered event projection (with source/model/event/time/cwd/file metadata and supported image references); it is human-readable handoff, not a schema-preserving import/export bundle. `SessionArchiveManager` snapshots pinned upstream source files and companions into the app's own local archive with manifest/hash metadata, for local continuity/offline viewing. It is not a target-Harness writer. Source review found no ChatGPT reader, Claude Cloud/API-key writer, Section/Project migrator, or Skills/MCP/plugin/schedule transfer path.

**Validation.** `scripts/xcode_test_stable.sh` initially exited 65 before tests because the machine lacked the `Mac Development` signing certificate required by the project. Re-running the full scheme directly with `CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO CODE_SIGNING_REQUIRED=NO CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY=` executed 4,596 tests: 4,586 passed, 9 skipped, 1 failed. The only failure was `TranscriptTelemetryPresentationTests.testCostShowsTwoDecimalsAndKeepsFullPrecisionInHelp`: expected `$12.37`, received `US$12.37` under the host region. An isolated run with `-testLanguage en -testRegion US` passed 1/1. Thus the full run is not green in the host locale; no release claim is made. No real CLI session was resumed, and no real source history or account data was opened. The current working tree stayed unmodified apart from disposable `.codegraph`/`.deriveddata-tests` artifacts.

**Assessment.** Rank high as a macOS session-catalog/search/resume UX and local source-retention reference; low as a migration adapter. Reuse only the separations between normalized transcript events, source identity, local resume capability and local archive policy. Nexus still needs its own canonical Project/Section/branch/resource graph and explicit destination writers; this app does not prove ChatGPT Projects/Sections → Claude API-key or hosted Cloud fidelity.

Sources: [pinned README](https://github.com/jazzyalex/agent-sessions/blob/3dfa0405d204279b7581d85218ea04fa218af8a7/README.md), [Session model](https://github.com/jazzyalex/agent-sessions/blob/3dfa0405d204279b7581d85218ea04fa218af8a7/AgentSessions/Model/Session.swift), [event model](https://github.com/jazzyalex/agent-sessions/blob/3dfa0405d204279b7581d85218ea04fa218af8a7/AgentSessions/Model/SessionEvent.swift), [source registry](https://github.com/jazzyalex/agent-sessions/blob/3dfa0405d204279b7581d85218ea04fa218af8a7/AgentSessions/Model/SessionSource.swift), [hierarchy builder](https://github.com/jazzyalex/agent-sessions/blob/3dfa0405d204279b7581d85218ea04fa218af8a7/AgentSessions/Services/SubagentHierarchyBuilder.swift), [local archive manager](https://github.com/jazzyalex/agent-sessions/blob/3dfa0405d204279b7581d85218ea04fa218af8a7/AgentSessions/Services/SessionArchiveManager.swift), and [transcript Markdown export/native resume UI](https://github.com/jazzyalex/agent-sessions/blob/3dfa0405d204279b7581d85218ea04fa218af8a7/AgentSessions/Views/TranscriptPlainView.swift).


## Deep review: slash9494/ai-config-sync-manager (2026-11-20)

- **Identity/snapshot:** original 303-record corpus entry; MIT; npm package ai-config-sync-manager 0.1.13; local pinned HEAD 3ac0566032807acabc79405245777edcc780d347; 47-star / 7-fork registry snapshot. Package metadata reports no runtime dependencies; README requires Node.js >=20. This is a local CLI for continuous Claude Code ↔ Codex developer-configuration synchronization. Its own README explicitly says it is not a one-shot migrator.
- **Implemented surface (source inspected):** global and current-working-directory project scopes; instructions, Skills, Agents, MCP servers, hooks and permissions; plugin inventory is status-only. The runtime diffs local configuration files and applies local filesystem writes. No ChatGPT export reader, hosted Claude Project/Session writer, conversation/branch/subtask graph, attachment archive, token/cost capture, or recurring-schedule adapter appears in the reviewed command surface. Memory and implicit runtime state are explicitly out of scope; symlinked Skills and plugin installation are not applied.
- **Transaction behavior:** status and sync are local; sync defaults to dry-run and requires --apply to write. Writes create backups and a per-item SHA-256 ledger. This is useful config-plane UX. It is not an atomic migration transaction: apply catches an operation error, records it and proceeds to later operations; it still runs vocabulary fixes after the operation loop. Sync state is written only if every result is applied/noop. Backups help manual recovery but do not make a partial apply atomic.
- **Approval semantics need caution:** README says a manual risk item should always be reviewed before apply, but source operations for hooks and permissions set approvalRequired=false and the apply loop skips only operations where that field is true. Missing-source cases do set it true. Therefore the manual risk label is a warning/review recommendation, not a per-item enforced gate. An explicit --apply can also propagate source-side deletions for supported areas after taking backups.
- **Credential policy:** safeEnv copies every string MCP env value by default. AI_CONFIG_SYNC_STRIP_SECRETS=1 opts into removing keys whose names match TOKEN|KEY|SECRET|PASSWORD|CREDENTIAL|AUTH. Tests explicitly assert both default secret-key copying and opt-in stripping. This is a documented product choice, but it violates Nexus Memory's default-deny transfer invariant; the name-pattern heuristic also cannot identify a secret stored under an innocuous key. Do not adopt this behavior as-is.
- **Test evidence:** clone at the pinned commit had generated dist bundles absent. First rtk npm test: 445 tests, 442 pass, 1 fail, 2 skipped; sole failure was the isolated connect fixture expecting the generated Codex plugin bundle. rtk npm run build:dist:no-sync exited 0 and built both plugin bundles without syncing active user plugin caches. The rerun rtk npm test exited 0: 445 total, 443 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped. Tests use temporary homes/synthetic fixtures, not a live provider or hosted Cloud. The repository's integration-test guide describes Codex→Claude one-way golden tests across six configuration areas and explicitly lists bidirectional, commands and deletion cases as uncovered in that integration matrix; some separate unit/CLI fixture tests do cover reverse MCP mapping, deletion and secret policy.
- **Disposition:** valuable adjacent reference for local config drift, selector UX, dry-run previews, backups and item-level hash ledgers. Not a ChatGPT/Codex session or Project/Section migrator, not a Cloud/API-Key destination writer, and not a safe credential-transfer dependency under Nexus policy. Preserve as a config-sync comparison only. Evidence: [pinned README](https://github.com/slash9494/ai-config-sync-manager/blob/3ac0566032807acabc79405245777edcc780d347/README.md), [pinned runtime source](https://github.com/slash9494/ai-config-sync-manager/blob/3ac0566032807acabc79405245777edcc780d347/bin/ai-config-sync.mjs), [pinned integration coverage guide](https://github.com/slash9494/ai-config-sync-manager/blob/3ac0566032807acabc79405245777edcc780d347/tests/integration/README.md), [pinned package metadata](https://github.com/slash9494/ai-config-sync-manager/blob/3ac0566032807acabc79405245777edcc780d347/package.json).


## Deep review: pgrigorr/memory-harness-kit (2026-11-20)

- **Identity and license:** original GitHub corpus row (303-source discovery set), 2-star snapshot, pinned clone HEAD 3374ead82060868b2398acffb40f945402bb4cac (2026-07-23). GitHub API metadata remains NOASSERTION, while the top-level LICENSE and pyproject declare Apache-2.0. CodeGraph indexed the 91-file clone (1,676 nodes, 3,925 edges). README calls it an engineering preview; package memoryctl is 0.1.0.
- **What is implemented:** file-backed memory pipeline with source inventory, normalization, atomic facts, retrieval indexes, summaries, review queues, MCP memory tools, and a generic/OpenClaw adapter boundary. Config contract accepts only generic or openclaw adapter/harness kinds. Claude Code integration is an optional MCP + lifecycle-hook setup. Stop hook can capture a Claude Code transcript path when installed; SessionStart drains pending indexing work. No native ChatGPT/Codex reader, Project/Section importer, Cloud/API-Key transcript writer, or cross-harness session resume path was found.
- **Session data path and fidelity:** the Stop hook stores the complete raw transcript envelope, but the normalized representation is one session_transcript record with project_slug=null and metadata limited to session_id, saved_at_utc, format and capture_source. Synthetic end-to-end CLI test preserved raw Claude JSONL (including parentUuid, project_id, section_id, tool_use and image markers) but normalized to one dialogue-only record: tool results, tool calls, image payloads, and branch/project/section relationships did not enter the typed record. The raw sidecar is not a target-native session restore format. Session-ID path sanitization is lossy: an isolated test with IDs a/b and a_b reproduced the same filename; the second ingest overwrote the first transcript.
- **Privacy finding:** README describes secret-like value filters, but session normalization's load_safe_source_text checks size, NUL bytes, UTF-8, emptiness and control-character ratio; it does not redact credentials. A synthetic secret-shaped marker was observed in both the stored raw transcript and normalized searchable record. The raw ingest writer also does not set restrictive file permissions or encrypt the file itself. Do not ingest/package real session data under Nexus policy until the secret filter/permissions boundary is resolved.
- **Scope and schedules:** the OpenClaw adapter binds one explicit external workspace and one documented recurrence-status slice; it does not import arbitrary runtime state. Windows bootstrap can register this kit's own local maintenance/health/summary/embed-server scheduled tasks, but documentation says task scheduling and hooks are environment-specific and not covered by automated tests. This is not a reader/writer for ChatGPT or Claude scheduled-prompt objects.
- **Verification:** canonical make ci-smoke exited 0 on macOS arm64 / Python 3.12.10: 289 tests passed; generic init+validate returned status=ok; optional OpenClaw smoke was intentionally skipped because no real workspace was supplied. make ci-artifact-proof exited 0: built wheel/sdist, installed each into isolated virtualenvs, and doctor/init/validate succeeded. Separate synthetic Stop-hook test captured a transcript and queued rebuild. None of these tests was a live ChatGPT/Codex/Claude account or cross-host import test.
- **Disposition:** worthwhile low-star reference for file-backed semantic memory and review-gated retrieval, but not a faithful conversation/project migration engine or a safe raw-transcript transport as-is. Borrow the separation of raw archive, normalized facts and retrieval context only after adding stable source IDs, explicit project/section/session/event relations, user-approved source adapters, secret redaction before disk/index/package, and target-native writers. Evidence: [pinned README](https://github.com/pgrigorr/memory-harness-kit/blob/3374ead82060868b2398acffb40f945402bb4cac/README.md), [session capture source](https://github.com/pgrigorr/memory-harness-kit/blob/3374ead82060868b2398acffb40f945402bb4cac/src/memoryctl/collectors/session_transcript.py), [normalizer](https://github.com/pgrigorr/memory-harness-kit/blob/3374ead82060868b2398acffb40f945402bb4cac/src/memoryctl/normalize/records.py), [source safety filters](https://github.com/pgrigorr/memory-harness-kit/blob/3374ead82060868b2398acffb40f945402bb4cac/src/memoryctl/normalize/safety_filters.py), [Claude Code Stop hook](https://github.com/pgrigorr/memory-harness-kit/blob/3374ead82060868b2398acffb40f945402bb4cac/scripts/hooks/session_stop_hook.sh).


## Deep review: yeelam-gordon/agent-cli-session-TUI (2026-11-20)

- **Identity/snapshot:** original corpus candidate; MIT; package version 0.3.3; pinned clone HEAD f81a993aa94071fd2150bd9cf4fd6710547b196e (2026-10-13); 0-star GitHub snapshot. It is a Rust TUI for discovering, searching, locally grouping, and resuming retained histories from Copilot CLI, Claude Code, Codex CLI, Qwen, Gemini CLI and Kimi. ChatGPT/hosted Project APIs are not a provider.
- **Transfer boundary:** it reads provider-native local state directories via six YAML definitions, parses metadata/tails for the TUI and local search, and resumes by launching the same provider CLI with that provider's resume flag/session ID in its original cwd. It does not transform one provider's conversation into another's native format, write/import into Claude Cloud/API-Key, or export a migration bundle. Session model contains one provider session ID, provider, cwd, title/summary, state, timestamps and state_dir; it has no parent/child/branch/event graph or Project/Section entities. The groups file is a flat session-key-to-group-name assignment plus optional description, not a nested hierarchy. Archived state is a local set of provider/session keys, not an export/archive of transcript contents.
- **Privacy/egress:** default engine is remote and may run automatically after first discovery. Source sends one representative per local cluster using the session title and cwd as file:/// URI to a Microsoft-hosted Edge tab-grouping endpoint. It does not send transcript/summary text or the provider session key; existing group names are sent only when the opt-in reuse_existing_groups is enabled (off by default). There is no published third-party request contract or version guarantee. The README warns users to set engine=wordmatch before scanning if path/title egress is unacceptable. We did not call the endpoint or run its ignored live-endpoint test.
- **Test evidence and coverage:** pinned local cargo +stable test --lib exited 0: 220 passed, 0 failed, 1 ignored (live remote endpoint test). cargo +stable test --no-run exited 0 and compiled the library, binary, and all six provider lifecycle integration test executables; those integration tests were not executed because they require real provider sessions. Project AGENTS.md specifically notes missing scanner unit tests for Copilot and Codex. The provider definitions document native formats, but the README marks tested native CLI versions unknown and says this is not live compatibility certification.
- **Disposition:** high-value discovery/resume reference for local session inventories and user-defined flat groups, but not migration. It is not sufficient for ChatGPT/Codex to Claude/Cloud fidelity because it leaves history in native provider storage and only relaunches the source CLI. Any use as a Nexus discovery connector should disable remote grouping before first scan, protect local files/logs, and be treated as a read-only source inventory adapter. Evidence: [pinned README](https://github.com/yeelam-gordon/agent-cli-session-TUI/blob/f81a993aa94071fd2150bd9cf4fd6710547b196e/README.md), [remote payload source](https://github.com/yeelam-gordon/agent-cli-session-TUI/blob/f81a993aa94071fd2150bd9cf4fd6710547b196e/src/grouping/remote.rs), [session metadata model](https://github.com/yeelam-gordon/agent-cli-session-TUI/blob/f81a993aa94071fd2150bd9cf4fd6710547b196e/src/models.rs), [native resume source](https://github.com/yeelam-gordon/agent-cli-session-TUI/blob/f81a993aa94071fd2150bd9cf4fd6710547b196e/src/supervisor/mod.rs), [usage/privacy evidence](https://github.com/yeelam-gordon/agent-cli-session-TUI/blob/f81a993aa94071fd2150bd9cf4fd6710547b196e/docs/usage.md).

## MethodoxTech/coding-agent-sessions-history-vscode — source and test review (2026-11-20)

- Snapshot: local clone HEAD 960a17ec2cecadeb202e3fdc18629a8febc2dfb3; package version 0.2.1; MIT; one-star repository snapshot. This is a VS Code local-history browser for Claude Code and Codex, not a Cloud history importer or Cloud/API-Key writer. It reads local Claude project JSONL and Codex session/archived-session JSONL, indexes those files, supports search/bookmarks/viewing, groups by date or project path, exports Markdown/date ranges, and resumes through the original CLI in the recorded working directory.
- Hierarchy and payload boundary: its derived ConversationMessage model keeps role/text/thinking/tool calls/timestamps/model/sidechain boolean/subtype/level/usage; it does not preserve raw parentUuid or a message branch-parent graph, nor ChatGPT Project/Section membership. Date/project groups are a flat browsing organization, not nested session containment. Range export combines sessions as Markdown, not a machine-readable import package. Tool output is capped at 4,000 characters by default (configurable); the image test confirms inline image payloads are omitted. Artifact URLs are references, not downloaded attachments. totalTokens exists in session metadata but is not written into Markdown.
- Local-data/privacy boundary: README explicitly describes a local-only design with no telemetry or account connection; it says transcripts are read without modification and cache/bookmarks stay in VS Code storage. The checked source paths contain local file discovery and VS Code webview messaging; no remote history/grouping service or Cloud/API-Key writer was found. This source review is not a network-sandbox audit. Local exports/tool inputs may still contain sensitive data.
- Resume/compatibility boundary: resume runs the original claude --resume or codex resume command, requiring the original CLI/authentication and recorded working directory; it is not session conversion or target-native restore. Codex raw rollout support is described as an internal format that may evolve.
- Test evidence: npm test ran TypeScript compilation and Node tests; observed 65 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. These are synthetic/package tests, not a live VS Code host, real account, or live provider history/resume certification. Range export has no direct test coverage in the reviewed source graph; a single-session Markdown export test exists. README install example says 0.1.0 while package.json is 0.2.1.
- Disposition: low-star but useful local archive-browser/resume UX reference; not a lossless ChatGPT Project/Section/session migrator and not the requested Claude Cloud/API-Key destination.
- Pinned primary sources: [README](https://github.com/MethodoxTech/coding-agent-sessions-history-vscode/blob/960a17ec2cecadeb202e3fdc18629a8febc2dfb3/README.md), [session types](https://github.com/MethodoxTech/coding-agent-sessions-history-vscode/blob/960a17ec2cecadeb202e3fdc18629a8febc2dfb3/src/claude/types.ts), [Markdown export](https://github.com/MethodoxTech/coding-agent-sessions-history-vscode/blob/960a17ec2cecadeb202e3fdc18629a8febc2dfb3/src/ui/markdownExport.ts), [session grouping](https://github.com/MethodoxTech/coding-agent-sessions-history-vscode/blob/960a17ec2cecadeb202e3fdc18629a8febc2dfb3/src/ui/sessionTree.ts), [resume/export actions](https://github.com/MethodoxTech/coding-agent-sessions-history-vscode/blob/960a17ec2cecadeb202e3fdc18629a8febc2dfb3/src/ui/actions.ts), [local index](https://github.com/MethodoxTech/coding-agent-sessions-history-vscode/blob/960a17ec2cecadeb202e3fdc18629a8febc2dfb3/src/store/sessionStore.ts).

## RubenGlez/lokalite — Skill-library/install adapter review (2026-11-20)

- Snapshot: pinned clone HEAD 6d95c788aa22ce39a4807b9a4c31c758a2036dc6; package ai-agent-skills 4.3.2; MIT license; original inventory snapshot 1,162 stars (GitHub page displays about 1.1k). The project is a curated Agent Skills library plus CLI/TUI manager. It supports library catalog, install/add/import/vendor/curate/sync/validate workflows, provenance metadata, and GitHub/local skill sources. It manages SKILL.md packages, not harness projects, sessions, memories, MCP server configs, plugins, attachments, or schedules.
- Inventory/validation: npm run validate exited 0 with 141 catalog entries (18 vendored house copies, 123 upstream references), 7 collections, 2 rich and 139 instruction-only skills; one warning says tiktok-slideshow description reads as a summary, not a trigger condition. Upstream catalog entries can remain remote rather than being vendored.
- Target behavior: default global scope is ~/.claude/skills; project scope is .agents/skills. Legacy --agent target roots include Claude, Cursor, Amp, VS Code/Copilot, legacy project .skills, Letta, Goose, OpenCode, Kilo Code, Gemini CLI, and Codex. Disposable dry-run matrix for all 12 aliases returned the mapped target roots and wrote no targets. The --all switch targets only the two scope roots (Claude global plus .agents project), not every legacy-agent-specific directory. A real throwaway project install of bundled verification-agent produced byte-identical SKILL.md plus .skill-meta.json provenance metadata; the sandbox was removed. Direct remote installation was not executed; dry-run displayed sparse checkout for an upstream Anthropic skill.
- Interoperability boundary: it places files at configured roots and does not semantically translate one harness-specific skill, project/session schema, MCP config, plugin, memory store, or scheduler. The generic .agents/skills/ route applies only to agents that understand the Agent Skills format; per-agent flags are explicit path mappings, not certification of feature parity.
- Safety/source boundary: CLI source includes target-root confinement, symlink skipping, a 50 MB copy limit, and provenance metadata; the isolated path-traversal tests pass. These safeguards do not establish that third-party SKILL.md instructions are benign or that a copied skill behaves equivalently on every host. No user home, real repository, key, Cloud account or external session was used.
- Test results: after npm ci, node test.js completed 228 checks: 225 passed, 3 failed. Failures are inventory/document synchronization: test expects 53 marketing-cli skills but skills.json has 76 entries with that source; package.json is 4.3.2 but docs/releases/4.3.2-changelog.md is absent and README lacks the 4.3.2 release heading. npm run validate nevertheless passes, with the warning above. Test suite exercises many CLI temp-directory flows but is not a live matrix against actual agent hosts.
- Disposition: strong reference for a dedicated Skill-library and target-path adapter, not a Nexus Memory session/project migration engine. Preserve the 12-root mapping plus explicit scope semantics; do not describe --all as installing to every framework.
- Pinned primary sources: [README](https://github.com/RubenGlez/lokalite/blob/6d95c788aa22ce39a4807b9a4c31c758a2036dc6/README.md), [MIT LICENSE](https://github.com/RubenGlez/lokalite/blob/6d95c788aa22ce39a4807b9a4c31c758a2036dc6/LICENSE), [path mappings](https://github.com/RubenGlez/lokalite/blob/6d95c788aa22ce39a4807b9a4c31c758a2036dc6/lib/paths.cjs), [CLI](https://github.com/RubenGlez/lokalite/blob/6d95c788aa22ce39a4807b9a4c31c758a2036dc6/cli.js), [tests](https://github.com/RubenGlez/lokalite/blob/6d95c788aa22ce39a4807b9a4c31c758a2036dc6/test.js), [validator](https://github.com/RubenGlez/lokalite/blob/6d95c788aa22ce39a4807b9a4c31c758a2036dc6/scripts/validate.js).

## erickochen/purple — MCP/SSH tooling boundary review (2026-11-20)

- Snapshot: clone HEAD 7129145952be6bb92022d43b4a25a8b38e88f595; purple-ssh 3.34.2, MIT. The original inventory row recorded 779 stars; a separately surfaced GitHub page snapshot showed 725, so the inventory value is retained as a dated/captured snapshot, not treated as a current live count. This Rust TUI/CLI manages local SSH config, cloud-provider host inventory, SSH, containers, tunnels and an MCP stdio server. It is not an AI harness migration or session/memory store.
- MCP interface: stdio JSON-RPC server exposes five typed tools: list_hosts, get_host, list_containers, run_command and container_action. --read-only filters tools/list to the first three and rejects state-changing tools before argument validation. The CLI bool is opt-in (without --read-only, run_command and container_action are exposed); the Claude Desktop .mcpb manifest explicitly passes --read-only and --audit-log. Direct Claude Code/CLI examples use purple mcp, so a user must opt into read-only there. Purple does not supply its own approval gate; approval depends on the MCP client.
- Audit/privacy: audit is enabled by default at the state-directory MCP log, JSON Lines, append-only, owner-only mode 0600 on Unix, and refuses a pre-existing symlink target. run_command command text is redacted, but other tool arguments/host aliases are recorded. A synthetic stdio E2E confirmed the 3 read-only tools, denial of run_command, redacted audit command and mode 0600 without opening an SSH connection. README/PRIVACY say no telemetry; however provider API tokens are stored as plaintext TOML under ~/.purple/providers. Provider sync uses configured cloud APIs; run_command/list_containers use SSH. The bundled MCP server skips the TUI version check.
- Transfer boundary: client setup is stdio command/args configuration (Claude Code, Cursor, Claude Desktop, other stdio clients), not a remote API-Key MCP endpoint. No ChatGPT/Codex Projects, Sections, Session hierarchy, prompts, memory, files/attachments, schedules, or cross-client config importer were found. This is a useful security/capability-policy pattern for Nexus MCP adapters, not a migration dependency or data source.
- Validation: cargo test ran in an isolated HOME with cloud credential variables removed; exit 0 across 23 test binaries: 7,344 passed, 0 failed, 5 ignored (live Proton smoke tests). The suite includes MCP unit tests and tests/mcp_e2e.rs. A separate synthetic read-only MCP E2E also exited 0; no actual SSH/provider or user config was used. Cargo test --offline first exited 101 because the 1.95 toolchain/dependency index was not locally cached (nucleo-matcher missing); retrying online completed successfully.
- Disposition: keep as a pattern for explicit read-only modes, capability filtering, audit redaction and stdio client configuration; exclude from harness data migration. Security caution: direct CLI MCP is not read-only unless flagged, plaintext cloud provider tokens are documented, and returned read-only host details still expose connection metadata.
- Pinned primary sources: [README](https://github.com/erickochen/purple/blob/7129145952be6bb92022d43b4a25a8b38e88f595/README.md), [MCP wiki](https://github.com/erickochen/purple/wiki/MCP-Server), [CLI arguments](https://github.com/erickochen/purple/blob/7129145952be6bb92022d43b4a25a8b38e88f595/src/cli_args.rs), [MCP server source](https://github.com/erickochen/purple/blob/7129145952be6bb92022d43b4a25a8b38e88f595/src/mcp.rs), [.mcpb manifest](https://github.com/erickochen/purple/blob/7129145952be6bb92022d43b4a25a8b38e88f595/mcpb/manifest.template.json), [privacy policy](https://github.com/erickochen/purple/blob/7129145952be6bb92022d43b4a25a8b38e88f595/PRIVACY.md), [MCP E2E tests](https://github.com/erickochen/purple/blob/7129145952be6bb92022d43b4a25a8b38e88f595/tests/mcp_e2e.rs).

## tsonglew/dutis — governed MCP and transaction-pattern review (2026-11-20)

- Snapshot: pinned clone HEAD fa122f83122b7cfbd31d2a08c9979f548ae4f234; package dutis 2.24.0. Cargo.toml and README declare MIT, but the repository snapshot contains no standalone LICENSE file and GitHub license metadata was NOASSERTION; treat reuse as license-confirmation-required before dependency adoption. The candidate is a macOS Launch Services default-app/association manager with CLI, MCP stdio server, one Dutis-specific Agent Skill, profiles, policy, snapshots, and audit. It is not a Harness Session/Project/Section or memory migration tool.
- MCP shape: 17 read-only tools expose app inventory, profiles, recommendations, drift, association query/get, plans, policy, history and audit; 2 mutation tools apply or roll back. Writes are disabled by default. Enabling them requires --allow-writes and DUTIS_MCP_APPROVAL_TOKEN (minimum 16 chars); dutis_apply additionally requires config TOML, fresh plan digest and requester, and rollback requires a reviewed snapshot plan. Mutations pass policy, pre-write snapshot, audit and post-write verification.
- Safety evidence: cargo test ran with isolated HOME/XDG directories and DUTIS/cloud credential variables removed; exit 0 across 6 test binaries: 147 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored. CLI tests use a fake duti executable and temporary configs/state, not actual Launch Services writes. A synthetic MCP stdio E2E exited 0: read-only tools were listed, dutis_apply was rejected as write_disabled, and no system change was attempted. The repo Skill reiterates inspect → plan → policy → approval → apply → verify; the recommendation stays a proposal, not approval.
- Adapter boundary: MCP is local stdio and its client configuration is manual; no Cloud/API-Key endpoint or cross-client config migrator was found. The included Skill operates Dutis only. No AI chat/session hierarchy, Projects/Sections, prompt/context/memory, attachments, or scheduled-task transfer exists. HTTP event forwarding is an outbound event sink, not conversation migration.
- Disposition: strong reference for Nexus's transaction and MCP safety layer (default-deny writes, plan digests, explicit approval, snapshots, policy, audit, verification), not a direct migration engine or dependency until the missing standalone license text is clarified.
- Primary references pinned to clone commit: [README](https://github.com/tsonglew/dutis/blob/fa122f83122b7cfbd31d2a08c9979f548ae4f234/README.md), [Cargo metadata](https://github.com/tsonglew/dutis/blob/fa122f83122b7cfbd31d2a08c9979f548ae4f234/Cargo.toml), [MCP server](https://github.com/tsonglew/dutis/blob/fa122f83122b7cfbd31d2a08c9979f548ae4f234/src/mcp.rs), [governance](https://github.com/tsonglew/dutis/blob/fa122f83122b7cfbd31d2a08c9979f548ae4f234/src/governance.rs), [snapshots](https://github.com/tsonglew/dutis/blob/fa122f83122b7cfbd31d2a08c9979f548ae4f234/src/snapshot.rs), [Dutis skill](https://github.com/tsonglew/dutis/blob/fa122f83122b7cfbd31d2a08c9979f548ae4f234/skills/dutis/SKILL.md).


## Deep review: shenysun/skills-manager (2026-11-20)

- **Identity and scope:** Original 303-row GitHub discovery candidate; MIT; 243-star metadata snapshot; npm CLI `skills-manager-cli` version `2026.11.4`; pinned clone HEAD `948db52fa5dcfdae994073cf9d8c1fd05ab89205`. README describes a CLI-first hub/distributor for 70+ agent path catalogs. Its maintained domain is Skill packages and installation state, not conversation/session migration.
- **Implemented model and writes:** A single canonical local hub holds imported Skills and provenance. User-scope distribution defaults to symlink; project-scope distribution defaults to copy. Source inspection found collision refusal unless explicitly forced, containment checking, snapshots/restore points, stale-copy/fingerprint checks, and scope-dependent rollback limits (user-scope rollback is supported; project-scope restoration relies on the project's own restore point). The catalog is extracted from Vercel's `skills` agent definitions, and `catalog refresh` fetches upstream `agents.ts`, `detect-agent.ts`, and the GitHub commit API. This provides a useful resource adapter pattern, but the inventory/path mapping is not proof that every target runtime discovers, trusts, or activates a Skill.
- **Nexus boundary:** No ChatGPT export reader, ChatGPT Project/Section model, Codex Session/Thread graph, branch/fork/subagent relation, session writer, attachment store, token accounting from transcripts, MCP/plugin/permission transfer, memory migration, or scheduled-task conversion was found in the reviewed command/domain surfaces. Its resident-cost ledger approximates context size from Skill frontmatter; that is not provider token usage or billable cost. Keep this as an adjacent Skill-library/distribution reference, not as a Nexus migration engine or core dependency.
- **Install and test method:** `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts` completed with exit 0 (360 packages; lifecycle scripts disabled). The full `pnpm test` run exited 1: 116 test files passed, 4 failed; 1,048 tests passed, 4 failed out of 1,052. The test command's prelude successfully ran clean + TypeScript compilation and copied the bundled catalog into `dist`.
- **Failure triage (verified, no source edits):** (1) `sync-init` and `sync-push` tests expected identity failure with isolated `HOME` and no `GIT_*` variables, but Git on this macOS host still auto-detects a local author identity from the OS account/hostname. Under the clean test environment those two tests failed because CLI exit status was 0; setting `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0=user.useConfigOnly`, `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0=true` made both tests pass (2 passed, 26 skipped). This verifies the error path but also shows that the existing “isolated HOME means no Git identity” fixture assumption is not portable. Do not claim the product rejected auto-detected identity on this host.
- **Host-dependent bootstrap test:** Its “no agent detected” assertion failed even with an empty child environment and a fresh temporary HOME: the CLI mounted to the ZCode path. The installed app `/Applications/ZCode.app` exists on this machine, and the current official upstream detector explicitly treats that absolute system path as installed; isolating `HOME` alone cannot hermetically test this case. The upstream path is visible in [Vercel's current agent source](https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills/blob/main/src/agents.ts#L2164-L2172).
- **Freshness finding:** `tests/services/doctor-catalog.test.ts` injects `2026-08-20T00:00:00Z` then asserts age `< 90`; at the 2026-11-20 review date the computed age was 92 days, so the fixture itself is stale. More importantly, the packaged catalog's own source stamp is `2026-08-18T20:06:26Z` (93 days by the CLI's floored calculation), while `DoctorService` warns at `ageDays > 90`. Thus the bundled agent-path catalog was genuinely stale at review time. This is a maintenance limitation for a product whose value depends on current harness paths, not merely a flaky test.
- **Refresh attempt:** Ran the packaged CLI's `catalog refresh` against a fresh `/tmp` Skill home with no credentials. It exited 1 with `Failed to fetch https://api.github.com/repos/vercel-labs/skills/commits?path=src/agents.ts&per_page=1: 403`. No refresh was written; this observation does not establish whether the 403 was rate limiting, policy, or an endpoint defect. The refresh path therefore remains unverified here. The current official Vercel [agent directory](https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills/blob/main/docs/agents.md) and [source table](https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills/blob/main/src/agents.ts) demonstrate that the upstream mapping is actively evolving, reinforcing that a stale bundled snapshot must be visible and safely refreshable.
- **Disposition:** Retain as a high-value adjacent Skills hub / path-distribution reference; exclude as a direct ChatGPT/Codex Projects/Session importer. Nexus may borrow canonical resource storage, per-scope apply mode, provenance and restore-point concepts, but should maintain its own versioned capability evidence and require per-target activation/resume verification. Full test suite remains not green at 1,048/1,052; four failing tests are triaged above, and the actual catalog freshness issue remains open.

Sources: [pinned README](https://github.com/shenysun/skills-manager/blob/948db52fa5dcfdae994073cf9d8c1fd05ab89205/README.md), [pinned MIT LICENSE](https://github.com/shenysun/skills-manager/blob/948db52fa5dcfdae994073cf9d8c1fd05ab89205/LICENSE), [pinned catalog refresh service](https://github.com/shenysun/skills-manager/blob/948db52fa5dcfdae994073cf9d8c1fd05ab89205/src/core/services/catalog-service.ts), [Vercel agent catalog](https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills/blob/main/src/agents.ts), and [Vercel agent path documentation](https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills/blob/main/docs/agents.md).

## Deep review: Sai-Nitin123/ContextBridge (2026-11-20)

- **Identity and provenance:** Original 303-row candidate, 0-star metadata snapshot, local HEAD `2a4b9b460387679c9dcced9b7cad3b6afa0aa6d8`; package `contextbridge` 0.2.0. README and package.json claim MIT, but the pinned git tree has no `LICENSE` file and the registry GitHub SPDX field is null. Treat license status as unresolved before reuse. The repository tracks 3,003 files, including 2,937 node_modules files and 9 dist files; no clean release artifact or standalone license text was present.
+- **Stated product vs implemented boundary:** README claims complete context capture, one-click ChatGPT/Claude/etc transfer, QR handoff, `.aicx` bundles and zero telemetry. The source implements browser-page DOM adapters and composer injection. `extension/manifest.json` runs the content script on `<all_urls>` and grants `<all_urls>` host permissions, plus activeTab, storage, clipboard, downloads, sidePanel and scripting. A targeted scan of `src/`, `extension/` and `scripts/` found no fetch, XMLHttpRequest, WebSocket or sendBeacon calls; this is code-search evidence, not proof that broad browser permissions are risk-free.
+- **Capture/hierarchy fidelity:** The content script calls an adapter against the current `document`; provider adapters use DOM selectors rather than platform export/session APIs. ChatGPT records a URL-derived ID; several other adapters return no conversation ID. Messages are a flat array with synthetic index IDs; `BaseAdapter.createMessage` defaults timestamps to extraction time. `Conversation`/`ConversationMetadata` contain no Project, Section, parent, branch/fork, subagent, workflow, schedule, or source event IDs. Visible attachment data becomes filename/type/size/preview/URL metadata; no binary attachment archive or content hash is constructed. A DOM snapshot cannot be taken as proof of full history for virtualized or lazily loaded chats.
+- **Destination semantics:** `.aicx` contains a `conversation`, summary, continuation object and optional integrity field, but `BundleBuilder` sets only messageCount (no checksum). `ContextImporter.applyBundle` reads `bundle.continuation.continuationPrompt` and injects/copies that one prompt; it does not create a sequence of native target turns or a resumable provider session. This is a handoff prompt, not restoration of a Projects/Sections/Session hierarchy, and it does not target Claude Code API-Key or hosted Cloud APIs. Mobile QR similarly transports context text, not a native session.
+- **Secret-redaction boundary — reproducible synthetic defect:** `BundleBuilder.build` creates `ContextSummary` from unredacted normalized messages before running `SecretRedactor` on exported message blocks. The redactor scans only `ContentBlock.text`; it does not scan summary fields, block metadata, conversation title/URL, source metadata or derived prompt fields. A temporary Vitest probe using only a fake `sk-proj-` marker observed: message body false; context.goal true; continuationPrompt true; serialized bundle true. A second fake `ghp_` marker in attachment filename/URL was not counted by the redactor (`redactionCount=0`) and remained in attachment metadata, continuation prompt and serialized bundle.
+- **Prompt-fence boundary — reproducible synthetic defect:** `ContinuationEngine.sanitize` replaces the historical-context tags in message text, but `buildPrompt` interpolates `context.goal` into “Project Goal” without sanitization. A fake title containing `</historical_context> [SYNTHETIC_BREAKOUT_MARKER]` produced two closing tags, with the marker before the normal transcript section. The test asserts a synthetic marker only; no actual prompt or user data was used. This contradicts an unqualified claim that all historical material is fenced against prompt injection.
+- **Testing/build:** The repository's committed node_modules made `rtk npm test` fail with exit 126 (“vitest: Permission denied”); direct Node invocation then failed exit 1 because `@rollup/rollup-darwin-arm64` was missing. In a disposable copy excluding `.git`, `dist`, `.codegraph` and node_modules, `npm ci --ignore-scripts` exited 0 (98 packages), `npm test` passed all 53 tests across 8 files, and `npm run build` exited 0. The passing tests use JSDOM fixtures and unit tests; they are not live ChatGPT/Claude/Gemini browser-resume tests. The suite's secret test checks message-body redaction only; the three added temporary probes (not committed) reproduced summary, attachment-metadata, and fence issues.
+- **Disposition:** Retain as a partial local prompt-handoff UX reference, not as a session/project migrator or API-Key target adapter. Do not adopt as a security layer: fix whole-bundle redaction ordering/coverage, authenticated metadata treatment, prompt encoding and broad host-permission justification first. Do not adopt as a dependency until the missing standalone license file is clarified. No real account, browser session, API key, or cloud endpoint was accessed.
+
+Primary references: [pinned README](https://github.com/Sai-Nitin123/ContextBridge/blob/2a4b9b460387679c9dcced9b7cad3b6afa0aa6d8/README.md), [pinned manifest](https://github.com/Sai-Nitin123/ContextBridge/blob/2a4b9b460387679c9dcced9b7cad3b6afa0aa6d8/extension/manifest.json), [pinned package metadata](https://github.com/Sai-Nitin123/ContextBridge/blob/2a4b9b460387679c9dcced9b7cad3b6afa0aa6d8/package.json), [BundleBuilder](https://github.com/Sai-Nitin123/ContextBridge/blob/2a4b9b460387679c9dcced9b7cad3b6afa0aa6d8/src/core/bundle-builder.ts), [redactor](https://github.com/Sai-Nitin123/ContextBridge/blob/2a4b9b460387679c9dcced9b7cad3b6afa0aa6d8/src/security/redactor.ts), and [importer](https://github.com/Sai-Nitin123/ContextBridge/blob/2a4b9b460387679c9dcced9b7cad3b6afa0aa6d8/src/import/importer.ts).

## Deep review: MaazzAlii/context-saver-extension (2026-11-20)

- **Identity and provenance:** This is one of the original 303 GitHub-search candidates, matched as a ChatGPT/Claude conversation-transfer project. At the recorded search snapshot it had 3 stars, 0 forks, and a null GitHub SPDX license field; the repository page still showed 3 stars, 0 forks and five commits at review time. The local source is pinned to `2e46bc79b266089a67ccc0c61d32f6cfce5a8f7e`. The tracked tree has no standalone LICENSE, although README.md says MIT; treat licensing as unresolved and do not adopt it as a dependency until clarified.
- **Implemented boundary:** It is a Chrome Manifest V3 extension with DOM content scripts for ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, Copilot and Perplexity. It scrolls the currently open page and reads visible DOM text; it does not call a provider's export, account history, Project, Section, session, or import API. Output is a continuation prompt for manual copy/paste, not native destination writes.
- **Capture completeness and structure:** The collector deduplicates messages by the first 150 text characters, truncates each captured DOM message at 5,000 characters, derives ordering from `rect.top + window.scrollY`, and scrolls in 600-pixel steps with 750-ms pauses, capped at 150 steps or six empty/no-movement steps. It has no `reachedTop`/completion flag in its result. If any messages were collected, the caller marks capture successful after loop termination even when the cap/empty-streak ended the loop before the top; the overlay then says all messages were captured. This is a source-level false-completeness risk, not a reproduced browser test. Nested scrollers can also make the window-relative ordering heuristic unreliable. Fallback mode alternates roles over at most 80 long DOM elements, which is heuristic rather than source role evidence.
- **Session shape and fidelity:** Extracted data is only `platform`, `messages[{role,text}]`, current `url`, page `title`, and capture `timestamp`. Saved items use `id`, `platform`, `title`, `url`, `timestamp`, `messages`, and a generated raw `prompt`; storage is capped at 20. The 5–100 slider defaults to 50 and limits raw-copy prompt messages; modes are full/minimal/smart. There are no source message IDs, event/tool/result records, parent/fork/subtask edges, project/Section grouping, model/token/cost data, asset bytes/checksums, Skills/MCP/plugins, memory, schedules, or target-native restore semantics. The Mistral summary is displayed in a separate box; despite a comment saying it updates the saved session, the summary handler writes no session record and save-session stores `promptBox.textContent`, not `summaryBox.textContent`.
- **Egress, keys and permissions:** Clicking AI Summary sends all captured cleaned messages to `https://api.mistral.ai/v1/chat/completions` using `mistral-small-latest`; each message is capped to 1,500 characters for that request, but there is no aggregate input-token budget in the inspected code. The API key is stored as a raw value under `mistralKey` in `chrome.storage.local`, after a bearer-authenticated `/v1/models` check. This is user-triggered external content egress, not an all-local workflow; README's local-storage row must be read alongside its explicit Mistral-egress disclosure. The privacy policy lists Claude API and other configurable providers, but a targeted scan of the pinned runtime source found Mistral fetches only; `api.anthropic.com` appears in manifest host permissions but not in the inspected request code. No secret redaction was found in the capture/summarization path.
- **Validation and disposition:** The clone has no package.json, test directory, or build script. `node --check` passed for `content.js`, `popup.js`, and `background.js`; `manifest.json` parsed as JSON. No browser extraction, live Mistral request, or target restore was run. Evidence level is E2 (pinned source); syntax checks are not behavior/fidelity tests. Retain only as a low-value manual prompt-handoff/UI reference; exclude it as a Nexus session adapter, Project/Section migrator, API-Key Cloud writer, or dependency until it gains verifiable source-preserving export/import, hierarchy/assets handling, bounded-loss reporting, behavioral tests, and a clear license.
- **Pinned primary references:** [repository snapshot](https://github.com/MaazzAlii/context-saver-extension/tree/2e46bc79b266089a67ccc0c61d32f6cfce5a8f7e), [README](https://github.com/MaazzAlii/context-saver-extension/blob/2e46bc79b266089a67ccc0c61d32f6cfce5a8f7e/README.md), [content collector](https://github.com/MaazzAlii/context-saver-extension/blob/2e46bc79b266089a67ccc0c61d32f6cfce5a8f7e/content.js), [popup and API flow](https://github.com/MaazzAlii/context-saver-extension/blob/2e46bc79b266089a67ccc0c61d32f6cfce5a8f7e/popup.js), [manifest](https://github.com/MaazzAlii/context-saver-extension/blob/2e46bc79b266089a67ccc0c61d32f6cfce5a8f7e/manifest.json), and [privacy policy](https://github.com/MaazzAlii/context-saver-extension/blob/2e46bc79b266089a67ccc0c61d32f6cfce5a8f7e/privacy-policy.md).

## Deep review: laohu-aiskills/agent-session-vault (2026-11-20)

- **Identity and provenance:** Original GitHub-corpus candidate; metadata snapshot records MIT, 1 star, 0 forks, last push 2026-09-28. The local shallow checkout is pinned at 8f46611c7a6b6ae2fc916f45888ca69f15fc4157; a standalone MIT LICENSE is present. The repository's page lists 1 star and 0 forks. CodeGraph was initialized and verified current (23 files, 354 nodes, 1,019 edges).
- **What it actually reads:** A local-first CLI/Electron catalog and archive utility, not a cloud account connector. Source adapters cover Claude Code, Codex CLI, WorkBuddy, Qwen Work, Kimi CLI/Code, ZCode, OpenCode and Pi; CodeBuddy is registered but unverified in this README snapshot. It reports Copilot CLI, Cursor, Cline and Antigravity as restricted when their local history bodies are unavailable. It has no ChatGPT export parser and no ChatGPT Projects/Sections index. A targeted source scan for fetch/HTTP/WebSocket request patterns in src/desktop/bin produced no matches (grep exit 1 = no matches); this is source-search evidence, not a proof about every future build/dependency.
- **Canonical/session fidelity:** The model is one Session metadata record plus a flat ordered Message list with role/kind/text/tool fields and optional provider metadata. It can parse many local formats, including Codex message, reasoning, tool-call/result and token-count events, but the normalized model has no parent/fork/branch/subtask/session-group edges. Claude subagent transcripts may be discovered as separate JSONL sessions, without a canonical parent-child edge. Project path/slug is captured, not a generic Project/Section hierarchy. Original raw files are available through backup; migration is a lossy projection.
- **Backup/restore strength and boundary:** Backup copies original files, records SHA-256/size and session metadata, and includes SQLite WAL/SHM for the ZCode/OpenCode provider. Restore defaults to a plan-only dry run; writing requires explicit --apply and --yes, and existing-file replacement additionally requires --overwrite. Cross-machine --target remaps source-home paths. These are useful transaction patterns, but the restore manifest is not treated as an untrusted archive: relToAbs splits/rejoins paths without rejecting dot-dot segments, planRestore accepts manifest paths directly, and applyRestore writes the resulting destination without checking containment under an authorized target/provider root.
- **Reproduced synthetic path-escape defect:** Using only temporary synthetic files, created a backup manifest whose file rel was ../escaped.txt and whose SHA-256 matched its synthetic payload. The plan printed ../escaped.txt; with --apply --yes --verify, the CLI wrote one file to the parent of its current working directory. Repeating with --target /tmp/.../target-home still wrote to the working-directory parent, not target-home; the target-home file did not exist. No user data or real Agent directories were read or modified. The finding is a local crafted/tampered-backup path-scope escape; the dry-run and confirmation gates do not enforce destination containment. Do not apply untrusted bundles until this is fixed.
- **Migration boundary:** Targets are local file dialects for WorkBuddy, Claude Code and Qwen Work (Claude-style JSONL) plus CodeBuddy (codebuddy dialect). Default migration keeps only text messages and emits a new session UUID, sequential JSONL, a machine-readable loss note and manual placement instructions. Tool calls/results/reasoning are counted then dropped by default; --force can write unsupported blocks but warns the destination may not parse them. It does not update the target native database/index. There is no Claude Cloud/API-Key writer, ChatGPT source, ChatGPT/Codex Project/Section graph, native fork restore, Skills/MCP/plugin/memory/scheduler transfer, or cross-provider artifact-byte migration. The text-only loss report is useful as a transparency reference, not proof of high fidelity.
- **Validation scope:** README advertises a 36-assertion npm run verify integration script, but the inspected test/verify.js indexes real local history under the caller's home directory and is not fixture-isolated; it was deliberately not run to avoid reading the user's private agent histories. It writes scratch output in the clone but reads and indexes host session sources. A safe static parser wrapped all 22 JavaScript files in a CommonJS/async context and passed; no native provider discovery/resume or build was run. Three earlier parser attempts failed because the harness did not model CommonJS top-level return, async test code, or a shebang; the corrected wrapper stripped a leading shebang and passed all 22 (these were checker-harness errors, not source failures).
- **Additional source/test limitations:** backup.js copies the SQLite database, WAL and SHM as separate filesystem copies; it does not call SQLite online-backup/checkpoint APIs or lock a writer, so consistency under concurrent database writes is unproven. The integration check verifies restored copies against hashes generated for the backup manifest, not against a frozen source snapshot. Under --force, the Claude tool_result writer sets tool_use_id to null and the CodeBuddy writer derives each tool call/result callId from that message’s new id, so tool pairing is not preserved. README says “three” sources are restricted but lists four (Copilot CLI, Cursor, Cline, Antigravity). These are source-review caveats; no live source database or real history was opened.
- **Disposition:** Keep as a valuable local provider/discovery, raw backup, explicit loss-report and dry-run workflow reference. Do not use as Nexus's ChatGPT/Codex-to-Cloud adapter or as a dependency for restore until manifest path containment and untrusted-input tests are added. Its strongest contribution to Nexus is a provider registry plus original-file archive; its main warning is that explicit confirmation/hash checks do not replace destination-root validation.
- **Pinned primary references:** [repository](https://github.com/laohu-aiskills/agent-session-vault/tree/8f46611c7a6b6ae2fc916f45888ca69f15fc4157), [README and advertised capability boundaries](https://github.com/laohu-aiskills/agent-session-vault/blob/8f46611c7a6b6ae2fc916f45888ca69f15fc4157/README.md), [provider registry](https://github.com/laohu-aiskills/agent-session-vault/blob/8f46611c7a6b6ae2fc916f45888ca69f15fc4157/src/core/providers/index.js), [migration writer](https://github.com/laohu-aiskills/agent-session-vault/blob/8f46611c7a6b6ae2fc916f45888ca69f15fc4157/src/core/migrate.js), [backup path conversion](https://github.com/laohu-aiskills/agent-session-vault/blob/8f46611c7a6b6ae2fc916f45888ca69f15fc4157/src/core/backup.js), [restore planner/writer](https://github.com/laohu-aiskills/agent-session-vault/blob/8f46611c7a6b6ae2fc916f45888ca69f15fc4157/src/core/restore.js), and [real-home integration script](https://github.com/laohu-aiskills/agent-session-vault/blob/8f46611c7a6b6ae2fc916f45888ca69f15fc4157/test/verify.js).

## Deep review: llxgdtop/Agent-Session-Migration (2026-11-20)

- **Identity and provenance:** Original GitHub-corpus candidate, Apache-2.0, pinned local HEAD 72263bfbcbf3a010c99ad8f1ed3242fd3a66add5. The repository page showed 2 stars, 1 fork and 45 commits at review time. The desktop release path is macOS-only; Windows/Linux are listed as roadmap items.
- **Real scope:** It is a local native app for Claude Code, Codex CLI and ZCode, with all six pairwise source/target directions. It reads Claude/Codex JSONL and ZCode's two SQLite databases, then writes new local Claude/Codex files or ZCode rows. There is no ChatGPT export/API adapter, no ChatGPT Project/Section index, no Claude Cloud API-Key/session writer, and no Skills/MCP/plugin/memory/scheduler transfer.
- **Hierarchy and metadata:** UnifiedSession stores session id/path/title/project directory/activity/message count and a flat sequence of User/Assistant messages; UnifiedPart has Text/Reasoning/ToolCall/ToolResult. It has no parent/fork/branch/subagent/session-group graph, event IDs, attachments/assets, model/provider, token/cost ledger or automation data. Claude scanning can find nested JSONL, but sidechain messages are skipped; parentUuid ancestry is not read, while the Claude writer generates a fresh linear parentUuid chain and sets isSidechain=false/gitBranch empty. Codex event_msg records (including token-count events) are skipped on input; the reader handles response_item text/tool/reasoning but drops encrypted reasoning and other event types.
- **What “tool calls included” really means:** The README says messages, reasoning and tool-call records are retained, but render_parts concatenates all parts into one text string. Reasoning is capped at 1,000 characters, tool input at 500 and tool output at 2,000; target writers emit text blocks rather than executable native tool-call/result events. The Codex reader drops event_msg token counts; the IR has no usage fields. ZCode reads only the first recognized part per message, and a tool part contributes state.input but not state.output. It ignores attachments/file blocks. Thus “included” means an annotated, sometimes truncated textual rendering, not structured tool replay or full context.
- **Target metadata is synthesized:** The Codex writer hard-codes CLI version 0.146.0, model gpt-5 and approval_policy on-request. The ZCode writer hard-codes provider glm and model GLM-5.3-Flash. These values may not describe the original source session or the user's actual target model configuration. Source token usage, model, permission decisions, costs and tool execution outcomes are not preserved as first-class data.
- **ZCode transaction failure — reproduced in a disposable test copy:** The writer commits the CLI database transaction before it begins the separate tasks-index transaction. I added a temp-only trigger that aborts task insertion and ran one integration test: the API returned an error, the CLI DB still contained the newly migrated session row, and the tasks DB had no task row. Result: 1 passed, 1 filtered. This contradicts an unqualified “atomic migration” claim across the two databases; only each individual database transaction is atomic.
- **ZCode backup/WAL gap — reproduced in a disposable test copy:** backup_db uses fs::copy on the main database file only; it does not include SQLite WAL/SHM or use the online backup API. A synthetic test kept a live WAL connection open with a committed sentinel row, migrated a fixture, then read the timestamped backup. The backup omitted the row still present in the live WAL. Result: 1 passed, 2 filtered. The app warns when a target process is running but lets the user proceed after confirmation, so a backup taken while ZCode is live is not a proven rollback point.
- **Other safety and fidelity notes:** Claude/Codex file writes use temp-file-plus-rename and refuse an existing target. ZCode checks a narrow schema-migration prefix and creates backups before writing. However, the two-store transaction is not atomic as a unit; backups are second-resolution names and can collide; sensitive-data redaction is explicitly on the roadmap, not implemented. ZCode source databases and active app behavior were tested only with synthetic schemas, not a real current ZCode installation.
- **Validation:** On the pinned checkout, cargo test --workspace exited 0 with 95 passed across six suites. Standard cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets exited 0 with two collapsible_match warnings in reader/codex.rs; adding -D warnings exited 101 for those lint errors. The Rust integration tests use hand-built fixtures/tempdirs and verify JSON shapes/source immutability, but do not launch Claude Code/Codex/ZCode to resume a migrated real session. No native app/API-Key Cloud test was run.
- **Disposition:** High-value reference for per-format readers/writers, additive file writes, schema guards, deterministic fixtures and explicit temp-directory tests. Not a complete or lossless Nexus solution, not a ChatGPT/Project/Section adapter, and not safe to reuse as a ZCode transaction/rollback implementation without cross-database coordination, WAL-aware backup, model/config provenance, secret scanning and structured loss reporting.
- **Pinned primary references:** [repository](https://github.com/llxgdtop/Agent-Session-Migration/tree/72263bfbcbf3a010c99ad8f1ed3242fd3a66add5), [README claims and platform scope](https://github.com/llxgdtop/Agent-Session-Migration/blob/72263bfbcbf3a010c99ad8f1ed3242fd3a66add5/README.md), [IR schema](https://github.com/llxgdtop/Agent-Session-Migration/blob/72263bfbcbf3a010c99ad8f1ed3242fd3a66add5/crates/hub-core/src/ir.rs), [text-flattening mapper](https://github.com/llxgdtop/Agent-Session-Migration/blob/72263bfbcbf3a010c99ad8f1ed3242fd3a66add5/crates/hub-core/src/mapper/mod.rs), [Claude reader](https://github.com/llxgdtop/Agent-Session-Migration/blob/72263bfbcbf3a010c99ad8f1ed3242fd3a66add5/crates/hub-core/src/reader/claude.rs), [Codex writer](https://github.com/llxgdtop/Agent-Session-Migration/blob/72263bfbcbf3a010c99ad8f1ed3242fd3a66add5/crates/hub-core/src/writer/codex.rs), [ZCode writer](https://github.com/llxgdtop/Agent-Session-Migration/blob/72263bfbcbf3a010c99ad8f1ed3242fd3a66add5/crates/hub-core/src/writer/zcode.rs), and [fixture-driven integration tests](https://github.com/llxgdtop/Agent-Session-Migration/tree/72263bfbcbf3a010c99ad8f1ed3242fd3a66add5/crates/hub-core/tests).


## Deep review: YURIBEDRAN/rubi (cross-harness memory sidecar; 2026-11-20)

- **Identity and current pin:** MIT, JavaScript/Node >=18, package version 0.6.0, zero declared runtime dependencies. Reviewed clean checkout `YURIBEDRAN/rubi` at `6780b453a1f8587b42bec318e55b912e13b4ca5e` (2026-10-21 commit); `git ls-remote` confirmed the public `main` ref still resolves to the same commit during this review. The live GitHub page showed 14 stars / 1 fork at capture. This is an evidence snapshot, not a recommendation based on popularity.
- **Real scope:** A shared, local-first memory/code-map service for Claude Code, Claude Desktop and multiple coding harnesses. Project memory is plain Markdown in `<project>/.rubi/`; personal memory lives in a vault. Its *memory* hierarchy is `mapa.md` (overview), `modulos/*.md` (module summaries), graph nodes in `conhecimento/*.md`, plus separate `handoffs/` and git-ignored `sessoes/` records. Automatic code structure uses file/import analysis and PageRank; memory retrieval fuses BM25, graph Personalized PageRank and RRF. Semantic fact extraction and query expansion still rely on the active model deciding what to register and which related terms to ask for.
- **Harness support is not data migration:** The bundled Claude plugin/skills and MCP server expose the Rubi memory tools. `scripts/conectar.mjs` configures ten additional clients (Codex, Gemini CLI, Cursor, Copilot for VS Code, Windsurf, Cline, OpenCode, Continue, Goose and Zed). Only Codex and Gemini receive installed hooks in that connector; most others receive MCP plus instruction files, so recall depends on the model following instructions. YAML, Goose and Zed snippets are printed for manual placement; JSON with comments falls back to a manual snippet. It can install its own connector, not transfer arbitrary user Skills, MCP servers, plugins, schedules, project metadata or session trees. The README explicitly says claude.ai/mobile support is experimental and not validated. The HTTP MCP mode is a local connector, not a Claude Cloud session-import/API-Key writer.
- **Session fidelity boundary:** The only inspected transcript reader is Claude Code's pre-compaction summarizer, not a cross-harness archive importer. `registroSessao()` begins after the latest `compact_boundary`, skips `isSidechain`, reads user string/text blocks only (and discards content arrays containing `tool_result`), clips each user request to 600 characters, and extracts edited files, short Bash descriptions/commands, and the last assistant text. The Markdown record retains only the last 15 requests, up to 40 files, up to 25 commands and 2,500 characters of the last assistant response.
