Non-text image/audio/file blocks, stable per-event IDs/timestamps, tool call/result structure, token/cost usage, branch/fork/subagent ancestry and project/Section membership are not represented by this record. Handoff is a concise continuation note, not a replayable transcript. Therefore Rubi's directory/graph hierarchy must not be mistaken for session hierarchy.
- **Security probes, synthetic only:** `rubi_registrar` documents “Never store secrets” but the write path has no deterministic secret scanner/redactor. A disposable MCP app-mode call stored the synthetic marker `SYNTHETIC_TOKEN_DO_NOT_USE_7f53a2` unchanged in a Markdown node (exit 0); no real credentials or user data were used. `lerDaMemoria()` checks the resolved *lexical* path prefix but does not resolve symlinks: a synthetic symlink inside `.rubi/conhecimento/` to a sibling file read `SYNTHETIC_OUTSIDE_MARKER` successfully (exit 0). Do not expose Rubi's reader to untrusted memory trees until realpath containment is enforced; do not treat natural-language instructions as secret redaction.
- **Network/config boundary:** Optional HTTP MCP requires `RUBI_TOKEN` length >=24, routes by `/mcp/<token>`, caps a request body at 2 MB and binds `127.0.0.1`. That is loopback-only unless separately forwarded/tunneled and does not create a hosted-session import endpoint. Connector backup logic keeps only the first `.bak-rubi`; it preserves existing config content where parsing succeeds but manually delegates unsafe/commented formats.
- **Validation and claims:** `npm test` exited 0 with 17/17 passing. The suite uses temporary project/vault fixtures and includes a Claude pre-compaction record/handoff test, MCP stdio/HTTP checks and Codex/Cursor configuration examples; it is not a live account, browser, cloud import, cross-harness session-resume or secret-redaction suite. README benchmark figures (transcript replay simulations and small controlled task studies) are author-reported context/cost results, not measured session-migration fidelity or independent accuracy rates.
- **Disposition:** Strong architecture/reference for durable project memory, deterministic code maps, hybrid recall, harness-specific MCP wiring and explicit tool-vs-instruction integration levels. Not a ChatGPT/Codex Project/Section/session importer, not a native Cloud/API-Key conversation writer, and not a replacement for a loss-aware source/target session adapter. Nexus may borrow the distinct memory sidecar and adapter-install pattern only after adding deterministic secret redaction, symlink-safe reads/writes, opt-in egress, and tests that distinguish summaries from full event archives.
- **Pinned primary references:** [current repository page](https://github.com/YURIBEDRAN/rubi), [pinned README](https://github.com/YURIBEDRAN/rubi/blob/6780b453a1f8587b42bec318e55b912e13b4ca5e/README.md), [connector matrix/installer](https://github.com/YURIBEDRAN/rubi/blob/6780b453a1f8587b42bec318e55b912e13b4ca5e/scripts/conectar.mjs), [transcript extraction](https://github.com/YURIBEDRAN/rubi/blob/6780b453a1f8587b42bec318e55b912e13b4ca5e/scripts/lib/transcript.mjs), [memory path handling](https://github.com/YURIBEDRAN/rubi/blob/6780b453a1f8587b42bec318e55b912e13b4ca5e/scripts/lib/memoria.mjs), [MCP schemas and HTTP server](https://github.com/YURIBEDRAN/rubi/blob/6780b453a1f8587b42bec318e55b912e13b4ca5e/scripts/mcp-server.mjs), and [test suite](https://github.com/YURIBEDRAN/rubi/blob/6780b453a1f8587b42bec318e55b912e13b4ca5e/test/plugin.test.mjs).

## Deep review: luckeyfaraday/athena-code (local history search and native resume; 2026-11-20)

- **Identity/current pin:** MIT, TypeScript/Bun, memory-focused OpenCode fork. Reviewed clean main at `63e5143d8122343fda0130e728ddd4215bd4edb1`; live `git ls-remote` for main returned the same SHA. GitHub page showed 27 stars / 3 forks; latest visible release was v0.5.0 (2026-06-24), while README still labels the project early development and notes unsigned macOS/Windows builds.
- **Actual value:** It combines Athena's global and per-project durable memory with a frozen per-session snapshot, query-scoped memory recall, and a local SQLite FTS5 index over Athena's own sessions plus local Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode and Hermes history. It can find a prior source session and launch that same native session with its original agent CLI. This is a strong *local search + source-native resume* pattern.
- **It is not a portable migration engine:** The on-disk scanner has only four external ScanRoots (Claude Code, Codex, Hermes, OpenCode). Targeted search of executable overlay/test sources found no ChatGPT adapter or CHATGPT_ENCRYPTION_KEY use. It creates no ChatGPT Project/Section objects, no Claude Cloud/API-Key conversation, no generic target session, and no cross-platform session archive writer. Its takeover runs a native resume command against the original session ID; it does not copy the conversation into another harness. MCP, arbitrary Skills/plugins, schedules, permission/config, assets and Cloud app state are outside this session-search feature.
- **Normalized search row is deliberately lossy:** The SQLite message schema stores only agent, session_id, workspace, role, timestamp and text; a source table stores path and a cheap fingerprint. No parent/fork/subagent/branch edges, original event/message IDs, tool-call/result structure, attachments, model/provider, tokens/cost or permission history are stored. Claude parser includes text and thinking but skips tool_use/tool_result/images; its root walker only enumerates JSONL directly in each project folder. Codex parser keeps user/assistant message text from response_item, drops event_msg/encrypted reasoning and non-text blocks. Hermes keeps user/assistant text and also flattens assistant reasoning into text; OpenCode reads text/reasoning parts only. Thus FTS results are excerpts, not replayable source records.
- **No ChatGPT means no Projects/Sections fidelity:** The executable scanner has no ChatGPT root or parser; its search identity is a flat (agent, session, workspace) grouping. There is no Project/Section membership, source thread tree, or per-message branch graph to map to the user's ChatGPT hierarchy.
- **Privacy/security:** README tells users not to store secrets in memory, but the external session-index path has no deterministic redaction before SQLite writes. A temp-only Bun probe indexed the synthetic marker `SYNTHETIC_TOKEN_DO_NOT_USE_59cd71` unchanged (inserted=1, persisted=true); no real user data or credentials were used. Claude thinking and Hermes reasoning are indexed as plain text too. The reviewed session index has no source-session deletion/retention routine; the only DELETE in that module reclaims an OpenCode row when Athena takes ownership. This matters because removed source files can leave stale indexed text until the local database is removed/rebuilt. Tool prompts default to permissionless/all tools allowed when the user has no rules; that is a major adoption/security consideration, unrelated to session portability.
- **Resume semantics:** session_takeover validates that an ID exists in the local search DB, then either emits an in-app takeover event or launches claude --resume, codex resume, opencode --session or hermes --resume. It does not create target history in another service. If a stored workspace path no longer exists, the plan may fall back to a supplied or current directory; this can launch a resumed CLI under the wrong workspace and deserves a visible confirmation gate in a migration product.
- **Tests/validation:** Repository suite run from a disposable HOME/ATHENA_CODE_HOME and temporary test roots with `npx --yes bun@1.3.14 test test`: 98 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed (99 tests; skipped optional sessions-search oracle). The first run exited 1 on macOS because the fixture path used /var/folders while git returned its canonical /private/var path; rerunning with TMPDIR under /private/tmp made all tests pass. Tests use synthetic files/databases and stub child commands; they do not resume a real agent session, connect to Claude Cloud, or test ChatGPT. No real local agent histories were scanned.
- **Disposition:** High-value reference for local session discovery, FTS, cross-agent attribution, workspaces, native resume, and separating curated memory from searchable history. Not a ChatGPT/Codex-to-Claude Cloud/API-Key migrator; do not use its search-hit schema as Nexus's canonical archive. Nexus should keep an immutable event/asset archive separate from any recall index, support explicit source consent and retention/deletion, redact before indexing, and treat native resume as a source-side action rather than migration success.
- **Pinned primary references:** [current repository page](https://github.com/luckeyfaraday/athena-code), [pinned README](https://github.com/luckeyfaraday/athena-code/blob/63e5143d8122343fda0130e728ddd4215bd4edb1/README.md), [session scanner](https://github.com/luckeyfaraday/athena-code/blob/63e5143d8122343fda0130e728ddd4215bd4edb1/overlay/packages/opencode/src/session/memory/agentscan.ts), [session-index schema and writer](https://github.com/luckeyfaraday/athena-code/blob/63e5143d8122343fda0130e728ddd4215bd4edb1/overlay/packages/opencode/src/session/memory/sessionindex.ts), [native takeover planner](https://github.com/luckeyfaraday/athena-code/blob/63e5143d8122343fda0130e728ddd4215bd4edb1/overlay/packages/opencode/src/session/agent/session-takeover.ts), [agent takeover tool](https://github.com/luckeyfaraday/athena-code/blob/63e5143d8122343fda0130e728ddd4215bd4edb1/overlay/packages/opencode/src/tool/agent-local.ts), [session-scan fixtures](https://github.com/luckeyfaraday/athena-code/blob/63e5143d8122343fda0130e728ddd4215bd4edb1/test/agentscan.test.ts), [release history](https://github.com/luckeyfaraday/athena-code/releases).

## Deep review: xtermium/xtermium (desktop terminal/session manager; 2026-11-20)

- **Current pin and release:** Apache-2.0, Rust/Tauri/Svelte, current main checkout and live main ref both  f7c55e017128615676573716ab66bac5b56f9902 (2026-11-18). The live GitHub page showed 16 stars / 2 forks. Latest visible release was v0.10.0 (2026-11-14), with a nightly build listed for 2026-11-15; main is newer than that release. The app ships Windows/Linux/macOS installers and describes itself as a terminal with a session manager. The hosted Pro sync server is proprietary, not part of this repository.
- **Important terminology split:** Its workspace/folder/session tree is primarily saved *connection profiles* (SSH, RDP, VNC, telnet, serial, web/BMC, local shells), layouts, logins, tunnels and notes. The same workspace Store has an agent_prompt string. It is not a ChatGPT Project/Section tree. The separate AI session-history panel lists local transcripts belonging to Claude Code, Codex or omp for an agent tab's working folder.
- **AI history capability is inspect/resume, not transfer:** Conversation rows contain only source id, title, created/last-used times, byte count and file path. Clicking resumes the original CLI session in a new terminal tab; renaming changes xtermium's own agent-titles metadata; deleting removes the original tool transcript after checking canonical path containment. The UI explicitly says the source tool file is deleted and cannot be undone. There is no parsed canonical message graph, destination session writer, cross-harness conversation import/export, ChatGPT source, Claude Cloud/API-Key target, or loss report.
- **Provider parsing is metadata-only and local:** Claude Code reads the per-workspace projects folder, titles from Claude title records or the first human prompt, and ignores agent-*.jsonl subagent logs. Codex walks rollout files, uses session_meta and session_index.jsonl for id/cwd/title, and filters thread_source=subagent. omp walks a bounded directory depth and reads its first-line cwd/id and session_info name, but the source comment says the format was not tested against omp. These readers use bounded head/tail windows to obtain listing metadata; they do not convert all message bodies, attachments, tools, token usage, or parent/fork edges. The only history unit tests cover Claude and Codex, not omp.
- **Import/export semantics:** The app's import/export module handles its own encrypted Xtermium backup, MobaXterm connection bookmarks and OpenSSH config; its source comments say passwords do not cross in those text exports. This is network/terminal connection-profile migration, not AI conversation transfer. Workspace clone/sync operates on Xtermium Store records (sessions, folders, prompts and connection-related settings). The local AI transcript files are read separately by agent_history.rs and are not part of the Store model; the proprietary hosted sync implementation is outside this repository, so its behavior for those external files is not independently verifiable.
- **MCP/agent safety boundary:** The MCP automation service exposes Xtermium's connection/session and terminal controls (open tabs, run commands, read output, type/press keys); the agent-history list/rename/delete are Tauri UI commands, not a transcript-import MCP API. Credentials are held in Xtermium's encrypted local store rather than returned to agents, but MCP can operate the authenticated remote shell/desktop. Remote automation is an opt-in network mode with a bearer token and self-signed HTTPS; generated Node client commands may disable TLS certificate validation for that run. Treat it as a privileged remote-control surface, not a harmless session converter.
- **Validation:** Targeted command rtk proxy env CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/nm-xtermium-cargo-home-20261120 CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/private/tmp/nm-xtermium-cargo-target-20261120 XTERMIUM_HOME=/private/tmp/nm-xtermium-test-home-20261120 cargo test --bin xtermium agent_history initially exited 101 because Tauri build expected the generated binaries/xtermium-rdp-aarch64-apple-darwin sidecar. Corrective command rtk proxy env CARGO_HOME=... CARGO_TARGET_DIR=... XTERMIUM_HOME=... node scripts/rdp.mjs exited 0, building the sidecar with one dead-code warning; temporary build/cache/sidecar outputs were removed afterwards. Re-running the targeted Rust tests exited 0: 2 passed (Claude and Codex), 0 failed, 154 filtered out; compile emitted three unrelated warnings. This was fixture-only; no real transcript, app install, network account or agent CLI was used. Full app suite was not run.
- **Disposition:** Useful cross-platform desktop-session-manager UX and local-agent-launch bridge; not a migration adapter. Borrow only the concept of a separate local agent-tab history browser, source-native resume and explicit workspaces; never equate its saved connection Sessions/workspaces or Pro sync with ChatGPT Projects/Sections or AI chat migration. A Nexus desktop shell must keep agent transcript imports separate from connection credentials, ensure deletion is never confused with export, and preserve raw event trees rather than just titles/paths.
- **Pinned references:** [current repository page](https://github.com/xtermium/xtermium), [pinned README](https://github.com/xtermium/xtermium/blob/f7c55e017128615676573716ab66bac5b56f9902/README.md), [agent history model/parsers](https://github.com/xtermium/xtermium/blob/f7c55e017128615676573716ab66bac5b56f9902/src/agent_history.rs), [MCP automation tools](https://github.com/xtermium/xtermium/blob/f7c55e017128615676573716ab66bac5b56f9902/src/automation.rs), [saved connection/workspace model](https://github.com/xtermium/xtermium/blob/f7c55e017128615676573716ab66bac5b56f9902/src/config.rs), [connection-profile import/export](https://github.com/xtermium/xtermium/blob/f7c55e017128615676573716ab66bac5b56f9902/src/formats/mod.rs), [release history](https://github.com/xtermium/xtermium/releases).


## Deep review: leox255/termem (local cross-agent memory index; not a migrator; 2026-11-20)

- Pinned checkout: main HEAD a7901bc3a3fed1bcaac4860002e9b31531a5e594, release v0.6.3. The live GitHub page showed 12 stars / 2 forks. Cargo.toml and README self-declare MIT, but the pinned checkout has no root LICENSE file and the GitHub repository page does not expose a license badge; verify licensing before redistributing or reusing source. No source was copied into Nexus.
- Scope: a local Rust CLI/TUI, SQLite index and MCP memory/search sidecar for Claude Code, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, OpenCode, and shell logs. It scans each tool's machine-local files/database and groups sessions by cwd/subtree. Normalized session metadata includes source/id/path/cwd/title/prompts/model/git branch/times/message count and a permission-bypass flag. termem resume reconstructs a source-native CLI command; it does not import or write a destination conversation.
- Loss boundary: MCP transcript pages expose only role, text, and an optional timestamp. Provider readers select text blocks; Claude thinking/tool_use blocks and non-text payloads are skipped, Codex event types other than user/agent text are skipped, OpenCode reads only type=text parts, and shell records are commands. The model has no attachment/asset, structured tool-call/result, token/cost, branch/fork/subagent, or user-edit/turn-control representation. OpenCode's session listing selects only rows with parent_id NULL; it does not emit a parent-child session graph. No ChatGPT Project/Section parser, Claude Cloud/API-Key destination writer, or session conversion/import-export path was found.
- Memory plane: local search index defaults to ~/.termem/index.db. FTS stores the first 400 transcript messages per session, capped at 200,000 characters, so this is a searchable local copy of sensitive text, not metadata-only indexing; no deterministic redaction was found in the reviewed content-body path. MCP offers search/recall/get_session and agent-authored summary/board tools. README claims no network calls; no real host, real history, account or credential was used or tested here.
- Reusable patterns: explicit local source roots; incremental stat-based indexing; text search; paginated MCP reads; per-harness instruction/Skill wrappers; source-native resume; separation between indexed transcript data and agent-authored summaries. Treat it as a local memory/search plane and UX reference only, not a canonical archive or migration dependency. Nexus must keep raw immutable evidence, normalized event graph, lossy working-memory summaries, and target-native writer as separate layers.
- Verification: CodeGraph indexed 40 source files (554 nodes / 1,387 edges). Isolated cargo test --locked passed 56 tests (46 library, 1 binary, 6 integration, 3 MCP); 0 failed, 0 ignored. The first attempt with a disposable HOME but no explicit RUSTUP_HOME exited 1 because rustup could not find a default toolchain; rerunning with the installed RUSTUP_HOME and separate temporary Cargo cache/target/HOME passed. Test fixtures used temp roots; no real local agent sessions were indexed.
- Pinned references: [repository](https://github.com/leox255/termem), [pinned README](https://github.com/leox255/termem/blob/a7901bc3a3fed1bcaac4860002e9b31531a5e594/README.md), [session model](https://github.com/leox255/termem/blob/a7901bc3a3fed1bcaac4860002e9b31531a5e594/src/model.rs), [transcript readers](https://github.com/leox255/termem/blob/a7901bc3a3fed1bcaac4860002e9b31531a5e594/src/transcript.rs), [MCP surface](https://github.com/leox255/termem/blob/a7901bc3a3fed1bcaac4860002e9b31531a5e594/src/mcp.rs), [release history](https://github.com/leox255/termem/releases).


## Deep review: zavudev/leon (local agent orchestrator and history importer; not a cross-harness migrator; 2026-11-20)

- Pinned clean checkout: main HEAD 1fe87724d62a5d29f40982daecd13392a977872b, Apache-2.0. Current GitHub page showed 10 stars / 1 fork; the latest release page showed Leon 0.16.2, released 2026-11-05. The README describes a keyboard-first desktop orchestrator for Claude Code, Codex and OpenCode, with local and SSH-connected machines, projects, worktrees and searchable history. It states Windows/Linux builds have not yet been tried by a human; do not treat cross-platform packaging as independent verification.
- The history importer is narrower than Leon's interactive agent interface: it imports local Claude Code JSONL, Codex rollout JSONL and OpenCode SQLite history, with optional additional Claude/Codex account roots. It writes into Leon's own local SQLite store; stable import keys/fingerprints and cursors skip unchanged source items, and OpenCode is opened read-only. The diagnostic command reports counts, paths, times, formats and skip/error reasons without titles, messages or account data.
- Own hierarchy: Leon's desktop tree organizes machines > projects > worktrees > sessions, plus direct-project, folder and pinned scopes. The history model associates a session with a known project/worktree from its cwd. This is useful workspace UX, but it is not a ChatGPT Project/Section Group, does not import sidebar membership, and does not encode general parent/fork/subtask/subagent session edges.
- What is retained: provider/session/external IDs, machine, account, cwd, project association, title, model, message timestamps and sequence, role/text, and token counts aggregated by model/day. Codex forked rollouts retain their own external id but the parent relationship is not represented in the normalized session record. The model has no cost fields or attachment/object references.
- Concrete losses found in source and fixture tests: Claude image blocks are ignored; tool_use is converted to a text-like tool line and tool_result is dropped; sidechain/meta records are skipped; Claude subagent transcripts are not kept as sessions or text, although their token usage is attributed to the parent. Codex function/custom/web-search calls become tool lines and outputs/results are dropped; unknown event types are ignored. OpenCode tool parts become tool lines; reasoning, file/patch and other non-text parts are ignored, and child sessions with parent_id are not listed as top-level imported sessions. Messages exceeding the importer cap are clipped and malformed records are counted/skipped.
- Target boundary: Leon opens a stored transcript or resumes a history session in its original local CLI; its remote-machine feature connects to user-controlled SSH/paired machines. The reviewed history subsystem has no ChatGPT web export parser, Projects/Sections migration, Claude Cloud/API-Key conversation writer, or target-native cross-provider conversion. The experimental common agent interface is a runtime adapter, not an importer or history writer for SaaS Cloud sessions.
- Reusable high-value patterns: provider-specific HistorySource plug-ins; account-aware paths; stable source fingerprints and idempotent cursors; read-only database access; WAL-aware fingerprints; per-source diagnostics; persistent local searchable store; and explicit machine/project/worktree grouping. Use as a native local-history UX/importer reference, not as the canonical cross-harness archive or a migration dependency.
- Verification: CodeGraph indexed 612 files (25,143 nodes / 120,582 edges, 99.87 MB); index up to date. Isolated command rtk proxy env CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/nm-leon-cargo-home-20261120 CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/private/tmp/nm-leon-target-20261120 RUSTUP_HOME=/Users/vincentadamnemessis/.rustup HOME=/private/tmp/nm-leon-home-20261120 cargo test --locked -p leon-history exited 0: 217 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored; doc tests 0. This focused parser/importer suite uses fixtures/temp stores; no real HOME history, agents, API keys or accounts were used. The full desktop application test suite was not run.
- Pinned references: [live repository](https://github.com/zavudev/leon), [pinned README](https://github.com/zavudev/leon/blob/1fe87724d62a5d29f40982daecd13392a977872b/README.md), [history roots](https://github.com/zavudev/leon/blob/1fe87724d62a5d29f40982daecd13392a977872b/crates/leon-history/src/roots.rs), [provider readers](https://github.com/zavudev/leon/blob/1fe87724d62a5d29f40982daecd13392a977872b/crates/leon-history/src/source.rs), [Claude parser](https://github.com/zavudev/leon/blob/1fe87724d62a5d29f40982daecd13392a977872b/crates/leon-history/src/claude.rs), [Codex parser](https://github.com/zavudev/leon/blob/1fe87724d62a5d29f40982daecd13392a977872b/crates/leon-history/src/codex.rs), [OpenCode reader](https://github.com/zavudev/leon/blob/1fe87724d62a5d29f40982daecd13392a977872b/crates/leon-history/src/opencode.rs), [normalized session model](https://github.com/zavudev/leon/blob/1fe87724d62a5d29f40982daecd13392a977872b/crates/leon-core/src/model.rs), [release history](https://github.com/zavudev/leon/releases).


## Deep review: itaywol/adeptability (skills/agent portability, not session migration; 2026-11-20)

- Pinned main HEAD c5b1b81b2e0ae71bd8afeeade28eaea6f8fd1bf5; rtk git ls-remote for refs/heads/main returned the same SHA, and the checkout stayed clean. MIT license. GitHub showed 9 stars / 0 forks; the latest visible release was v1.10.0 (2026-07-24). The README advertises 50+ harnesses; a built CLI enumerated 56 registry entries: three renderer kinds, with five specialized renderers (Claude Code, Cursor, OpenCode, Codex, Copilot) and generic per-skill adapters for the rest.
- Scope is genuinely valuable for the Skills/agent-definition layer: canonical skill files can include SKILL.md plus scripts/references/assets sidecars; canonical subagents can render to multiple harness-native agent formats; libraries resolve shared skills from Git refs; sync/diff uses content hashes and drift states. The canonical Skill model has ID, description, activation, globs, allowed tools, targets, tags, metadata, model, per-harness overrides, body and sidecars. However, per-harness overrides are preserved in canonical YAML but explicitly are not reconstructed on import. Model hints are emitted only by Claude Code; other renderers ignore them.
- Losses are harness-specific, not “perfect” transfer: Codex aggregates skills into root AGENTS.md with a 32 KiB budget and drops sidecars; Cursor is a single .mdc file and drops sidecars; Copilot buckets per glob under 64 KiB, skips agent/manual-activation skills and drops sidecars (no in-file truncation manifest); OpenCode emits plain Markdown and ignores activation; Claude encodes glob activation in a description hint rather than a native glob field. Generic adapters emit their declared per-skill layouts but cannot create target semantics the schema does not model. Reverse import reconstructs only fields the harness file carries, so a full metadata round-trip is not guaranteed.
- Coverage boundary: this is a skill and subagent package manager. Its project scope is a local repo/global skills directory; it has no ChatGPT Projects/Sections, session/message graph, history importer, Cloud/API-Key conversation writer, MCP server configuration migration, or plugin manager. The CLI exposes agent/harness/skill/library/loop/exchange commands, not an MCP-session export/import path. The loop command scaffolds a discovery skill and evaluator agent plus an optional GitHub Actions cron YAML skeleton; schedule execution/auth remains with the chosen harness or CI, and it does not migrate an existing ChatGPT/Cloud scheduled task.
- Safety: the static scanner categorizes prompt injection, malicious code, excessive permissions, secret exposure, supply chain, URL and frontmatter findings. An optional LLM review failure falls back to static findings; this is a content review aid, not a sandbox. Separate synthetic filesystem probe found an out-of-root write: a temp-only project with .claude symlinked to a sibling outside directory, mode=copy, and one synthetic skill returned sync --dry-run as one write, then sync exit 0 and created outside/skills/synthetic-probe/SKILL.md. A second default symlink-mode probe also created the outside path and a relative SKILL.md symlink. Source AtomicWrite uses MkdirAll/Rename without canonical-root containment, while the CLI trusts the project tree's existing harness path. Treat syncing untrusted worktrees with directory symlinks as a safety risk until realpath/root containment or an explicit opt-in is added. No user files were accessed.
- Verification: Go was absent initially (go test exit 127, env: go: No such file or directory). Downloaded only to a disposable temp directory the official Go 1.25.14 darwin/arm64 archive and verified its SHA-256 against go.dev: 5b26c0b6f308240fca2614fb02f622cfcc8c0cc3b69c78bba4845489a4590259. Isolated command rtk proxy env PATH=<temp Go bin> HOME=<temp HOME> GOCACHE=<temp> GOMODCACHE=<temp> GOPROXY=https://proxy.golang.org,direct GOSUMDB=sum.golang.org go test -count=1 ./... exited 0; all listed packages passed, pkg/adeptschema had no test files. Temporary toolchain, caches, CLI test homes and fixtures were removed after evidence capture.
- The full-source review found this as a strong specialized candidate for Nexus Skills/agents: it may reduce the adapter burden for that resource type. Do not use it as the central Nexus Memory migration engine. Keep session/event, project/section, MCP/plugin, permission and schedule adapters as independent resource types with explicit per-harness capability and loss records.
- Pinned references: [repository](https://github.com/itaywol/adeptability), [pinned README](https://github.com/itaywol/adeptability/blob/c5b1b81b2e0ae71bd8afeeade28eaea6f8fd1bf5/README.md), [canonical schema](https://github.com/itaywol/adeptability/blob/c5b1b81b2e0ae71bd8afeeade28eaea6f8fd1bf5/pkg/adept/canonical.go), [Codex renderer](https://github.com/itaywol/adeptability/blob/c5b1b81b2e0ae71bd8afeeade28eaea6f8fd1bf5/internal/render/codex/renderer.go), [Copilot renderer](https://github.com/itaywol/adeptability/blob/c5b1b81b2e0ae71bd8afeeade28eaea6f8fd1bf5/internal/render/copilot/renderer.go), [sync command](https://github.com/itaywol/adeptability/blob/c5b1b81b2e0ae71bd8afeeade28eaea6f8fd1bf5/internal/cli/commands_sync.go), [loop scaffold](https://github.com/itaywol/adeptability/blob/c5b1b81b2e0ae71bd8afeeade28eaea6f8fd1bf5/internal/cli/commands_loop.go), [release history](https://github.com/itaywol/adeptability/releases).


## Deep review: AblazeGHR/pan (local session orchestrator/importer; not a Cloud migrator; 2026-11-20)

- Pinned main HEAD 74f475250b54959aaab13ffa83be820cada68a32; remote refs/heads/main matched and checkout remained clean. Root license is AGPL-3.0. Current GitHub page showed 8 stars / 1 fork. README describes a local web app that manages CLI-agent Sessions, Workspaces, worker/manager tasks, Jobs, per-session MCP configuration and optional QQ/Cloudflare integrations. The API defaults to unauthenticated loopback; changing PAN_HOST can expose the same API without authentication. Cloudflare Remote is opt-in. Do not expose the default service to untrusted networks, and treat AGPL as a legal dependency gate for a proprietary Nexus product.
- Import sources: the UI import modal advertises CBC, Kimi, OpenCode, Codex CLI and DSH; the backend registry additionally registers a Claude sessions provider, although the current modal does not list it. This is local native-history import into Pan's own Session store, not ChatGPT web/Cloud history export. No ChatGPT Project/Section importer or Cloud/API-Key session writer was found.
- Session/workspace structure: a Pan Session stores adapter, CLI session id, model/permission config, workdir, history, usage, workspace_ids, managed/managed_by, reports and task queues. Root Sessions have zero or one Pan Workspace membership; managed descendants inherit membership. Workspace metadata names a group and optional shared directories, but does not move/copy disk folders. Pan can represent its own manager/worker session tree, yet the import route creates a Pan Session from provider history and does not reconstruct the source's general parent/fork/subagent relationship graph. DSH listing itself emits parent_id as empty.
- Fidelity: provider parsers normalize native logs into Pan's history-block shape, generally role/content/nativeItemId; import captures raw/total usage separately. DSH reads the versioned compressed event log and filters out system snapshots, runtime context/skill reminders and uncommitted assistant attempts; it keeps committed user/assistant text, reasoning and tool-call summaries, but tool/result events are not duplicated as full structured results, and message timestamps/parent edges are not represented in parsed blocks. CBC/Kimi also filter to text/reasoning/tool summaries; OpenCode handles text/reasoning/tool input/output; Codex has the richest event mapping and lineage reader but its import still materializes a Pan projection rather than a round-trip raw log. Binary images/attachments and source-specific config/auth/MCP settings are not reconstructed by the importers.
- Reimport is notably careful: native snapshots detect concurrent source change; Pan-origin queue messages are matched by identity, stale/ambiguous reimports require a short-lived HMAC confirmation, and guarded failures retain existing history. Imported Sessions are created without starting a worker. The UI can assign a selected Pan Workspace and template/MCP settings, but this is an explicit Pan-side choice, not source hierarchy migration.
- Pan has a Jobs UI, scheduled Session messages and per-session MCP setup; its README does not show an importer for ChatGPT scheduled tasks, Projects/Sections, MCP server files or plugins. Memory/Character are explicitly described as paused, so it is not a ready-made Nexus memory plane.
- Focused validation: repository tests are isolated by tests/conftest.py to pytest temp directories. rtk proxy env PYTHONPATH=. pytest -q tests/test_session_import.py tests/test_dsh_sessions.py tests/test_reimport_confirmation.py tests/test_cbc_import_guard.py exited 0; 82 import/reimport/DSH guard tests passed (pytest emitted an Unknown config option: timeout warning because pytest-timeout is not installed). A broader bundle that also included tests/test_codex_session_conversion.py exited 1 with 18 failures in that separate native-control conversion suite; each failing request returned 501 because platform.supports("terminal.native") is false on this Darwin/arm64 host. Direct platform probe confirmed False. This is a platform-gated live-agent terminal feature, not evidence that the import tests fail; no full repo suite or live agent/account integration was run.
- Safety boundary: source README and local route code show the unauthenticated-by-default local API and optional remote access. MCP/plugin/jobs capabilities increase privilege; configure only on trusted local networks, and do not conflate per-session Pan MCP with source MCP transfer. AGPL licensing also means keep it as a research/architecture reference unless the licensing model is intentionally accepted.
- Pinned references: [repository](https://github.com/AblazeGHR/pan), [pinned README](https://github.com/AblazeGHR/pan/blob/74f475250b54959aaab13ffa83be820cada68a32/README.md), [DSH session reader](https://github.com/AblazeGHR/pan/blob/74f475250b54959aaab13ffa83be820cada68a32/packages/core/adapters/dsh/sessions.py), [session/workspace model](https://github.com/AblazeGHR/pan/blob/74f475250b54959aaab13ffa83be820cada68a32/packages/core/session.py), [workspace metadata](https://github.com/AblazeGHR/pan/blob/74f475250b54959aaab13ffa83be820cada68a32/packages/core/workspace.py), [import pipeline](https://github.com/AblazeGHR/pan/blob/74f475250b54959aaab13ffa83be820cada68a32/packages/web/server.py), [Codex history reader](https://github.com/AblazeGHR/pan/blob/74f475250b54959aaab13ffa83be820cada68a32/packages/core/adapters/codex/sessions.py).

## Deep review: termide/termide (local terminal-agent harness and event log, not a migration tool; 2026-11-20)

- **Pinned source and provenance:** clean checkout of `main` at `5c3eec4915380da479c2bb03f6843342e7a9e17d`; `git ls-remote refs/heads/main` returned the same SHA. Root `LICENSE` is MIT. GitHub page at capture: 189 stars / 10 forks; `gh release list` and the official 0.50.0 tag page show v0.50.0 published 2026-11-16. Main is one commit beyond the release tag.
- **Product boundary:** Termide is a local/desktop/server terminal IDE and agent workbench. It has a built-in agent for OpenAI-/Anthropic-compatible endpoints and can run Claude Code, Codex and Gemini CLI as ACP external agents. The latter is runtime delegation/continuation, not a ChatGPT export importer or a Cloud conversation writer. A model API key lets an agent call a provider; it does not make a Claude.ai Project/Cloud session portable or create a native Claude Code Cloud session.
- **Project organization:** a project is anchored by a local root path and a path-derived name/hash. `project.toml` holds the root and `ProjectLayout` (panel groups, focused group, panel state); project trust/grants, logs and buffers are separate local store files. The visual `panel_groups` are columns inside one project, not a sidebar Section Group or parent hierarchy over projects. Agent logs live separately under `ai/sessions/<panel-directory>/` in Termide's config tree, so migration must keep project identity/layout and conversation content as separate resources.
- **Native session log:** append-only JSONL, one file per session. Header fields include `version`, `id`, `created`, `cwd`, optional `name`, optional `parent`, and optional `copied`; each later line has `id`, `parent_id`, timestamp and a typed event kind. The source has 24 event kinds, including typed messages, model/agent/connection/mode/toolset changes, compaction and failed compaction, fitting/rewind/pruning, run boundaries, external-session identity, retries/held reports, usage/spend, context fill, goal verdict and autorun state. Message records can include typed user text/images, assistant text/thinking/tool calls (including provider-specific opaque call content), tool results, timestamps, timing and token usage. This is useful evidence for Nexus's immutable-event/derived-transcript split, but it is Termide-specific storage, not a cross-harness interchange contract.
- **Relationships and branching:** entries form a `parent_id` chain; rewinds select an earlier leaf, and fork writes a copy of the current branch to a new log/id with a `copied` prefix count. That preserves the fork's shared event prefix but the copied header does not identify the source session ID as a fork-parent edge. Delegated-task logs have a separate `SessionParent { session?, agent, call }`; external ACP state is a separate `{agent, session_id, log, cwd}` reference. Do not conflate these event-parent, copied-fork and delegated-child edges.
- **External ACP continuity:** when the adapter offers `session/resume` or `session/load`, Termide reopens that native external session; otherwise it opens a fresh external session and replays/reconstructs a bounded conversation. This is not a parser for arbitrary ChatGPT/Codex files and does not recreate a source's full event/assets graph in Claude Cloud.
- **Export and concrete losses:** “Save chat as Markdown” is a user-readable export of current-branch user text and assistant text only. Source `chat_from_session` excludes reasoning, tool-call blocks, tool results and panel notices; it is not lossless and there is no reviewed structured import/export counterpart. Termide's internal JSONL keeps much richer events than its Markdown exporter, so tests of that export must not be represented as cross-harness restoration tests.
- **Portable resources / configuration:** Termide reads Agent Skills in the `SKILL.md` shape from its `ai` levels, `.agents/skills` in panel/project directories, and `~/.agents/skills`; this is a high-value Skills interoperability reference, subject to discovery precedence and Termide-specific invocation semantics. It reads `mcp.toml` and a subset of Claude/Cursor/VS Code `.mcp.json` keys; unsupported transport/input references are skipped and logged. OAuth material is local under `ai/mcp-auth.json`; API keys are supplied through environment-backed settings and secrets/vault flow, not something to copy into a migration bundle. Termide's local process-plugin system is not a ChatGPT plugin importer.
- **Scheduled/recurring work:** `/loop` repeats a prompt at an interval (optionally judged until a condition) in the agent panel, and `/goal` loops toward a judged outcome; the active session log records autorun state. This is useful to map into a target automation capability, but it is a session-bound agent runner, not evidence of a standalone portable schedule definition with universal cron/calendar/time-zone semantics.
- **Focused tests, isolated:** `cargo test --locked -p termide-agent-core --lib session::tests` — 41 passed; `cargo test --locked -p termide-project` — 41 passed; `cargo test --locked -p termide-panel-agent export::tests` — 4 passed. Temporary HOME/CARGO_HOME/CARGO_TARGET_DIR were used; no real conversation store, credential, API key, provider request or full workspace suite was used. These tests establish Termide's own log/project/export behavior only.
- **Disposition:** reviewed as a valuable local event-log / project-layout / ACP-runtime / Skills-and-MCP-adapter reference, not a ChatGPT Projects/Sections or cross-harness session migrator, not a native ChatGPT/Codex import path, and not a Claude Cloud/API-Key session writer. It is MIT-licensed, but no dependency is justified when the relevant formats can be independently specified and adapter behavior must remain source-versioned.
- **Pinned references:** [https://github.com/termide/termide](https://github.com/termide/termide); [release 0.50.0](https://github.com/termide/termide/releases/tag/0.50.0); [session schema](https://github.com/termide/termide/blob/5c3eec4915380da479c2bb03f6843342e7a9e17d/crates/agent-core/src/session.rs); [message schema](https://github.com/termide/termide/blob/5c3eec4915380da479c2bb03f6843342e7a9e17d/crates/agent-core/src/message.rs); [project layout](https://github.com/termide/termide/blob/5c3eec4915380da479c2bb03f6843342e7a9e17d/crates/project/src/lib.rs); [project store](https://github.com/termide/termide/blob/5c3eec4915380da479c2bb03f6843342e7a9e17d/crates/project/src/store.rs); [Agent docs](https://github.com/termide/termide/blob/5c3eec4915380da479c2bb03f6843342e7a9e17d/doc/en/agent.md); [architecture docs](https://github.com/termide/termide/blob/5c3eec4915380da479c2bb03f6843342e7a9e17d/doc/en/architecture.md); [Markdown exporter](https://github.com/termide/termide/blob/5c3eec4915380da479c2bb03f6843342e7a9e17d/crates/panel-agent/src/export.rs).

## Deep review: bobmatnyc/claude-mpm (Claude Code coordinator with several distinct session stores; not a cross-harness migrator; 2026-11-20)

- **Pinned provenance / license gate:** shallow clean checkout at main `25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b`, matching `refs/heads/main`; latest commit date is 2026-08-31. `gh repo view` reports 158 stars / 33 forks, updated 2026-11-19; the live HTML page showed 159 / 33 during the same capture, so the dynamic star count varied by one. Latest GitHub release is v6.5.83 (2026-07-27), with four commits to main since the release. Root `LICENSE` and Python `pyproject.toml` say Elastic License 2.0; GitHub reports “Other”, but `package.json` labels the NPM wrapper MIT. The repository README still says “Current stable version v6.5.43,” while package/`VERSION` and latest release are v6.5.83. Treat license and version metadata as inconsistent; registry keeps `license_spdx=NOASSERTION`. Elastic-2.0 restricts providing the software as a hosted/managed service to third parties, so no dependency/code reuse is justified for a hosted Nexus Memory product without legal clearance.
- **Product boundary:** the README explicitly requires Claude Code CLI, not Claude Desktop. It is a Claude-centric PM/orchestration, plugin, agent/Skills/MCP and session-management framework. The SDK runtime is described as experimental. README “Cloud Sled” (Codespaces/Gitpod/Daytona/remote containers) is stated as a future Phase 6 vision, not evidence of a shipped Claude.ai Cloud session adapter. No ChatGPT source parser, Project/Section importer, Cloud conversation writer, or cross-harness target session writer was found in the reviewed paths.
- **Do not collapse its multiple “session” representations:**
  1. `core.SessionManager` and `services.cli.SessionManager` keep UUID/context/timestamps/use counts/agent-use/task snippets in `~/.claude-mpm/sessions/active_sessions.json`; the active metadata is archived as gzip JSON after age limits. An `agents_run` task is truncated to 100 chars. These are ID/usage metadata, not transcripts.
  2. `claude-mpm run --resume` delegates to Claude Code's native `--resume`; the CLI runtime can also call `--fork-session`. This resumes/forks a pre-existing Claude Code session by its native ID, not by importing a transcript.
  3. `session pause --export` writes a project-local `.claude-mpm/sessions/<id>.json` plus Markdown checkpoint and `LATEST-SESSION.txt`. The default snapshot records project path, git context, task-list state and an optional human-supplied pause message; the default conversation summary is a placeholder and token usage is hard-coded to 0 unless an enrichment path supplies it. This is a resumable handoff/checkpoint, not the complete conversation.
  4. `ResumeLogGenerator` writes structured Markdown and JSON summaries (mission summary, accomplishments, findings, decisions, next steps, critical context, usage metrics) from a supplied state or a minimal fallback. It does not store the full event stream.
  5. The MCP Session Server tracks child `claude-mpm run --headless` processes and session IDs/status/output in an in-memory dictionary. It exposes start/continue/status/list/stop. `SessionInfo` is status/cwd/timestamps/count/last-output metadata; restarting the server loses this registry, even though the underlying Claude CLI session may still be resumable by ID.
  6. Optional `EventAggregator` connects to a local Socket.IO monitor (`localhost:8765`), consumes `claude_event` and a bounded history feed, and stores an MPM-specific `AgentSession` JSON containing initial/final text, typed captured events, delegations, metrics and project metadata. `claude-mpm aggregate export` can emit that captured projection as JSON, summary JSON, or events JSON. It is not a copy of Claude Code's raw JSONL and depends on monitor capture being active. The aggregate CLI test in this review exercises dispatch/mocks; the code path was source-reviewed, not run against a live session.
  7. A separate `session_analysis.parse_session(session_id, cwd)` reads Claude Code's native main JSONL and subagent JSONLs for a report. It redacts secrets by default and derives token/cost/tool/delegation timeline entries. It is a useful Claude-source adapter reference, not a target writer or an import command.
- **Transcript-reader fidelity:** `_extract_text_from_content` keeps plain text and nested tool-result text, not image blocks. The parser omits `isSidechain` entries; user/image non-text is skipped; assistant text and `tool_use` are projected into report events; tool results are paired by `tool_use_id` but stored in a 500-character response excerpt. It does not preserve the entire raw event stream, file-history snapshots, every provider-specific control field, or binary attachment bytes. Subagent JSONL is correlated to parent `Agent` calls by timestamp proximity, with explicit ambiguity handling; this is not a durable source fork-point/parent-session ID graph. Default redaction is a positive pattern, but it does not substitute for retaining encrypted raw evidence plus a separate sanitized projection.
- **Pause and event-capture boundary:** pause snapshots are project-local checkpoints rather than account/Section/Project hierarchies. `EventAggregator` caps in-memory active sessions at 50; a temp-only synthetic probe seeded 50 unfinished sessions and delivered a 51st. It evicted the oldest from `active_sessions` without writing it first (`ACTIVE_CAP=50`, oldest inactive, persisted files before/after=0), exit 0. Treat this capture path as best-effort telemetry, not an authoritative archive.
- **Resources, schedules and credentials:** the plugin exposes Claude Code-specific hooks, slash commands, 56 Skills and MCP config. `claude-mpm migrate` performs version/config migrations such as binary-name rewrites in `.mcp.json`; it is not a cross-harness session conversion. OAuth/API tokens are described as encrypted/keychain-backed and must never be exported with resources. The only scheduled `cron` work found in the reviewed services is Huey housekeeping for notification/queue cleanup (every 5 minutes / 6 hours); it is not a user-created portable automation model. Memory integrations such as `trusty-memory` are separate external services, not the raw session archive.
- **Security:** HTTP MCP Session Server defaults to `127.0.0.1`, exposes root/health/SSE/messages routes, and has an optional ngrok tunnel. The reviewed route setup contains no authentication layer; remote tunnelling should be treated as high risk unless wrapped with explicit auth/network controls. No server was started in this review.
- **Focused, isolated test evidence:** two `uv run --frozen --python 3.13 --group dev pytest -q` bundles ran under a disposable HOME/XDG/UV environment: (1) session manager/server/subprocess, CLI session manager/integration, CLI runtime, resume-log system/QA: 201 passed, 1 skipped (missing optional configuration fixture), 1 warning; (2) aggregate CLI, transcript session analysis, pause/MCP cleanup, pause/worktree pruning: 206 passed, 0 skipped, 2 warnings. Total: 407 passed, 1 skipped, 3 warnings. No full repo suite, real Claude CLI subprocess, user transcript, provider request, API key or exposed HTTP server was used.
- **Disposition:** high-value, Claude-specific references for reading Claude Code JSONL, secret-redacted report projections, checkpoint/resume UX, MCP lifecycle control and explicit separation of metadata versus transcript. Not a ChatGPT/Codex Projects/Sections importer or general Cloud/API-Key transcript writer. License conflict restricts reuse; borrow only independently designed concepts after legal review and preserve a loss ledger.
- **Pinned references:** [https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm); [release v6.5.83](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/releases/tag/v6.5.83); [license](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/LICENSE); [README](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/README.md); [core session metadata](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/core/session_manager.py); [project-local pause export](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/services/cli/session_pause_manager.py); [resume-log generator](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/services/infrastructure/resume_log_generator.py); [MCP session server](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/mcp/session_manager.py); [HTTP MCP transport](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/mcp/session_server_http.py); [event capture/export](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/services/event_aggregator.py); [captured-session model](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/models/agent_session.py); [aggregate export CLI](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/cli/commands/aggregate.py); [Claude transcript reader](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/services/session_analysis/transcript_parser.py); [session-analysis report writer](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/services/session_analysis/markdown_writer.py); [channel-session registry](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/services/channels/session_registry.py); [internal periodic jobs](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/blob/25203d34f20982c5871574b5059949ab85ab5a6b/src/claude_mpm/services/communication/message_bus.py).

## Deep review: rolecraft-sh/rolecraft — Skills/MCP resource manager, not a session migrator (2026-11-20)

Pinned source: [`rolecraft-sh/rolecraft` @ `edfd87e`](https://github.com/rolecraft-sh/rolecraft/tree/edfd87eed27288fa9e29521eb90e946130075842), package v2.6.2, MIT, Node >=22, zero runtime dependencies. The manifest is the single support registry: 90 agents total, 65 `verified`, 4 `community`, 5 `legacy`, 16 `experimental`; 9 declare MCP support. Instruction formats include `SKILL.md`, `.mdc`, `AGENTS.md`, and custom; MCP formats include standard `mcpServers`, Continue `experimental.mcpServers`, and Copilot `servers`. These figures were recomputed from `src/agents/manifest.js`, not copied from search snippets.

**What it actually moves.** RoleCraft installs a skill's files by copy or symlink into manifest-defined agent directories; project-scoped skills go to `.agents/skills`. Its lock records source, source type, `contentSha`, file hashes, install time and targets. Skill frontmatter can declare MCP servers: `install` resolves and scans these declarations and writes servers only to supported agent targets; `setup` installs skills across detected agents but its API returns the declared MCP records without installing or scanning them. This is a useful resource/configuration portability plane, not a whole-project or conversation migrator. The inspected code has no canonical project/section tree or session graph for parent-child tasks, message/edit/interrupt events, attachments, token/cost traces, or scheduled-task state.

**MCP security behavior and documentation drift.** `resolveMcpSource` fetches and bounded-scans GitHub source; runtime command forms such as `uvx:`, `pipx:`, `go:`, `deno:`, `cargo:`, local paths, and `npm:` do not yield source files to this scanner. In the pinned implementation, absence of scannable files creates a high-severity `unscanned_source` issue; `requiresMcpApproval` is true, `install` requires explicit `--yes` (with a warning), and `ci` has no approval override and fails closed. The temp-only probes observed `uvx` score 90 and `npm` score 89, both `REVIEW`/approval-required; targeted tests pass cases “flags uvx as unscanned rather than safe” and “requires explicit approval for unscanned npm MCP servers.” The pinned README's Known Limitations still says these sources “report as SAFE”; that statement is stale relative to the implementation/tests at this commit and must not be repeated as current behavior. The general skill scanner is still static pattern matching, not semantic policy or a source-trust verdict; `--dry-run` skips scanning.

**Profile fidelity/security.** Profiles are agent-configuration snapshots (config, instructions, MCP servers, skill references), not conversation history. The validator accepts `projectOverrides`/`localOverrides`, but `writeProfile()` serializes only name/version/timestamps/agents/description, dropping those keys. `profile export` removes timestamps/version but does not redact nested environment values. A synthetic `apiProfileImport` → `profileExportCommand` round-trip preserved a unique fake API-token sentinel in `agents.*.mcpServers.*.env.API_KEY` and dropped both override objects; no real credential was used. Profile apply does scan MCP config and drops execution-authority keys, but that does not make exported secrets safe.

**Source drift.** `rolecraft ci` re-resolves each recorded source and compares the current `contentSha` with the lock value; it detects changed content, but does not pin moving refs or provide a frozen-source guarantee. CI rejects review/unscanned MCP findings rather than asking for approval.

**Nexus decision.** Keep RoleCraft as a candidate adapter/installer for the portable Skills/MCP resource plane and as a useful catalog of agent-specific paths/config formats. Do not delegate the canonical session/project graph, attachments, chronology, schedules, or provenance to its profiles/lockfiles. Import only after secret redaction, round-trip validation, scope mapping, explicit confirmation of MCP source policy, and target capability checks.

**Dev-tooling audit.** With explicit `--registry=https://registry.npmjs.org`, `npm audit --json` exited 1 with two dev-tree findings: one high transitive `vite` (`<=6.4.3`) and one low direct devDependency `vitepress` (`<=1.6.4`), both `fixAvailable=false`. The configured npm mirror first returned 404 `[NOT_IMPLEMENTED]`; that failed query yielded no audit result. These findings concern test/docs tooling, not zero-dependency runtime.

**Evidence snapshot.** Repository SHA `edfd87eed27288fa9e29521eb90e946130075842`; CodeGraph status current (173 files, 1,918 nodes, 5,646 edges). Targeted source tests: 195 passed, 0 failed across security/install/setup/CI/profile; full npm suite was previously observed at this same checkout with 1,535 passed plus 4 release-prep tests, 0 failures. Synthetic probe outputs and source paths are preserved in `docs/research/evidence/rolecraft-codegraph-review-2026-11-20.txt` and the workspace `VERIFICATION.txt`.
## Deep review: vanillagreencom/kendex — cross-harness customization manager with Pi-local session tools (2026-11-20)

Pinned main source: [kendex @ 260b649](https://github.com/vanillagreencom/kendex/tree/260b6492363b47d84ba1f893c5223120ad6a9f4a), MIT, version 1.22.2. The latest observed tagged release is v1.22.2 (2026-11-18); main was already at 260b649 on 2026-11-20. The repo page showed 92 stars / 37 forks when checked. The checkout and remote refs/heads/main agreed on the same SHA.

**What it manages.** Kendex is a Rust/Tauri desktop app plus CLI for agent, skill, hook, command, MCP, plugin, and Pi-extension packages across eight coding harnesses: Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Cursor, Pi, Gemini CLI, GitHub Copilot, Antigravity. Its capability table is explicit per harness, resource kind, scope, and operation; unsupported/read-only/managed distinctions are first-class rather than marketing shorthand. Users declare a personal configuration and per-local-project kendex.toml; it renders harness-specific files, previews diffs, records package provenance/installed paths and updates/removes managed copies. This is a substantial resource-plane adapter reference and a plausible setup manager, not a Cloud Project/Section or conversation importer.

**Session-adjacent features are Pi-specific packages, not cross-harness archive semantics.**
- @vanillagreen/pi-session-manager reads Pi's own saved session files; it searches, resumes, renames, deletes, and shows related sessions as a tree for the current project or all Pi projects. It does not import/export another harness's transcript.
- @vanillagreen/pi-session-bridge connects to a running Pi process on the same machine and exposes prompt/follow-up/event-stream commands. Its BridgeHistory is an event buffer, not a durable transcript: bounded to 100 events / 1,048,576 serialized characters / one hour; raw spill is only for events emitted while a subscriber is attached and spill is enabled; session shutdown removes the raw sidecar.
- @vanillagreen/pi-qol persists scheduled prompt events into Pi's current session branch and reconstructs timers from that branch. This is useful Pi-local schedule state, not a harness-neutral scheduler record.
- @vanillagreen/pi-claude-bridge uses the Claude Agent SDK and a logged-in Claude Code account. It can resume the same Claude Code conversation across Pi turns, but its saved marker is validated against the Pi session id, cwd, message fingerprint, and an existing Claude session. This is a narrow Pi↔Claude Code bridge, not a ChatGPT/Codex importer or a Claude API-Key workflow.

**Nexus assessment.** Borrow Kendex's capability-table approach, explicit per-harness scope/format support, source-revision/install provenance, diff preview, and conservative handling of read-only/unsupported items. Treat its project scope as a local filesystem root, not ChatGPT's Projects/Section Groups. Keep Nexus's own canonical graph for project/section/session relationships, event history, parent/fork/subtask edges, attachments, token/cost traces, and schedule identities. Pi-specific session features can inform an optional Pi adapter, but cannot stand in for full-source fidelity.

**Verification limit.** CodeGraph current at the pinned checkout (58,072 nodes / 224,756 edges). Source/docs were inspected for the main capability table and the four Pi session-adjacent packages. Package tests were not run: Bun is not installed (rtk proxy bun --version exit 1); the installed rustc is 1.95.0 while Cargo.toml requires 1.96. This is an environment limitation, not a test failure or pass. No user sessions, account data, or API credentials were accessed.
## Deep review: lasoons/AgentSkillsManager — VS Code Skills browser, not cross-harness migration (2026-11-20)

Pinned main checkout: commit f603f685e46d53bbf538f0c3014545255ee50e4c, package version 0.6.0, MIT, VS Code engine >=1.96.0. The remote main ref matches this commit; last commit date observed in Git is 2026-01-17, with 17 commits total and no GitHub releases listed at review time. GitHub showed 91 stars / 9 forks. It is a VS Code extension, not a standalone CLI/Desktop app.

**Actual scope.** It browses Git repositories containing SKILL.md files and the claude-plugins.dev cloud skills catalog (README claims about 58K entries), then installs one skill into the active IDE's workspace skills directory. It recognizes Antigravity, CodeBuddy, Cursor, Qoder, Trae, Windsurf and VS Code; the documented project directories include .github/skills, .cursor/skills, .trae/skills, .agent/skills, .qoder/skills and .windsurf/skills. It can also scan Claude/Codex personal directories. The core Skill type is name/description/path/repoUrl plus local path/status—no source commit/digest, project/section graph, conversation event stream, attachments, token usage, MCP/plugin mapping, or scheduler model. It is a skills-only viewer/installer, not a project or session importer.

**Transfer and integrity limitations from source.** Remote repo skills are cloned and scanned for SKILL.md/frontmatter; cloud skills are returned as sourceUrl and downloaded via a ZIP proxy, expanded with adm-zip, then copied into the selected skill directory. No app-level ZIP digest/size/timeout cap is visible in this path; the HTTPS helper buffers the full body and follows up to three redirects without an allowlist. If the destination already exists, the installer removes it before copying the extracted tree, so a later copy error can leave the previous skill absent. There is no test suite or content-security scanner in this checkout; local repo scanning validates SKILL.md frontmatter and extracts its name/description.

**Current supply-chain finding.** package-lock pins production dependency adm-zip 0.5.16. Official-registry npm audit reports this direct dependency vulnerable (high severity); the library's published advisories say crafted ZIPs can force a multi-gigabyte memory allocation and DoS extraction, with affected versions including the pinned 0.5.16. The install path directly constructs AdmZip from the cloud ZIP buffer and calls extractAllTo. Audit also reports four additional dev/transitive vulnerable package entries (total summary: 1 critical, 3 high, 1 moderate); this is a point-in-time audit, not an exploit test. No malicious ZIP or user content was executed. The package is not a safe import boundary for arbitrary cloud content without dependency remediation, bounded download/extraction, integrity checks, and atomic replacement.

**Nexus assessment.** At most, use it as a reference for showing skills found across workspace directories and selecting an IDE-specific skill target. Do not depend on it for preserving Skills provenance, projects/section hierarchy, Sessions, message edits/interrupts, artifacts, MCP/plugins, memory, or scheduled tasks. The cloud catalog is a skills search API, not the Claude API-Key or conversation cloud target requested here. Any skill ingest should go through Nexus's own quarantine, content hash, provenance and secret/data-loss policy instead.

**Verification.** CodeGraph current on the 12-file checkout (219 nodes, 604 edges). Source and package manifest reviewed at the cloned SHA. Package tests were not run: Bun is not installed (rtk proxy bun --version exit 1); the installed rustc is 1.95.0 while Cargo.toml requires 1.96. This is an environment limitation, not a test failure or pass. No user sessions, account data, or API credentials were accessed.

### Incremental candidate review: hnaymyh123-henry/skills-compat-manager (2026-11-20)

**Disposition: useful Skills compatibility/preflight reference only; not a Project/Section/Session migration tool and not ready for Nexus runtime use without fixes.** Reviewed the clean local checkout at `8be1357e06a350cf13eb670b4d7ff24e4fc54bae`; remote `HEAD` matched. The repository metadata reports MIT, Python >=3.11, package version 0.1.0/Alpha, `fastmcp>=2.0`, and no release tag was present in the checkout. GitHub page snapshot: 84 stars, 0 forks, 45 commits; these counts are time-sensitive, not quality evidence.

The implementation discovers `SKILL.md` directories and checks declarations in `COMPAT.yaml`; the declaration has four requirement dimensions (Python packages, system tools, external services/environment variables, and runtime/platform capabilities). The manager uses a central `~/.skills-manager/compat/<skill-name>.yaml` with legacy in-skill fallback, and provides eight MCP tools for list/get/search/scan/refresh/suggest/apply-fix. `_detect_env_vars` records only whether a variable is present, not its value. This is a useful resource-plane idea for a Nexus compatibility contract, but is not a conversation format. No parser/writer or entity model for ChatGPT/Codex Projects, Section Groups, Session/fork/subtask relations, turns/interruptions, attachments, tool traces/tokens, or schedule migration was found in the reviewed CLI/MCP implementation.

**Reproducible runtime defect.** `pyproject.toml` allows unbounded `fastmcp>=2.0`. On an isolated Python 3.12 environment the resolver produced `fastmcp=4.2.3`, `mcp=2.3.0`. The direct `from app.mcp_server import mcp` command exited 1 with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'mcp.server.fastmcp'`; the official MCP Python SDK v2 migration guide documents that `FastMCP` was renamed to `MCPServer` and moved to `mcp.server.mcpserver`. The full test suite exited 2 during collection on `tests/test_mcp_server.py` for the same import; the other 39 tests passed when that file was excluded (one `pytest-asyncio` fixture-scope deprecation warning). This is a pinned, observed environment result, not a claim that every compatible dependency set fails.

**Security and data-integrity concerns.** `apply_fix` requires `confirm=True`, but after that it invokes the supplied command through `subprocess.run(..., shell=True)` and the safety filter is string-prefix plus regex checks. An AST-only probe (no shell command executed) showed `_is_auto_executable` accepts `pip install requests && python -c 'print(1)'` and `pip install requests; touch /tmp/compat-probe` as safe. Existing tests reject selected patterns (`curl | sh`, `&& rm`) but do not reject arbitrary shell chaining. Do not treat its allowlist as a sandbox; replace with parsed argv/known package-manager operations and reject shell metacharacters before any automatic execution.

A synthetic, temporary-only discovery test created two independent skill roots both named `shared`; `find_skills` returned one path, because de-duplication and central COMPAT storage key on basename. Nexus adapters should namespace by source identity/path and preserve collisions rather than silently dropping one skill. No real API key, provider call, or remote skill payload was used. Full command/output evidence is in `docs/research/evidence/skills-compat-manager-review-2026-11-20.txt`.

Primary sources: [repository](https://github.com/hnaymyh123-henry/skills-compat-manager), [pinned MCP server source](https://github.com/hnaymyh123-henry/skills-compat-manager/blob/8be1357e06a350cf13eb670b4d7ff24e4fc54bae/app/mcp_server.py), [pinned project dependencies](https://github.com/hnaymyh123-henry/skills-compat-manager/blob/8be1357e06a350cf13eb670b4d7ff24e4fc54bae/pyproject.toml), [official MCP Python SDK v2 migration guide](https://py.sdk.modelcontextprotocol.io/v2/migration/#fastmcp-renamed-to-mcpserver).

### Incremental candidate review: itlackey/akm (Agent Knowledge Manager, 2026-11-20)

**Disposition: high-value local knowledge/session-ingestion reference; not a cross-harness transcript/project migration tool. Do not adopt as the transfer engine as-is.** Reviewed both the public default branch `main` at `ec8318eabc6f15b912b3fe4f86b17130c0e3746f` (package `0.10.261129`) and the newest active release line `release/0.11.0` at `fce58373e7cf373cd07f57ec854c31268697e4d9` (package `0.11.0-beta.0`; merge of `0.10.261130-beta.6`). The GitHub releases page showed stable `v0.10.261129` from Nov 16 and prerelease `v0.10.261130-beta.6` from Nov 20; the 0.11 branch is not the same thing as a shipped stable release. Repository is MPL-2.0; live page snapshot was 70 stars/4 forks.

**What is relevant:** AKM has a broad local capability/knowledge library (native skills, memories, knowledge, agents, tasks, workflows; read-only adapters for Claude Code/OpenCode folders, Agent Skills, OKF/wiki, and other sources), path-based project scopes, OS-scheduled task v4 (`cron`/`launchd`/`schtasks`), and native integrations for Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode and others. It is the closest candidate in this review stream to a local-first memory manager with real session readers. Its docs explicitly position it as complementary to MCP/native skills, not as an MCP server/config migration tool; platform plugins are in a separate `itlackey/akm-plugins` repository and need a separate review.

**Session readers are not a canonical session importer.** The registry exposes exactly three readable session-log providers: Claude Code JSONL, Codex rollout JSONL, and OpenCode 1/2 SQLite. The documented `akm proposal extract --sessions claude|codex|opencode` / `--auto` mines durable insights and queues model-generated proposals; it is not a source-transcript-to-Claude-Cloud writer. `SessionEvent` stores essentially `{harness, text, ts, sessionId, role, filePath}`; `SessionSummary` adds start/end, cwd/project hint, title. There is no ChatGPT history provider or representation for Project/SectionGroup containment, parent/fork/subtask edges, attachments, interruption/steering events, structured tool I/O, token/cost usage, or provider-specific continuation state.

Loss behavior from code: Claude subagent JSONL is folded into the parent event list and assigned the parent session identity; Codex subagent/guardian/internal rollouts are excluded; OpenCode 2 lists only top-level sessions and excludes subagent sessions. Readers flatten tool use/results into text events. Codex block extraction takes only `.text` fields (image/audio blocks carry no text); OpenCode v2 emits user/assistant text and tool input/result text; other structures are not represented. The session asset writer emits frontmatter (`session_id`, times, project label, `log_path`, access instructions, tags) plus only an LLM-generated `Summary` and `Key topics`; it does not copy the source transcript. A moved/offline raw log path leaves only the summary/pointer. Pre-filtering truncates long events and applies a recency-biased total-character budget; session extraction is explicitly marked **Evolving** in `STABILITY.md`.

**Projects / schedules / API keys:** AKM `projects` are path-match scopes and search/write defaults, not a nested project tree or Section Group graph. `--project` selects write defaults and a project string on resulting memory/session assets. Task v4 + native scheduler sync is useful for constructing local recurring jobs but there is no ChatGPT scheduler importer. Model `connections` are OpenAI-compatible request endpoints; an `apiKey` is an environment-variable reference used for AKM model work. That can drive extraction/proposals, but it is not a Claude Cloud session-history writer.

**Verified defect on macOS, including latest 0.11 beta branch.** The 0.11.0 checkout still compares `path.resolve(source.path)` to `path.resolve(stashDir)` in `resolveProposalQueueTarget`, while `resolveSourceEntries` stores `safeRealpath` paths. On this host, a temp root is `/var/folders/.../T/...` but `realpath` is `/private/var/folders/.../T/...`; the path comparison misses its configured owner. Direct temporary-only reproduction on `release/0.11.0` exited 1: source list had the real `/private/var/...` path, then `resolveProposalQueueTarget` threw `No bundle owns proposal queue /var/folders/...`. The branch's `tests/integration/extract-projects.test.ts` likewise exited 1, **0/3 passed**, all three blocked by this error. No real user data was opened. The same bug was reproduced on `main`. This is narrower than a general data-loss claim but blocks extraction for macOS temp/symlink path roots; canonicalize both operands or compare a stable path identity before treating this pathway as dependable.

**Test evidence, latest release branch `fce5837`:** isolated Bun 1.4.5 with frozen lockfile; four focused reader/pre-filter/summary/OpenCode SQLite files passed **81 tests / 0 failures / 212 assertions**. The three project-writing integration tests failed as above. A broader 6-file main-branch run had 108 pass/17 fail, failures concentrated in extraction queue resolution. This is not full-suite certification.

Use AKM for architecture comparison: source adapters, hash-based extraction tracking, dry-run, bounded/pre-filtered summaries, read-only SQLite snapshots, project scopes and scheduler activation. Preserve a separate canonical Nexus event/project graph and explicit loss report. Do not market AKM session assets as transcript migration.

Primary sources: [repository and license](https://github.com/itlackey/akm), [releases](https://github.com/itlackey/akm/releases), [pinned release-branch session writer](https://github.com/itlackey/akm/blob/fce58373e7cf373cd07f57ec854c31268697e4d9/src/commands/improve/session-asset.ts), [Codex reader](https://github.com/itlackey/akm/blob/fce58373e7cf373cd07f57ec854c31268697e4d9/src/integrations/harnesses/codex/session-log.ts), [path ownership gate](https://github.com/itlackey/akm/blob/fce58373e7cf373cd07f57ec854c31268697e4d9/src/commands/proposal/repository.ts), [AKM / MCP boundary](https://github.com/itlackey/akm/blob/fce58373e7cf373cd07f57ec854c31268697e4d9/docs/guides/use-with-any-agent.md).

### Incremental candidate review: beautyfree/skiller (desktop skills manager, 2026-11-20)

**Disposition: relevant Skills/UI adapter reference only; not a ChatGPT/Codex conversation, Project/Section or Session migration tool. Source-code reuse is license-restricted.** Clean checkout and remote `HEAD`: `019d1ab13ecbb194dc4b6052f22e1db249f9d932`; latest GitHub release `v0.3.8` was published 2026-10-23 (57 stars / 6 forks in the live-page snapshot). Its Electron desktop package supports macOS, Windows and Linux; the README's 49 native agent definitions were confirmed as 49 `apps/desktop/agents/*.toml` files.

Skiller manages skill libraries and installs/syncs skill files to agent-native locations. Its canonical library is `~/.agents/skills`; it reads the Skills CLI v3 global `.skill-lock.json` read-only, and uses a `dotagents`-owned library/sync repository with source/provenance, hash, conflict-preview and secret-finding concepts. The sync manifest is only `{ profile, agent_policy, skills }`; it has `bundled`, immutable repository-reference, and skills.sh source entries. This is useful for Nexus's **resource adapter** design (portable skill package, installation matrix, source hash, conflict plan, and file/path safety), but it does not transfer MCP server configurations, Plugins, model connections, session transcripts, attachment payloads or token/tool histories.

The word **Project** is not a conversation Project here. The app stores a local project path/name and optional plain group label; project skill records are `{enabled,id,name,description,path}` and point at `SKILL.md` folders under the project `.agents/skills` convention. UI operations install/disable/compare project skills. There is no chat Session or SectionGroup entity, branch/subtask edge, or transcript importer/writer in the RPC data model.

**License gate:** root `LICENSE` is Sustainable Use License v1.0 (`SUL-1.0`), not an OSI-style permissive license. It grants use/modification only for own internal business purposes or non-commercial/personal use, and limits distribution to free non-commercial distribution. For a publicly distributed or paid Nexus Memory product, do not copy/derive from this code without separate permission; treat this repository as an externally observed architecture example only.

**Observed test scope:** with isolated Bun 1.4.5, `bun install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts` succeeded, then selected Project/sync/skills compatibility suites passed **45 tests / 0 failures / 206 assertions** across ten files. These test skills inventory, per-project skill toggles/comparison, dotagents sync, skill pack transfer, and Skills CLI lock handling; they do not exercise conversation/session migration. No app window, marketplace credential, or user skill folder was opened.

Recommendation: keep Skiller in the Skills-plane comparison set, but discard it as a direct Nexus migration dependency. Its Project grouping is a skill-library UI concept, not a ChatGPT project/section/session archive. Evidence: `docs/research/evidence/skiller-review-2026-11-20.txt`.

Primary sources: [repository](https://github.com/beautyfree/skiller), [latest release](https://github.com/beautyfree/skiller/releases/tag/v0.3.8), [pinned project/skill model](https://github.com/beautyfree/skiller/blob/019d1ab13ecbb194dc4b6052f22e1db249f9d932/apps/desktop/src/shared/rpc-schema.ts), [project implementation](https://github.com/beautyfree/skiller/blob/019d1ab13ecbb194dc4b6052f22e1db249f9d932/apps/desktop/src/main/projects.ts), [sync manifest](https://github.com/beautyfree/skiller/blob/019d1ab13ecbb194dc4b6052f22e1db249f9d932/apps/desktop/src/main/sync-profile.ts), [SUL-1.0 license](https://github.com/beautyfree/skiller/blob/019d1ab13ecbb194dc4b6052f22e1db249f9d932/LICENSE).

### Incremental candidate review: jacopobonomi/venv_manager (dependency-environment control, not history migration, 2026-11-20)

**Disposition: adjacent MCP/Python-environment reference only. It is not a chat, Project, Section, Session, or cross-harness migration tool.** Source checkout and remote `HEAD` agreed at `fd561c3501cf031aba0b589bde05dd147fe22f30` (2026-08-24, “docs: add official website domain”); the pinned module declares Go 1.24.2 and the repository is MIT. The latest GitHub Actions page showed CI #22 for this exact commit completed successfully on 2026-08-24. Local Go was unavailable (`go: No such file or directory`), so no local Go test result is claimed.
