The project provides a shared CLI/TUI and stdio JSON-RPC MCP server for durable Python venvs: create/list/remove/describe, install/run, ephemeral execution, package snapshots/diffs/rollback, import scanning, diagnosis, and a file watcher. A global registry stores venv creation/last-use metadata plus an optional project filesystem path and tags. The project relation is an environment-to-path association only: no nested Projects, Section Groups, Session records, transcript events, attachments, subtask/branch graph, model/token/tool history, per-harness resource importer, or scheduled-task model was found. It can help record which local Python environment a source project uses, but it cannot turn that association into the requested **Destruction Era** ChatGPT-to-Cloud session transfer. The MCP server itself is a service endpoint, not an importer for user MCP configuration.

**Policy and safety reading:** `NewServer` defaults to `safe`; `read-only` blocks non-read operations; safe mode permits creation, while removal, package installation, arbitrary `run_in_venv`, rollback, registry metadata mutation, and non-sandboxed `exec_ephemeral` need caller-supplied `confirm: true`. The `full` mode bypasses policy checks other than an optional allowlist. The `confirm` boolean is an MCP argument checked by server code, not independent proof of human approval. `exec_ephemeral` passes safe-policy authorization when `sandbox: true`, but the MCP handler then explicitly rejects that mode as “not yet supported”; the OS sandbox documented for the CLI is not available through MCP at this pinned commit. Therefore keep any future integration allowlisted, read-only by default, and never treat this tool as a source of transcript/session fidelity.

**Verification basis and caveat:** pinned README enumerates the CLI/MCP capabilities; CodeGraph-assisted source inspection covered `NewServer`, policy allowlists/authorization/tests, `execEphemeral`, `runInVenv`, and `SetRegistryMetadata`. No package execution or real Python environment was invoked during this review. GitHub Actions CI is upstream evidence only; local unit/integration tests remain unverified because Go is absent. Full notes and pinned sources: `docs/research/evidence/venv-manager-review-2026-11-20.txt`.

Primary sources: [pinned README and tool inventory](https://github.com/jacopobonomi/venv_manager/blob/fd561c3501cf031aba0b589bde05dd147fe22f30/README.md), [MCP policy implementation](https://github.com/jacopobonomi/venv_manager/blob/fd561c3501cf031aba0b589bde05dd147fe22f30/internal/mcp/policy.go), [MCP command execution implementation](https://github.com/jacopobonomi/venv_manager/blob/fd561c3501cf031aba0b589bde05dd147fe22f30/internal/mcp/exec.go), [pinned module and Go version](https://github.com/jacopobonomi/venv_manager/blob/fd561c3501cf031aba0b589bde05dd147fe22f30/go.mod), [successful upstream CI history](https://github.com/jacopobonomi/venv_manager/actions), [release notes](https://github.com/jacopobonomi/venv_manager/releases).

### Incremental candidate review: kirodotdev-labs/config-control-kiro (Kiro-specific config and chat dashboard, 2026-11-20)

**Disposition: high-value Kiro-specific configuration/session-visibility reference; not a cross-harness session or Cloud-history migrator.** Local and remote HEAD matched `580d2df3f2edafe4d7b979fbd249901cc5ddf346` (2026-11-12, Config Control for Kiro 3.0.0), and the repository's `v3.0.0` release is Apache-2.0. Upstream Build and Test #41 passed for the commit. This is a single-machine browser UI over Kiro CLI's configuration, not a hosted account console.

The app models **Global** (`~/.kiro/`) and project **Workspace** (`<project>/.kiro/`) scopes. It edits native Kiro agents, MCP servers, permissions/sandbox, hooks, steering, skills, prompts, workflows/specs, powers, ACP settings, code intelligence, and Kiro memory. Skills can be imported from local folders or GitHub into Kiro; the project does not provide a generic resource translation matrix for Claude/Codex/Cursor/etc.

Its chat dashboard is unusually useful as a Kiro source-adapter reference. The V3 reader scans local `<kiro-home>/sessions/<workspace-key>/sess_<id>/session.json` and `messages.jsonl`; it models ID/title/workspace/engine/time, agent/model, message count, running status, context use and credits. It preserves V3 `parentSessionId` for subagent chats and separates `/tangent` or rewind branches as `kind + branchOf`, while aggregating subagent credits into the parent. The V2/classic path asks `kiro-cli chat --list-sessions --all-cwds --format json` and reads local usage metadata. Critically, the V3 code explicitly reads **only metadata/metering**, not prompts, replies or tool results. Its `resumeCommand` is a convenience command for Kiro itself, not a portable session writer.

The dashboard imports Kiro's local V3 bookmark/title/tags metadata read-only. Its own tags are separate labels in `~/.kiro/kiroui/chat-tags.json`, not Kiro-native session tags. Unit tests explicitly cover tangent/rewind/subagent relations, those local dashboard fields, and ignoring a remote dashboard metadata entry. No Section Group / chat-Project graph, message transcript exporter/importer, attachment copier, or cross-harness parent/fork writer is implemented.

Kiro memory is a separate useful adapter seam: the app lists shared and account-scoped SQLite memories, typed `USER_PREFERENCE`/`SEMANTIC` and Global/repository scopes; create/update/delete are sent through Kiro's `_kiro/memory/*` ACP methods so Kiro itself validates/writes them. This is Kiro-specific memory management, not portable memory synchronization.

**Cloud boundary:** official Kiro docs describe Cloud sessions as stored in the account's remote session store; local `/chat save` and `/chat load` archives are unavailable for Cloud sessions. The app's V3 file reader is local-only; although its legacy listing delegates to `kiro-cli`, it has no explicit remote/cloud field or archive transfer path, and no live cloud listing/resume was exercised here. Therefore cloud session coverage is **unverified**, and this app cannot be counted as evidence that Cloud sessions or their transcript/attachments can be exported or migrated. Kiro's own CLI supports local V3 `/chat save` and `/chat load`; that is a different native-format path, not functionality provided by this app.

**Test/evidence:** source checkout clean at the reviewed commit; CodeGraph index contained 339 files / 4,270 nodes. Isolated Go 1.27.4 (temporary official toolchain, checksum verified) ran `go test -race ./internal/... ./tools/... -count=1 -timeout 120s` successfully across all listed packages; frontend `npm test` passed 51/51; `npm run build` succeeded (12,447 modules, with the main JS chunk at 702 KB and a Vite chunk-size warning). `npm audit --audit-level=high` first failed because the machine's configured npmmirror endpoint returned `[NOT_IMPLEMENTED]`; repeating with the official npm registry found 0 vulnerabilities. No Kiro CLI process, account, session store, cloud session, or real user's content was read or modified.

Recommendation: keep as a **Kiro local-config/session-metadata/memory adapter reference**, not a direct ChatGPT-to-Cloud converter. If Nexus adds a Kiro source adapter, prefer Kiro's documented local session archive where available, retain tangent/rewind/subagent graph edges and version the reader; keep remote Cloud sessions explicitly unsupported until a real documented export/import route is verified. Evidence: `docs/research/evidence/config-control-kiro-review-2026-11-20.txt`.

Primary sources: [repository and Apache-2.0 license](https://github.com/kirodotdev-labs/config-control-kiro), [v3.0.0 release](https://github.com/kirodotdev-labs/config-control-kiro/releases/tag/v3.0.0), [V3 session reader](https://github.com/kirodotdev-labs/config-control-kiro/blob/580d2df3f2edafe4d7b979fbd249901cc5ddf346/internal/sessions/store_v3.go), [session model and listing](https://github.com/kirodotdev-labs/config-control-kiro/blob/580d2df3f2edafe4d7b979fbd249901cc5ddf346/internal/sessions/service.go), [session dashboard metadata](https://github.com/kirodotdev-labs/config-control-kiro/blob/580d2df3f2edafe4d7b979fbd249901cc5ddf346/internal/sessions/dashboard.go), [Kiro session archive and session-tree docs](https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/chat/session-management/), [Tangent docs](https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/chat/tangent/), [Kiro Cloud sessions](https://kiro.dev/docs/cloud-sessions/), [upstream build/test and CodeQL runs](https://github.com/kirodotdev-labs/config-control-kiro/actions).


### Adjacent candidate review: danieljustus/symaira-vault (agent credential service, not conversation memory, 2026-11-20)

**Disposition: adjacent credential/MCP-security reference only; not an AI Session, Project, Section or memory migrator.** Repository `HEAD` and remote `HEAD` match `3ae37916b834e3fb81caddc038f4022967411ef8` (2026-11-18); this is a documentation-only product-boundary revision. Latest published release observed is `v0.25.0` (2026-11-16, Apache-2.0); its release page says there are 10 commits on main since. The project remains pre-1.0. Its accepted product boundary keeps `symvault` an independent encrypted credential CLI/service and assigns normal credential-management UI to the Brain product; neither is the conversation-memory owner.

The core record is an encrypted credential vault: path-keyed entries with fields/metadata, TOTP/passkey/SSH-key and notes, recipient/identity handling, manifest integrity, versioning and Git-backed history/sync. Directory-like names such as `projects/local-dev/` are secret namespaces, not chat Projects. The vault can import 1Password, Bitwarden, `pass`, CSV/browser exports and CXF credential archives; CXF collection nesting is translated into path prefixes. Those are password-manager/Credential Exchange imports, not chat transcripts, turns, branch/fork relationships, attachments or token-use records. README “Session Caching” means the unlocked vault key/session cached in the OS keyring for 15 minutes, not an AI conversation session.

**Actual harness integration:** the service exposes its own MCP tools to selected clients (including documented Claude Code, Codex, Hermes, OpenClaw and OpenCode paths) with per-agent profiles, `allowedPaths`, tool allowlists, capability gates, scoped expiring bearer tokens, approvals and audit logging. A small set of bundled Symaira prompts/skills appears as `/mcp__symvault__…` commands in clients that surface MCP prompts; `agent install` installs this service's own config/skill. This is useful design evidence for credential resource adapters and least-privilege MCP activation, but it does not inspect or migrate third-party MCP server lists, arbitrary Skills/Plugins, harness settings, Projects/Sections, or sessions.

Its API-template and egress-broker features can resolve a vault-held API key at call time without injecting it into the agent's process environment; this is potentially useful as an **optional secret reference provider** for a later Nexus integration. Do not copy decrypted values into the portable bundle, session transcript, manifest, or logs. It does not transfer a ChatGPT credential or register a hosted Cloud workspace, and it cannot replace verifying the destination's own auth model.

**Platform / maturity / quality:** the README lists native macOS Apple Silicon and Linux CLI/server support, plus iOS/Web where applicable; Windows and Intel Mac binaries are explicitly unsupported. GitHub shows 34 stars / 3 forks in the captured repository snapshot, so this is a small project rather than a broadly adopted harness standard. The upstream latest release is v0.25.0; main is 10 commits ahead. Its release notes disclose RustSec advisories in locked `quinn-proto` / `quinn-udp` versions and state released binaries did not enable that optional dependency. This review did not run a separate Cargo advisory scan or verify release SBOMs. Upstream CI run #3392 on parent `98b76ff` failed because a hosted macOS arm64 runner could not be acquired; the run page records the runner-capacity error. Main's immediate child `3ae3791` changes only `docs/product-boundaries.md`, so this is not evidence of an application test regression, nor does it prove a fully green CI check for current HEAD.

**Local verification (disposable target/cache; no user vault or real keychain):** `cargo test --workspace --all-features --locked` exited 0 under Rust/Cargo 1.98.0 with `SYMVAULT_TEST_KEYRING=memory` and `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/tmp/symaira-vault-target-2026-11-20`; test and doc-test binaries completed with zero failures. This includes source-bound Go differential contracts, encrypted vault/store/MCP tests, importer tests and approval/scope boundaries. The run includes one public-release check and is therefore network/time-sensitive. `cargo fmt --all --check` exited 0; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings` exited 0. One process-contract fixture printed `kill: 34136: No such process` while the overall suite still exited 0; retain it as a test-harness warning, not a product failure. No production vault, account, token or credential was opened or modified. No Cargo advisory audit or formal cryptographic review was performed.

Recommendation: retain Symaira Vault as an adjacent **credential-security / API-key / scoped-MCP reference**, not as a migration engine. Nexus should model secret values as references resolved under explicit user authorization and destination-side validation, not portable data to be copied. Its nested credential folders do not satisfy the requested **Destruction Era** Projects/Sections/session graph. Evidence: `docs/research/evidence/symaira-vault-review-2026-11-20.txt`.

Primary sources: [pinned repository README and license](https://github.com/danieljustus/symaira-vault/blob/3ae37916b834e3fb81caddc038f4022967411ef8/README.md), [credential migration guide](https://github.com/danieljustus/symaira-vault/blob/3ae37916b834e3fb81caddc038f4022967411ef8/docs/migration.md), [agent integration / scoped access](https://github.com/danieljustus/symaira-vault/blob/3ae37916b834e3fb81caddc038f4022967411ef8/docs/agent-integration.md), [release v0.25.0](https://github.com/danieljustus/symaira-vault/releases/tag/v0.25.0), [CI failure summary for parent commit](https://github.com/danieljustus/symaira-vault/actions/runs/44338046446), [HEAD product-boundary commit](https://github.com/danieljustus/symaira-vault/commit/3ae37916b834e3fb81caddc038f4022967411ef8).


### Incremental candidate review: pwnera/artbucket (brand-asset platform, adjacent MCP/import reference; not a session migrator)

**Disposition: medium-value adjacent reference, not a direct session/Cloud adapter.** Pinned main and remote HEAD agree at `84e34122c7eff9d401bef48edd20d21000464087` (2026-10-23). The source commit explicitly removes pointers to Artbucket Cloud because that hosted product closed; the open-source core is self-hosted.

Artbucket's tree is an organization-scoped digital-asset library: Projects hold assets, Collections, Brands, Portals and access grants; Brand pages contain content sections and have versions/comments/releases. MCP tools cover project selection and this DAM domain. Its `/api/v1/mcp` interface, project-bound consent/scopes, stateless Streamable HTTP contract, stable versioned tool schemas, Claude-specific skill, and preview→review→apply→undo (30-day) asset/brand import flow are useful adjacent patterns. The import guide explicitly says it has no direct Frontify/Bynder/Brandfolder/Drive connector: export files manually; ZIP folders can become Collections and a brand PDF can seed rules/draft pages; custom fields, rights and old links require separate work. None of those are ChatGPT Projects/Section Groups or AI chat archives.

Nuance: its web app has an Artbucket assistant sidebar with recent conversations per Artbucket Project, turn/step/action rendering, and a browser `sessionStorage` chat-ID pointer. The open-source UI delegates `/chats/{id}` and `/ask` to a separately configured `ASSISTANT_URL`; the app repo has no corresponding conversation database, API route, portable transcript exporter/importer or Cloud-session writer. Only the pointer is in browser sessionStorage. The external service's persistence/export/attachment/token behavior is outside this source review and remains unknown. Its `ab_...` keys are Artbucket service keys, not Anthropic/Cloud LLM API keys.

Local pinned-source quality review: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts` succeeded; `pnpm test` 734/734 pass; `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, and `pnpm contract:check` exited 0. DB integration tests were not run because the harness requires Postgres via `DATABASE_URL`, and Docker was unavailable. No real user account, API key or session was used. Full findings, limits and pinned primary links: `docs/research/evidence/artbucket-review-2026-11-20.txt`. Recommendation: reuse only the adapter-agnostic import safety and API governance patterns; exclude this repository from the direct migration capability set. After this record: **38/303 reviewed, 265 unreviewed**; next first unreviewed registry entry is `yeasy/ask`.

**Disposition: high-value adjacent asset-manifest/runtime-materialization reference; not a Project/Section/Session or Cloud API-key session writer.** Apache-2.0, Python. Source checkout and remote `main` match `43d6a659cb0d1b3409f8b0a98166f964bc067e7a` (2026-08-28); repository capture is 26 stars / 2 forks. Public GitHub release and PyPI both show v0.2.0 (2026-08-12) as latest; release notes say 34 Skills and 50 commits have landed on `main` since that tag.

This is useful for Nexus's **portable asset plane**: typed `harness-ai-kit.yml` plus lockfile resolves versioned Skills, CLIs, plugins, hooks, subagents, MCPs and loops, then stages and materializes them to runtime-specific locations. Main-branch source has profiles for Codex, DeepSeek Harness, Pi, Claude Code, Kiro, Cursor, OpenCode and Qoder; Skills use directory, Kiro-steering-Markdown or Cursor-rule materialization. Managed non-Skill assets are placed in generic `.agents/` asset directories; this alone does not prove native harness registration. The dedicated plugin install route is DSH-specific. The repository's “loop” state is autonomous workflow iteration state (iteration/status/errors/metrics), not chat-session turns or transcript history.

Important distribution split: current `main` source and README say 0.18.18, while PyPI's public page shows only 0.2.0; the exact 0.18.18 PyPI JSON URL returned HTTP 404. I downloaded the official 0.2.0 wheel without importing it; its 279,148-byte payload matched PyPI's SHA-256 `1fd3289fec8b2cff3af7d6185fd883adb76cb53c02476f741af1f3a761df765f`. Static inspection of that actual wheel found manifest/runtime/loop workflow code but no `conversation`, transcript, or chat-history import/export implementation. The exact-main CI public-release gate passed (run #33100658273), but the subsequent v0.18.18 release workflow #33101008389 failed during PyPI upload: its OIDC scoped token was rejected as invalid for project `harness-ai-kit`. Therefore do not treat README/main functionality or version 0.18.18 as a verified installable release.

Quality check: local `rtk pytest tools/test_public_release_gate.py` passed **30/30**. No full repository test suite was run. Other caution: the checked-in `docs/concepts.md` / `docs/asset-map.md` material is stale relative to current README/source and disagrees on runtime/asset counts; record version-specific evidence rather than repeating headline counts. Evidence, exact commands, output and distribution hashes: `docs/research/evidence/harness-ai-kit-review-2026-11-20.txt`.

**Recommendation:** retain as a strong implementation pattern for typed asset manifests, locks, runtime capability profiles and safe staged installs. Do not count as a ChatGPT-to-Claude/Cloud session migrator: no source Project/Section/session graph, turns, branch/child-task relation, attachments, tool-event/token accounting, schedule importer/exporter or Cloud session writer is implemented. After this review: **40/303 reviewed, 263 unreviewed**; next exact unreviewed registry entry is `24KaratAu/openhub`.


### Incremental candidate review: 24KaratAu/openhub (Skills catalog/exporter; not session migration)

**Disposition: small, useful adjacent Skill-catalog/export UX reference; exclude from direct ChatGPT/Cloud session-migrator coverage.** MIT, Python/Textual/SQLite; pinned main and remote HEAD `eb8a50b01fea5b88d8e028dcd03cfe6ac7880d66` (2026-09-27), latest tag v0.2.0. GitHub metadata at review: 21 stars / 4 forks. The tool browses a GitHub repository catalog, installs via OpenCode's `opencode get`, and exports a generated `SKILL.md` plus OpenCode-style agent Markdown into local/global `.opencode`, `.agents` and `.claude` directories. It scans installed `SKILL.md` files and can update from release tarballs with an external backup and rollback. These are Skills package-management behaviors, not full harness interchange.

Compatibility claim vs implementation: README lists OpenCode, Claude Code/Desktop, Cursor, Windsurf, Roo/Cline, AutoGen and CrewAI; source writes files to a subset of skill/agent directories and does not check that each receiving host actually loads or activates them. “MCP server” appears as a catalog type, but install is an `opencode get {slug}` shell call, and the exporter has no `mcpServers`/Claude MCP config, plugin/extension, prompt, key, scheduled task, Project/Section/Session or transcript writer. Its SQLite `history_log` records repository actions (`installed`, `exported`, `failed`, `updated`), not AI conversation turns or session history. A static scan of `app/` found no chat transcript/session importer or conversation model (the only `conversation` match was a seeded repository description).

Quality/safety: exact HEAD's upstream CI and release actions succeeded on 2026-09-27 (Python 3.10/3.11/3.12, GitHub Actions runs #36314091626 and #36314099009). The local CI runner was re-run on 2026-11-20 in an isolated HOME/venv; it exits 1 because `test_core.py::test_quality_scorer` expects a July 20 pushed date to remain “Excellent/Great,” but the date-dependent scorer computes a “Good” score after enough days have elapsed. The other official runner subprocess suites passed (16 and 7 tests). This is a clock-sensitive regression since the green CI result, not evidence of a current green run. A separate `pytest` all-tests-in-one-process attempt showed 23 passed/4 failed; three additional failures were test cross-contamination because `test_core` writes exported files into the shared working directory before local-skill scans; the project’s official `run_all_tests.py` avoids that by launching test files in subprocesses.

Additional guardrail concerns observed in source: `run_all_tests.py` deletes `~/.cache/opencode-hub/repos.db` before testing, so do not run it under a real HOME; this review sandboxed HOME. Skill export opens destination files with write mode and does not preview/back up existing same-slug files; update has separate rollback protection. Installer interpolates catalog slug into `create_subprocess_shell("opencode get …")`; the value is GitHub metadata and no exploit was demonstrated, but argv-based invocation/validation is preferable. README claims Linux/macOS/Windows whereas CI is Ubuntu-only. Code uses deprecated naive UTC methods and `tarfile.extractall()` without an explicit filter under Python 3.12/3.13.

**Recommendation:** reuse only the content/metadata display, explicit project/global Skill export paths, source/installed version discovery, and transactional *update* backup pattern—after adding conflict previews and native-host capability checks. Do not treat its files as activated skills without testing each target harness; do not use it as Project/Section/session, attachment, branch/subtask, memory, MCP-config, plugin, schedule, or Cloud API-Key migration evidence. Full pinned source/test evidence: `docs/research/evidence/24karatau-openhub-review-2026-11-20.txt`. Registry review count after this row: **41/303 reviewed / 262 unreviewed**; next exact unreviewed registry entry is `xiaode-ai/nanofile-connector`.

## Incremental candidate review: xiaode-ai/nanofile-connector (2026-11-20)

- **Classification:** Windows-local NanoFile file-manager MCP server/CLI/Skills/plugin bundle; adjacent host-manifest example, not a conversation/session or Project/Section migrator. MIT; pinned `26a75c6d31b99594f449a0539d34a126aeb0de80`; GitHub API at review: 22 stars / 1 fork, 5 commits ahead of v1.0.9 release. README/package describe Windows 10/11, seven Skills, and 27 MCP tools. The 27 unique `mcp/schema.json` names and seven skill directories are present; no session import/export implementation was found in the reviewed module trees.
- **Compatibility value and boundary:** use only as an example of how host-specific Skills, MCP config and plugin manifests are packaged. Its filesystem tools do not create a ChatGPT/Codex Project or restore conversation turns, Section membership, attachments, parent/fork/child-task links, token history, memory, schedules, or Claude Desktop client-managed sessions. Root `plugin.json` uses the Antigravity schema and root `mcp.json` is not the current portable Agent Plugins format. The `.claude-plugin` manifest contains skill paths without `./`; the local older Claude Code 2.1.76 validator reports invalid `skills` input, while current official manifest docs require component paths to start with `./` except limited cases. A current target-version validator is still required before claiming Claude-plugin support.
- **Protocol/reliability evidence:** `callMcp` fallback hardcodes MCP `2024-11-05`, sends `tools/call` immediately after `initialize` without `notifications/initialized`, and parses an entire stdout `data` event as one JSON object rather than framing newline-delimited messages. A synthetic fake-server test reproduced a timeout when the initialize response was split across two chunks; a whole-message fake server succeeded but recorded no initialized notification. This is a bounded local client probe, not a live NanoFile Windows-server failure. The MCP 2026-07-28 specification is stateless and removes the initialize handshake, making the hardcoded legacy path a version-compatibility concern.
- **Quality/supply-chain:** all 16 JSON files parsed and both JS syntax checks passed, but `npm test` exits 1 because package.json has no test script; `npm run` lists only `bump` and `release`; no `tests/` directory or test/build workflow exists. The only workflow is Release. The Windows installer fetches `nf.exe` from `main` and checks only MZ+minimum length, not a cryptographic digest/signature. The release script stages everything, commits/tags, pushes main/tag and creates a release; it was not run. Local MCP runtime was not tested because the NanoFile desktop app is Windows-only and unavailable in this macOS environment.
- Evidence and literal commands/results: `docs/research/evidence/nanofile-connector-review-2026-11-20.txt`. Candidate inventory is **42/303 reviewed / 261 unreviewed**; next exact unreviewed row is `cbruyndoncx/obsidian-skills-manager`.

Installer stages before rename and attempts rollback, but remote file names are written below the staging path without a visible canonical containment check; no traversal exploit was reproduced.

A direct build fails in a bare checkout because dependencies are absent; in a disposable copy, `npm ci --ignore-scripts` followed by `npm run build` succeeds. - **Limits:** no ChatGPT Project/Section/Session or Claude Desktop Chat/Cowork/Code session import/export; no transcript, attachment, branch, child task, token, memory, schedule or Cloud/API-Key session path. The Claude MCP target is Claude Code project `.mcp.json`, not a claim about Desktop. No migration-fidelity result.
- **Use in Nexus:** borrow the asset-plane manifest/renderer/rollback idea only; keep credentials out of bundles and record host acceptance separately. Evidence: `docs/research/evidence/skillet-review-2026-11-20.txt`. Inventory now **52/303 reviewed / 251 unreviewed**; Claude Desktop Chat/Cowork/Code all remain in scope, and local/self-hosted Claude Code stays distinct from hosted Cloud.


### Candidate review: harperreed/toki (2026-11-21)

- **Disposition:** adjacent project-aware todo/MCP system, not a chat/session migrator. The Project contains Todos, not sessions, Sections or Section Groups. ### Adjacent candidate: `inventlist/localvault` (2026-11-21)

**Verdict:** useful security/MCP reference; not a project, conversation, or session migrator. The Ruby CLI stores encrypted secrets in named vaults; dot-notation groups namespace secret keys, and its `import` command reads `.env`/JSON/YAML credentials. Optional cloud sync sends encrypted vault bundles through the InventList bearer-token API, not an LLM provider gateway. Its stdio MCP exposes secret listing, explicit plaintext reveal, secret writes/deletes, and a non-executing command builder. Claude Code-only guard hooks and the 8-hour `SessionCache` refer to secret hygiene and unlocked vault keys, not AI session behavior.

It has no ChatGPT Projects/Sections, transcript graph, branches/forks, message/tool/attachment import/export, Claude Desktop Chat/Cowork/Code session integration, memory/skills/plugin migration, or fidelity tests. Treat secrets as references and re-bind on destination; never bundle values. The guard fails open and is not a sandbox. License metadata conflicts (Apache-2.0 source README/LICENSE vs MIT gemspec/RubyGems), so do not adopt as a dependency until clarified. Main `9a9750b` matched live main; upstream Test #72 passed on that commit, while local tests were not run because the available Ruby 2.6.10/Bundler 1.17.2 does not satisfy the project's Ruby >=3.2/Bundler 2.6.9 requirements. See [full evidence](research/evidence/localvault-review-2026-11-21.txt), [CI #72](https://github.com/inventlist/localvault/actions/runs/44269992948), and [RubyGems v1.15.1](https://rubygems.org/gems/localvault/versions/1.15.1).


### Adjacent candidate: `opencue/cuecards` (2026-11-21)

**Verdict:** high-value reference for project-scoped agent profiles and capability-oriented Skills/MCP adapters, but not a session migrator. One `.cue.profile` selects a YAML profile; full native runtimes are Claude Code CLI/Codex, while nine other adapters emit partial Skills/rules and MCP config. The schema also includes plugins, persona, playbooks and gates, but external adapter output is not equivalent to full runtime materialization. It has no ChatGPT Projects/Sections, Claude Desktop modes, hosted Cloud or provider-key Session writer.

Local analytics records skill-use events (including session ID/cwd/profile/duration and redacted miss prompts), not a conversation graph, attachments, fork topology, tool results, token usage or restore archive. Repo-only importers for Claude Code history, Copilot and Hermes turn selected text into Skill-eval examples; these are absent from the published npm `files` allowlist.

Quality is mixed: npm current dist-tag is 0.10.0 (gitHead `1737340b…`), but GitHub's Releases page marks v0.9.4 latest. The current main HEAD `6c81ebd` has no visible standard CI run; latest visible #653 failed and repeats the same grader-sandbox output failure as #648/#650. A local isolated profile test subset passed 171 tests/553 assertions, but the targeted eval test reproduced that failure (8 pass, 1 fail, 1 skip); no full-suite pass is claimed. Details and primary links: [evidence report](research/evidence/cuecards-review-2026-11-21.txt).

### Adjacent resource candidate: smartsheet/cli-agent-power-tools (2026-11-21)

**Verdict:** a narrowly useful prompt/MCP adapter example, not a migration product. Pinned main `7e41cca47d4487a28106b403d9cf448ca0b2216e`, MIT. It supplies six Markdown agents for Claude Code and Gemini CLI with distinct host frontmatter, restricted Smartsheet MCP tool names, structured output contracts and write previews. Smartsheet’s article verifies the Claude Desktop **Code Tab** reuse path only; it does not cover Chat/Cowork session state or Anthropic-hosted Cloud. The current Smartsheet MCP server may connect to ChatGPT, Claude and other clients, but that is external data access, not migration of ChatGPT Projects or Sessions.

**Important gaps:** no Project/Section/SectionGroup or conversation graph, turn/branch/child-session importer, attachments, usage ledger, model-provider/API-key gateway translation, session writer, or round-trip fidelity tests. Cron snippets wrap local invocations; they are not a scheduler transfer format. Six `.claude/agents` and six `.gemini/agents` files are duplicated and not behaviorally identical. Gemini's automated-chain text instructs automatic continuation without confirmation while the same write agent separately forbids writes without explicit approval. Treat this as a high-priority approval-gate regression test, not a pattern to copy.

**Synthetic safety observations:** mocked temporary-HOME runs (no real CLI/API/credentials/network) show both setup scripts remove an existing `smartsheet-mcp` before reading/validating the replacement token; a piped `y<Enter>` confirmation sequence then fails token input after the mock removal. The successful isolated paths place a synthetic token in CLI process arguments. Do not run these scripts with real credentials without safer secret handling and rollback. The pinned tree has no project tests or workflow; CodeGraph indexed zero source files. License MIT. Evidence: [cli-agent-power-tools-review-2026-11-21.txt](research/evidence/cli-agent-power-tools-review-2026-11-21.txt).

**Disposition:** retain as a low-adoption, high-specificity asset-plane reference only. It strengthens the Nexus requirement for per-host manifest/permission serializers and semantic behavior tests. Inventory **58/303 reviewed / 245 unreviewed**; next candidate `RubenGlez/lokalite`. The user-confirmed target remains all three Claude Desktop modes (Chat, Cowork, Code) plus local/self-hosted Claude Code; Anthropic-hosted Cloud remains a separate category.


### High-value adjacent credential-plane candidate: `RubenGlez/lokalite` (2026-11-21)

**Verdict:** keep as a security/credential-reference design reference, not a cross-Harness Session engine or model-provider gateway. Pinned main `1fa254361836ac180d0e4d059a1afd684fdc6a32`; MIT; latest release page `v2.9.0`; macOS 14+ only. The product is a local encrypted secrets vault with a menu-bar app, CLI and stdio MCP. Its project/environment hierarchy scopes credentials; that use of “Project” is not an AI conversation Project/Section hierarchy.

**Borrow:** typed `lokalite://` references, env-scope mapping, key material outside portable artifacts, a brokered MCP handoff that omits the raw secret from MCP responses, explicit per-secret access tiers, activity logging and tests for secret non-disclosure. Destination adapters should preserve a source credential reference but require human re-binding; values never go in a Nexus bundle.

**Gaps / caveats:** no ChatGPT Project/Section or transcript/session import/export, turn/branch/child-session graph, media handling, desktop-mode-specific session API, model-provider API-key routing/base-URL config, hosted Cloud writer, scheduler or fidelity metric. MCP registration writes Claude Code/Desktop, Cursor or Windsurf config, not conversations. The default MCP tool set includes `use_environment`, which changes the one active environment despite the “read-only” label. The `block` secret tier is best-effort process-name detection; strong approval tiers require the local GUI daemon. Source-level config writer uses an empty dictionary after malformed/unreadable JSON, and a temp Swift probe reproduced replacing malformed config with a minimal Lokalite-only config; preserve/backup before applying any generic client-config writer. Product metadata is plaintext though secret values are AES-GCM/keychain-protected; handoff files temporarily contain plaintext with owner-only permissions, self-delete/30-second sweep, and then export into an agent-controlled shell.

**Quality:** exact pinned `main` local `rtk swift build` exit 0; full `rtk swift test` exit 0 (180 tests, zero failures). GitHub CI #340 succeeded on v2.9.0 app commit `8c5b1cf`; current 1fa2543 head is a docs-only roadmap commit. Local build/full tests only; no actual Desktop UI/provider connection, Touch ID UX, or migration runtime was exercised. Detailed evidence and correction ledger: [lokalite-review-2026-11-21.txt](research/evidence/lokalite-review-2026-11-21.txt). Inventory **59/303 reviewed / 244 unreviewed**; next `bunhine0452/Ocul-PM`. All three Claude Desktop modes remain in target scope; this candidate only offers a generic local Claude Desktop MCP config path, not per-mode session migration.

## Claude Desktop third-party inference: current official import and storage boundaries (2026-11-21)

The user's target now explicitly includes Claude Desktop 3P **Chat, Cowork and Code**. Current Anthropic deployment docs describe `Developer → Configure Third-Party Inference`, requiring an Anthropic Messages-compatible gateway (`POST /v1/messages` with streaming and tools; optional `GET /v1/models`) and supporting static API key or interactive OIDC. The app's 3P history remains local; the API key is an inference credential, not an import/session API.

The 3P local store is mode-specific: Chat/Cowork JSON state + working directories under `local-agent-mode-sessions/`, with uploads, outputs and an `audit.jsonl`; Code metadata under `claude-code-sessions/` while transcripts remain in `~/.claude/projects/` shared with local CLI. The audit records prompts, assistant responses, tool inputs/results, returned thinking, permission decisions, model and usage, in plaintext; file/full-disk protection, retention and deletion matter. Project memory under `spaces/<projectId>/memory/` differs from global Cowork memory and instructions. Chat/Cowork's UI is being gradually unified; preserve mode/version as observed metadata instead of assuming stable boundaries.

The official import wizard imports only Claude-origin data: chats/projects from claude.ai plus local Cowork/Code sessions on the device. Its copy is one-time, has project-file/attachment caveats, and does not import cloud Cowork sessions. The device-to-device export is another feature and is an unencrypted ZIP; it omits API keys/connector credentials, some settings and artifacts. This provides strong reference material for a future target-native restore test, but no documented arbitrary ChatGPT-to-Claude Desktop API was found. The Enterprise Compliance API can retrieve certain managed local sessions, but its auth/product scope is not the generic 3P/API-key session store. Full citation ledger: `docs/research/evidence/claude-desktop-3p-chat-cowork-code-2026-11-21.txt`.

## Ocul-PM (`bunhine0452/Ocul-PM`) source and runtime review (2026-11-21)

Ocul-PM is a MIT/Tauri local-first project manager that writes Markdown journals/plans and tracks agent file activity. Its `Session` aggregates activity intervals/file counts and a list of native conversation IDs; `FileChangeEvent` is not a turn/event archive. Its transcript parser identifies successful Claude Code file-edit paths for verification, not complete conversation reconstruction. No runtime caller for `SessionId::imported()` was found; backdated manual journal creation does not provide an external conversation importer. The one-click Claude Desktop MCP setup was removed; mode-specific Desktop import is absent.

Local macOS checks on pinned `9dd0f85`: Vitest 1,975/1,975, Rust 1,234 passed / 5 ignored, tsc/build/custom lint/fmt/clippy passed; upstream Windows Portability #305 failed two Git-spawn performance-budget assertions despite release CI and E2E passing. Synthetic source-hook probe showed full JSON payloads can be appended to `.oculpm/hooks/claude-events.jsonl` with prompt/assistant-text canaries in plaintext mode 0644 under umask 022; log compaction only occurs once all lines are consumed and file size is at least 1MiB. Treat this as a privacy warning, not as proof of malicious handling. Full architecture/code/test probe and command ledger: `docs/research/evidence/ocul-pm-review-2026-11-21.txt`.

## Norte (`compilando/norte`) — audit plane and UI-state reference (2026-11-21)

Pinned to exact `main` `52256ccb816999705fb681a5336d39839497d099`; local `git ls-remote` matched. Norte's versioned `<state_dir>/session.json` stores screen state and opaque `SessionBody` data. The body includes layouts and per-slot location/cursor/back-forward/marks/search plus panel settings; the core does not interpret the body. Its in-memory built-in `Assistant` stores Reader/Order/Answer/Failed turns with a 200-turn cap; the controller initializes a fresh assistant at boot, and neither `SessionBody` nor `SlotState` carries its turns. The separate `--session` value on `norte mcp serve` establishes the `Actor::Agent` identity used by policy/approvals/undo. Its `journal.db` hash-chains operation/actor/path/reversal/batch rows; it records file mutations, not the prompts or response that caused them. This is a rich adjacent reference for an **audit plane**, not a transcript/Project/Section migrator.

The model provider abstraction supports Anthropic, Ollama and OpenAI-compatible streaming chat, but a provider API is not a native conversation archive. No ChatGPT/Codex source importer, arbitrary transcript writer, Projects/Sections membership adapter, attachments, token-usage ledger, branch/subagent graph or schedule migration was found in focused source/docs scan. The app's filesystem VPaths and profiles/layouts are not conversation Projects/Sections.

Quality/portability is mixed. Release page has `v0.3.0-alpha.16` (Oct 16); current README calls macOS source builds and Windows x64 preview. GitHub page reports ordinary CI/GUI/Release workflows disabled; scheduled remote-integration #62 passed on Nov 20, limited to SFTP/FTP/S3 tests. On this Mac, the documented just-test workspace command failed to compile `norte-vfs-local` due `libc::O_PATH` not available and `u64::From<i32>` type errors, despite source comments claiming a macOS fallback. Three selected-crate tests also failed on this host (two arbitrary non-UTF8 filename fixtures and one native watcher precise-pane assertion); 2,058/2,061 focused tests passed. Selected doctests 484/484, format check and focused Clippy passed. `just ci` and the full coverage/license/docs gates remain unverified because `just` is not installed and the macOS compile gate is red.

License is per-crate: `norte-core`, `norte-ai`, `norte-mcp`, and `norte-cli` are AGPL-3.0-only; some protocol and frontend crates are MIT OR Apache-2.0. Do not treat the GitHub repository's multi-license badge as one permissive license. Detailed code paths, errors, exact commands and exit statuses are recorded in `docs/research/evidence/compilando-norte-review-2026-11-21.txt`.

## JoeyWangTW/todo-cli — low-value Gemini/tmux wrapper (2026-11-21)

Pinned main at 22caf3182d01d321264f1ee189e8ce457b25c190 (2025-10-14 commit; 9 commits, MIT, 7 stars/1 fork). ## t59688/skillpack-manager — Skill resource plane with confirmed path-boundary blocker (reviewed 2026-11-21)

Pinned main `2d8f5507c4926fccb776c121879b5bed449d7f9d`, MIT, npm `@t59688/skillpack` v0.1.3. It packages versioned `SKILL.md` folders and shared references/assets, installs/syncs them into 13 global skill-directory adapters, and tracks pack/target checksums. It does not migrate Project/Section/session history, attachments, MCP/plugins, memory records, schedules, or Claude Desktop Chat/Cowork/Code. Local suite/typecheck/lint/build pass (37 tests), but two isolated malicious-manifest probes confirmed path traversal: `skill.name=../../escaped` wrote outside the selected target root, and `skill.path=../secret-source` copied a sibling directory into an installed Skill while `audit` reported no issues and risk level Low. Do not use it on real targets or trust external packs until strict path containment/atomic staging and regression tests are added. Keep only as a resource-plane design lead; its target directory copy is not semantic portability. The GitHub repo has no releases; npm v0.1.3 was last published 2026-05-27 and main was last pushed 2026-05-28. [Full source/POC evidence](research/evidence/skillpack-manager-review-2026-11-21.txt).

This is a substantial **sub-agent OrchestrationPlane**, not a conversation/session migration engine. The MCP server launches and supervises local Claude/Codex agents, optionally routes its own tasks to configured Claude Messages or OpenAI-compatible endpoints, and has a small durable context-handoff plus per-session latest metering sample. `AgentState` is an in-memory map for running processes/drivers, not a persistent parent/child transcript graph. The direct API provider path can target configured base URLs, but makes one non-streaming Bearer-auth POST to `/v1/messages` or `/v1/chat/completions` and normalizes output; it does not restore or persist a Claude Desktop client session. Claude Desktop is documented as MCP-only, without per-turn hook injection. No ChatGPT Project/Section/session import/export, conversation tree, multimodal attachment transfer, schedule import, or three-mode Claude Desktop writer was found. **Disposition:** retain as a high-value adjacent orchestration, handoff, permission and API-adapter architecture reference; exclude as a Nexus cross-harness migration engine.

Reviewed main at exact SHA 2537ee2ba8941ace891dfced324f2b411a5c0ba9 (latest release v0.1.2). ASM is a local central store/projection manager for Skills, MCP configs/binaries and a subset of Plugins across four agent paths. Its migrate command imports unmanaged resources; it does not read or write conversations, ChatGPT/Codex Project/Section graphs, Claude Code sessions, or any of Claude Desktop Chat/Cowork/Code/API-key gateway sessions. GitHub metadata has no license designation and the clone contains no LICENSE, so code reuse is not cleared.

Local go test ./..., go vet ./..., and go build ./... passed under an official temporary Go 1.27.4 toolchain (checksum verified); however, there are no tests for internal/migrator, MCP injection or plugin migration, and the only upstream workflow is build/release. Two temporary tests of the exact released binary confirmed path safety issues: (1) an MCP command located at a sibling path sharing the configured agent-home string prefix was treated as internal, copied into ASM and deleted from the sibling; (2) an unvalidated --id ../../../../escaped-package wrote files outside ASM_HOME. MCP environment values are stored as plaintext config.json with mode 0644 (observed with a dummy value), and migration removes the original binary before config injection. Retain only as a resource-plane architecture study; do not adopt unchanged. Full evidence: [asm-cli-asm-cli-review-2026-11-21.txt](research/evidence/asm-cli-asm-cli-review-2026-11-21.txt).

Inventory is now **87/303 reviewed, 216 unreviewed**; next: b2amedina/omaset. The survey remains incremental, not exhaustive.


## b2amedina/omaset — local settings/MCP control plane, not Harness migration (2026-11-21)

Pinned `5548b2789706a950f3d954fd234190ee4d9d367d`, MIT, manifest 0.3.0. Omaset is an Omarchy-targeted keyboard-first settings UI, CLI and MCP server sharing a typed settings/backend model. It is not an AI conversation archive or migration system: no ChatGPT/Codex Project, Section Group, Session/event graph, branch/subtask, attachment, tool-call/token ledger, memory, or agent schedule representation was found. Its “night light schedule” is a local display setting, not a scheduled task. Claude Desktop Chat, Cowork and Code plus the third-party inference/gateway path are unsupported; the Desktop config-path helper is used only in tests, not the runtime MCP injector.

The strongest design reference is an intentionally narrow shared control plane (schema, UI/CLI/MCP), field-level edits, backup/history/undo, validation and bounded subprocesses. A temporary macOS fixture run using Lua 5.1.5 reported 23/29 passing; six failures involved BSD `wc` padding, a missing Omarchy locales registry, or unavailable `setsid`. This is not a clean cross-platform test result. Full source and test evidence: [omaset-review-2026-11-21.txt](research/evidence/omaset-review-2026-11-21.txt).


## dougrn/MCP-MegaBrain — MCP memory sidecar, not a session migrator (2026-11-21)

Pinned `2b8fd6b6e05fd74ea5ca4cf085b120815e4fb14e`, MIT. The project exposes a local SQLite/FTS5 “brain” through stdio MCP and CLI, storing rules, mandatory-spec directives, manually recorded failure/fix experiences, and golden snippets. It is the *resource/instruction/memory plane*, not import/export of ChatGPT/Codex Projects, Section Groups, conversation sessions, branches, tools, attachments, usage, or schedules. A tested stdio session successfully recorded and consulted one synthetic rule via a temporary database; this proves MCP tool operation only, not Desktop mode compatibility or archive migration.

Source review found that project IDs are normalized to only their final path segment. In-memory synthetic testing showed `org-one/secret-app` and `org-two/secret-app` collide and that a consultation from the latter returns the former’s rule, spec, experience, and pattern. Treat project labels as non-security boundaries; separate test also observed 0755 data directory and 0644 SQLite file under umask 022. The application does not explicitly encrypt or make the database private. Prompt instructions to consult/record are not runtime enforcement, and a “mandatory spec” is only stored as a string directive.

Local locked test suite: 17 passed. README is inconsistent: its badge says 17, but the test section says 15 suites and claims schema-migration coverage not found in source/tests. Recommendation: retain only as an adjacent MCP memory/record-schema pattern; do not adopt as a Nexus migrator or directly share its DB across projects until scope keys and file permissions are hardened. Full POC, test, and cleanup evidence: [dougrn-mcp-megabrain-review-2026-11-21.txt](research/evidence/dougrn-mcp-megabrain-review-2026-11-21.txt).

 
## eddieran/skillpm — high-value Agent Skills ResourcePlane, not session migration (2026-11-21)

Pinned main 26fb1c66b274b8729f5bcca0c2e31d2013cf3141, Apache-2.0, 2-star snapshot, latest release v4.0.0. This is a deterministic CLI for Agent Skills acquisition, source/version/dependency resolution, scanning, lockfiles, project/global manifests, sync and filesystem injection to seven documented verified and three best-effort targets. It does not migrate ChatGPT/Codex Projects or Section Groups, conversations/sessions/branch graphs, attachments, MCP/plugin config, memory, or scheduled tasks; its own architecture says memory/scheduler subsystems were removed in v4.0.0. The adapter writes Skill files, not target session/API state.

Quality evidence is stronger than most low-adoption candidates: exact-HEAD CI passed formatting, vet, unit/integration tests, coverage floors, build, sync regression and race; exact-HEAD nightly E2E passed six file/provider lanes. Local Go 1.27.4 tests and coverage gates passed. However, scheduled security run 26863966648 failed at govulncheck and skipped Trivy/SBOM/secret scan; run logs expired. Current local govulncheck on its outdated pinned x/mod 0.33.0 reports no reachable vulnerable symbols but two module-level advisories fixed in x/mod 0.40.0.

Temporary-copy POCs found reasons not to adopt its code unchanged: project manifest AtomicWrite follows a predictable .tmp symlink and overwrites a file outside the project; Git skill resolution follows source symlinks outside the checkout and the scanner misses prompt injection in ancillary files; two same-leaf skill refs collide and last-write wins; crafted adapter ref org/.. writes outside the skills dir and Remove recursively deletes the parent .claude tree (normal Git resolution separately rejects ..); and a late lock-save failure leaves state pointing at v2 while rollback removes both v1 and v2. These were all synthetic temporary files, not user data or the upstream checkout. Full trace/results: [eddieran-skillpm-review-2026-11-21.txt](research/evidence/eddieran-skillpm-review-2026-11-21.txt). Retain as a high-value ResourcePlane/reference only, with a security and transaction redesign before borrowing implementation.


## Elixion-ai/claudepass — local SecretPlane reference, not session migration (2026-11-21)

Pinned main a18b332b8bbfe6661eab2841ec169d3b1f878da3, MIT. ClaudePass stores encrypted local secrets behind opaque Handles and project/global manifests; it injects them only into a child process, redacts known output forms, and offers Claude Code hooks/skill, a Codex AGENTS snippet, and three MCP tools. This is a useful resource/security boundary, not an importer/exporter for ChatGPT/Codex Projects, Section Groups, conversations, sessions, attachments, memory, or schedules. Its own PRD/Threats explicitly exclude cloud-hosted sessions/relay, team sync, and meaningful Windows support; its Vault has no per-agent/session ACL.

Mode-specific: `integrate claude` writes a local Claude Code skills-directory plugin. Anthropic’s current matrix says skills load on Chat/Cowork/Code, hooks are ignored on Chat but load in Cowork/Code, and local MCP is ignored on Chat. This repository has no account-backed Chat/Cowork installer or Desktop/gateway session adapter; a shared plugin folder is not proof of behavior across all modes. Keep only as a SecretPlane reference; model raw secrets separately from transferable conversation artifacts. Local race tests, CI, and static vulnerability scan are recorded in [claudepass-review-2026-11-21.txt](research/evidence/claudepass-review-2026-11-21.txt).

## interrupted-sh/murk — adjacent SecretPlane and scoped-agent credentials, not session migration (2026-11-21)

Pinned HEAD 8e884703eceb63bf6f1102535ff60da20ddbcc23, latest v0.15.2, dual MIT/Apache, small adoption snapshot. murk stores age-encrypted secrets in a Git-friendly vault and adds recipient/private/group scopes, revocation, time-bounded agent grants, tag policy, environment injection/masking, MCP and editor configuration writers. This is useful for separating API-key references from conversation archives; it is not a Projects/Sections/session importer or a history converter.

Its own docs caution that agent grants/MCP/policy are experimental guardrails: an agent under the same OS user can reach the owner key or unset environment flags; `murk_get` sends raw values into model context; output masking does not sandbox child writes or transform outputs; revoked ciphertext remains in Git history until values are rotated. Local all-feature Rust tests, formatting and Clippy pass; exact-HEAD CI and security jobs pass. Keep as SecretPlane design reference only. [Detailed review](research/evidence/interrupted-sh-murk-review-2026-11-21.txt).

## 2026-11-21 scope confirmation and incremental GitHub review — Claude Desktop 3P + skillspp #98

The user confirmed Claude Desktop **Chat, Cowork, and Code** are all in scope alongside local/self-hosted Claude Code. Current first-party Claude Desktop third-party documentation confirms a first-party `gateway` inference provider using a gateway base URL/key; the gateway must implement Anthropic Messages API `POST /v1/messages` with streaming/tool use, while `GET /v1/models` is optional. Docs explicitly name Chat, Cowork, and Code as sending cache-control breakpoints. This config is only the provider/inference route and a separate secret plane; it is not a history importer or portable session API. Desktop's local 3P import flow writes to the local session store and specifically imports Claude.ai histories/projects plus earlier local Cowork/Code sessions, not ChatGPT exports. The export is a one-time copy. The import docs mark Desktop 5.11492.0 as the cutoff for most automatic copies and 5.1231.0 for additional project/folder/pin completeness, so native Desktop app version and OS remain Gate 0.

OpenAI's current Project docs describe chats/files/instructions, project memory, and branched chats; its export produces conversation JSON and other account data/files, but is an archive, not an account rehydration path. Preserve Projects, source-qualified sidebar Section Group membership, Codex conversations, task/subtask/session relations, and attachments as separate source nodes/edges until an actual export establishes the fields. Do not promote the unverified voice-recognition correction to source metadata; the user-supplied canonical label remains **Destruction Era**.

Candidate #98 `omer-ayhan/skillspp`, pinned at `2440a90013889d59e915f838dac8710088c42955`, is a Skills package manager, not a conversation migrator. Local typecheck/lint/unit/integration/contract/e2e/build/test:ci checks passed; the only MCP tool is `skills.validate`. Harmless isolated tests reproduced automatic GitHub hook execution and a symlink destination escape, so reject its installer as-is. Keep only a small adjacent Skill ResourcePlane reference. Inventory: **98/303 reviewed, 205 pending**; next `patterson-agents/cli`. Research remains incremental and non-exhaustive. [Candidate ledger](research/evidence/skillspp-review-2026-11-21.txt).

### Candidate #128 follow-up — current Skills-plane comparator and upstream status

GitHub's current `gh skill` is a stronger native baseline for the ResourcePlane than this repo's branch-based lock: its public-preview docs (CLI >=2.90) include preview, explicit agent/scope, ref pinning and source/ref/tree-hash provenance. It still does not migrate Projects or Sessions. A fake Git clone failure in a disposable project produced stderr warning `Failed to update 'demo'`, but `agentskills update demo` still exited 0 and printed `All skills are up to date.` The installed file remained unchanged. ## 2026-11-22 official Claude Desktop 3P store/gateway verification

The user again confirmed all Claude Desktop Chat, Cowork and Code modes. Current official docs now make the target precise: Claude Desktop on third-party inference routes model requests through the configured provider while keeping conversations on-device; developer-mode configuration is the Desktop 3P config object rather than the CLI's `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` setting. Chat/Cowork and Code store records are mode-specific, and the first-party importer copies Claude-origin chats/projects plus local earlier Cowork/Code sessions rather than a generic ChatGPT export. Gateway mode also does not create Anthropic-hosted cloud environments or Remote Control. This sharpens the adapter matrix but does not prove a ChatGPT-origin writer; version/OS-specific native listing, resume and attachment integrity tests remain open. ## 2026-11-22 candidate #138 — safebuffer/cli-manager-mcp

Pinned HEAD `92fd151a239994cbf8c94e723c2c7b59d5ca8d68` (Apache-2.0, 1 star / 0 forks, created and last pushed 2025-09-22; no release, tag, Actions run or test suite). It is one Python MCP server (`server.py`) exposing four background-process tools: execute a command, fetch output/status, list processes, and clean up. This is neither a project/session migration tool nor a cross-harness MCP configuration importer.

Security is the main result: the only execute tool schema is `cmd: str`; the manager computes `shell = isinstance(cmd, str)` and calls `subprocess.Popen(cmd, shell=shell, ...)`, so the exposed path executes arbitrary shell commands as the server user's OS account. There is no command allowlist, per-call approval/sandbox, cwd, timeout, or user-scoped process registry. Results return command text plus full stdout/stderr, and the code logs the command without redaction; the repository warns that it must be used only with trusted agents or in a sandbox, but does not enforce that itself. Process state is a global in-memory dictionary, not a persistent session store. Keep this as a security-gate example for MCP capability inventory; do not bundle or launch it for Nexus.

Checks: `rtk codegraph init --yes` indexed 1 Python file / 44 nodes / 86 edges; `server.py` AST parse passed. The tracked tree contains only `.gitignore`, `LICENSE`, `README.md`, and `server.py`; no dependency manifest, test, or CI configuration is present, so no formal suite or package build was available. No live MCP server or shell-command execution was started during review. Full pinned-source and test-surface evidence: `research/evidence/safebuffer-cli-manager-mcp-review-2026-11-22.txt`.

## 2026-11-22 research checkpoint — candidate #144 agents-mcp/cli

Pinned `agents-mcp/cli` at `cbf03ff339f876dbb3d7792a806c1c30bf3d4c6e` (MIT CLI / CC0 self-authored spec, package 0.1.1; 0 stars/0 forks; no GitHub release). It is an MCP configuration compiler for project/global `.agents/mcp.json`, not a Project/Section/Session migrator. Runtime adapter IDs are Claude Code CLI, Cursor, VS Code, OpenCode, Windsurf, Zed, and Antigravity; it has no Codex MCP adapter, Claude Desktop 3P Chat/Cowork/Code adapter, gateway URL/key writer, transcript parser, media/session graph, Skills, memory, or schedule transfer. Antigravity is per-plugin output; the import loop scans only adapters with a single native `getConfigPath`, so it is not an import source. The repo README/spec say six/`any` in places while runtime has seven, and the bundled v0.1.0 matrix/spec omit Antigravity.

Useful ResourcePlane patterns are typed native adapters, explicit target loss warnings, deterministic source order, JSONC AST edit of only the MCP root, unmanaged/manual entry preservation by prior ownership state, and per-file atomic writes. The matrix itself acknowledges `cwd` is dropped in every target; `:-fallback`, server-level disable/target metadata and SSE semantics are not preserved in all adapters. This is a narrow config projection, not the full credentialed MCP service or any conversation/session transfer.

Disposable synthetic CLI probes exposed material issues: `import --no-sync` still executed sync; two native configs with different same-name `shared` servers silently selected Claude Code's first entry with no conflict warning; default `import` warned about a fake `ghp_...` value but left it in `.agents/mcp.json` and generated plugin `mcp.json`; a synthetic unknown `CUSTOM_SETTING=NEXUS_UNKNOWN_SYNTHETIC_SECRET_VALUE_987654321` was not detected and also remained canonical. Explicit `--extract-secrets` did correctly replace the known fake key with a placeholder, create `.env` mode 0600 and key-only `.env.example`, and gitignore `.env` plus sync state. The canonical schema accepts arbitrary server-map keys and does not apply the UI-only `SERVER_NAME_PATTERN`; `sync` with `"../../../escape"` dynamically wrote generated `plugin.json` and `mcp.json` outside the project/plugin root in a disposable `/tmp` tree. No real key/config or user project was used. Full evidence/outputs: `docs/research/evidence/agents-mcp-cli-review-2026-11-22.txt`.

Quality gates: isolated `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund` passed, TypeScript and Prettier passed, and after building first, 31 test files / 332 tests passed. On a clean checkout, `npm run verify` failed because its script order runs tests before build, while the packaging test expects `dist/index.js`; GitHub's latest CI at this pinned HEAD is red for that same test across Node 22/24/26. The Windows path job also has path-separator assertions that expect `/` while the Windows host returns `\\`; this is a test-fixture mismatch, not by itself proof of runtime path failure. No GitHub releases are listed. Do not treat a local green run after building as proof that the clean `verify` workflow passes. CodeGraph indexed 75 files / 876 nodes / 3,420 edges; `rtk git status --short` shows only the expected `.codegraph/.gitignore` edit (removed its self-preserving exception); no source/test files changed.

## Claude Desktop API-key destination: current native boundary

The user confirmed all three Claude Desktop surfaces—**Chat, Cowork, and Code**—in third-party-gateway mode, plus local/self-hosted Claude Code. Current Anthropic documentation describes Desktop 3P as sending inference to the configured cloud provider or LLM gateway while storing sessions/projects/memory on-device. Its Claude-origin import is a one-time copy of the user's own Claude.ai chats/projects plus local Cowork/Code sessions; it does not establish a ChatGPT/Codex importer, and project knowledge-file contents, some attachment bytes, cloud Cowork/Code sessions, and CLI transcripts in the Desktop ZIP flow have distinct exclusions. The official Desktop session ZIP is unencrypted, may contain API keys/passwords, and does not include Claude Code CLI sessions. Keep four independent acceptance tracks: `desktop-3p/chat`, `desktop-3p/cowork`, `desktop-3p/code`, `claude-code-local`; do not treat API-key gateway mode as Anthropic-hosted Claude Code Cloud. On target 3P, Chat/Cowork session state/transcript/audit/uploads/outputs live under the local-agent-mode-sessions tree; Code session index metadata lives separately from transcripts in `~/.claude/projects/`. Treat as a rejected writer until canonical path containment, correct ownership accounting and whole-transaction rollback are fixed. The user confirms scope as Claude Desktop on a third-party provider/gateway in Chat, Cowork, and Code, plus local/self-hosted Claude Code. No outside-of-temp/user file was touched.

`cargo audit` against the current RustSec DB reports `libsqlite3-sys 0.30.1` through bundled `rusqlite 0.32.1`, CVE-2026-119375 (medium 6.8): SQLite before 3.54.0 can expose stale process-memory bytes when opening an attacker-crafted DB with a sibling WAL whose page size differs. The condition is a less-trusted party supplying the DB/WAL that the process opens; RustSec says `libsqlite3-sys >=0.39.0` is patched. `chrono 0.4.45` is additionally marked unmaintained. This is a dependency-risk finding, not a demonstrated exploit in this repo. Official advisories: https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0459.html and https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0543.html.

The live public release install smoke was not completed: no-token GitHub API returned 403 after fixing the local CA path; no user token was used. No repository license is declared, so retain design concepts only.

## 2026-11-24 research update — candidate #230 skillpkg and PyPI name split

Pinned `xfan3696-collab/skillpkg` main/origin-main `ba406e139f12a0db6abcd1da232e5273e057b05f` (2026-03-13). It is a small S3-backed registry for versioned individual SKILL.md bundles, not a cross-Harness or session migration tool. It has no Project/Section, Session/event, lineage, media, MCP/plugin, schedule, memory, token/cost, or target-resume model, and it stores installs under a generic skillpkg directory rather than native per-Harness roots. The client can verify a SHA from an index but cannot authenticate that mutable index/publisher; `requires` is not resolved and `update` is not implemented.

The source provides some good resource-plane ideas (versioned package index, object-store backing, SHA, server-side S3 credentials) but failed isolated safety probes: `skillpkg remove ../../../victim` deleted a synthetic directory outside configured skills root; `install ../../../../...` overwrote a synthetic directory outside that root; a local mock HTTP index with `file: ../outside/payload.tar.gz` caused the real client to write package bytes outside its temporary staging directory; unauthenticated publish succeeded with optional token unset and wrote package+index to fake S3; a login token was plaintext in a mode-0644 config under umask 022; publishing 0.9.0 then 0.10.0 left latest at 0.9.0. A separate archive extraction filter is present, but it does not repair these other path/auth issues.

Build smoke passed in isolated Python 3.12.10 venv; no test directory/CI/release artifacts, and source `update` explicitly errors as unimplemented. No user files, keys, cloud bucket or session were touched. Candidate is MIT but unsafe for reuse as-is. Full evidence: `research/evidence/xfan3696-collab-skillpkg-review-2026-11-24.txt`.

PyPI collision: the README says `pip install skillpkg`, but current PyPI `skillpkg` is a different 0.0.6 project maintained by parkerbrown. A distinct `skillpkg-cli` project points to GitHub `Exaggarate/skillpkg`; its public README advertises cross-runtime checks/security scanning and is queued as a separate manual source review, not counted as reviewed. Do not conflate these projects or install the ambiguous `skillpkg` name.

## 2026-11-24 research update — candidate #232 AI Threads Kanban

Pinned `yyeret/ai-threads-kanban` main/origin-main `9f948221f805f486205afc0763bcef164e5a4dfb` (2026-10-13), MIT, 25 commits, 0 stars/forks, no CI/release/issue/PR. It is a no-dependency local board that scans Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI and Antigravity native history and gives a user a workflow overlay, goal tracking, resume links and optional explicit Codex name/archive actions. This is a valuable adjacent reference: unlike a generic Skill installer, it does discover and group sessions across harnesses. But “thread” is a heuristic task cluster, not source session identity or hierarchy: first-prompt/title hash + repository-directory basename and same-repo Jaccard thresholds can collide or merge unrelated work; session rows are flat and do not preserve branches, forks, parent/subagent edges or message-event ordering. It saves summaries and local transcript pointers, not a portable raw transcript/event bundle. No ChatGPT Projects/Sections or Claude Desktop third-party Chat/Cowork/Code input/output exists.

Source and temp-only probes exposed actionable safety boundaries. Although server binds only `127.0.0.1`, its mutation routes are GETs with no auth, method or Origin guard; a synthetic GET carrying `Origin: https://evil.invalid` changed a synthetic registry record from Implement to Plan and returned HTTP 200. A crafted synthetic Codex index ID appears verbatim in the resume string as `codex resume fixture; echo NM_UNEXECUTED_SENTINEL`; no terminal command was executed. The native Codex archive action moves rollouts and deletes index entries, so it requires explicit, auditable confirmation and compensation rather than being treated as export. `/refresh` can `git pull` and, for a package change, run `npm install`. The install script writes home-level configuration/hooks, scans native history and can create a LaunchAgent/Scheduled Task; it was not run. The registry retains first/latest prompt snippets and local file paths and may be shared via Drive/Dropbox; no secret redaction or encryption contract was found. No real profile or chat was accessed.

The `npm test` suite passed 20 tests under Node 26.7.0/npm 11.19.0, and its helper creates disposable HOME/registry roots. It covers multiple registry/edit/goal/board flows, but not the scanner/classifier or the above CSRF/resume risks; the test README is stale when it says server handlers are entirely uncovered, since one current integration test exercises some goal and board routes. The README's “No daemons” tagline also conflicts with installer behavior that can start a macOS LaunchAgent or Windows Scheduled Task.

Disposition: high-value **adjacent local SessionInventory/workflow board** prior art, not Nexus's canonical migration layer. Borrow only the idea of per-harness scanners plus a user-reviewed workflow projection. Keep immutable source records, portable SessionGraph, Project/Section relationships, field-level loss report and target native resume separate. Do not install or integrate this code as-is; first add argv-only resume, input containment, POST+CSRF/origin protection, secret minimization, transactional Codex archive receipts, cross-machine locking and representative scanner fixtures. MIT allows reuse in principle but does not make present implementation safe or migration-faithful. Full source and exact synthetic results: `research/evidence/yyeret-ai-threads-kanban-review-2026-11-24.txt`.

## 2026-11-24 research update — candidate #235 `agent-history`

`lvtd-dgtl/agent-history` (canonical GitHub page now redirects to `d3layd/agent-history`) was cloned at main HEAD `fefe2d1833af2c93f0cf3048d5e2fcf5787b35d6`, dated Nov 21, and reviewed after checking its MIT license. This is a recent, maintained local semantic/full-text history archive, not a cross-Harness migration product. The code registers local read adapters for Claude Code, Codex CLI, OpenCode, Hermes, OpenClaw, Grok, Gemini CLI and T3 Code, with opt-in docs and hub inbox; Claude.ai data exports are a separate explicit snapshot importer. Claude Code parsing recognizes local project memory, subagent files, some fork/sidechain links and remote-control bridge IDs. Codex parsing reads text portions of local rollouts, not a ChatGPT Projects/Sections source.

Claude.ai exports can yield separate searchable records for conversations, Project descriptions/instructions/docs, account/project memory, design chats and extracted attachment text. But the current importer source says chats are not linked to Projects in the export; sequential conversation messages are not rebuilt as a branch/event graph; tool/thinking blocks, uploaded image bytes, usage/token data and general typed content are omitted; attachment text is capped at 100,000 characters. The README's `doctor --coverage` explicitly calls live claude.ai Chat, claude.ai/code Cloud sessions and Claude Desktop Cowork surfaces no adapter can see. There is no native write/re-home adapter for Claude Desktop Chat, Cowork or Code, nor evidence that local session-file indexing supports third-party gateway/API-key account restore.

The repository's plugin/MCP/cron/Windows Scheduled Task material concerns keeping its own index current and injecting/retrieving search hits, not moving source Skills, MCP/plugin definitions or scheduled task objects. Its searchable SQLite database retains full transcript text; the author documents local redaction as off by default and no at-rest encryption, so treat the index as a credential store. This is a high-value adjacent reference for read adapters, torn-line coverage, aliases, read-only SQLite access and loss/coverage visibility; not a substitute for a canonical Session/EventGraph plus target writer. Keep any future use behind source-license, dependency, security and local fixture review.

## 2026-11-24 research update — candidate #236 `neosun100/aiw` and corpus integrity correction

`neosun100/aiw` was cloned at main HEAD `a9e4221aa39e20aa7c041528805f8a763c035158` (2026-01-08), with an MIT license. It is a tmux-backed live CLI workspace manager with CLI, REST/WebSocket and stdio MCP, not a conversation import/export product. Its `sessions.json` stores workspace name/tool/model/directory/description/created; no ChatGPT transcript graph, Projects/Sections, parent/fork/task/artifact hierarchy, attachments or usage is represented. It starts/attaches/sends/terminates local commands, but has no target-native writer for Claude Desktop's third-party gateway Chat/Cowork/Code modes or local Claude Code API-Key history. No Skills/plugins/memory/schedule migration layer was found.

Security and correctness prevent using it as a migration backend: the README and implementation default the HTTP listener to `0.0.0.0`, and `api.py` has no auth middleware while exposing create/send/kill/log/status and interactive terminal WebSockets. `tmux.py::_run` uses `subprocess.run(..., shell=True)` with interpolated names/paths/commands. The README itself advises adding authenticated reverse proxy and HTTPS for exposure. `rename_workspace` references undefined `SESSION_PREFIX` (then also undefined `run_tmux` / `META_DIR`); an isolated synthetic probe observed `NameError: name 'SESSION_PREFIX' is not defined` before any tmux operation.

The dependency contract is fragile: the declared `mcp>=1.0.0` permits 2.x, but tested 2.3.0 breaks the imported decorator API, causing 14 MCP tests to fail and 52 non-MCP tests to pass. Pinning `mcp==1.0.0` in a disposable venv passes all 66 tests. The tests do not exercise live tmux, rename or auth; no server, credential, user config or provider request was used. Public repository state at review: latest main commit Jan 8, 2026, v0.2.0; Releases page empty, 0 open Issues. CodeGraph current at 13 files / 218 nodes / 434 edges.

**Integrity correction discovered while continuing research:** the followup234 transaction ledger listed `docs/research/evidence/zxu756-agentvm-review-2026-11-24.txt` as newly created, but reopening the actual #235 ZIP showed that file absent and the inventory row still `unreviewed`. This update restores that evidence from the recorded followup234 run, revalidates its pinned MIT checkout at the same HEAD, and updates the primary row to a reviewed status. Primary reviewed count remains cumulative (236/303); actual `unreviewed` status count now matches 67. The VM tests remain previously observed evidence; they were not rerun in this #236 review.

Scope unchanged: ChatGPT **Destruction Era** project/Section Group/full Session hierarchy and resources; Claude Desktop third-party gateway in separate Chat, Cowork and Code modes; local/self-hosted Claude Code API-Key workflows. AIW adds no migration edge. Detailed evidence: `research/evidence/neosun100-aiw-review-2026-11-24.txt` and restored `research/evidence/zxu756-agentvm-review-2026-11-24.txt`.
